# Architecture — Sentinel

> 🌐 [Русский](ARCHITECTURE.md) (основная версия) · **English**

> Autonomous Self-Healing Playwright UI-Testing Agent.
> Polyglot: **Go** spine / **Python** LangGraph brain / **TypeScript** Playwright executor.
> Generated from a 3-phase design workflow (4 independent architects → 3 adversarial judges → lead synthesis), 2026-06-23.
> Deep mechanics live in `docs/` (see §7). Design provenance in `docs/DESIGN_RECORD.md`.

---

## 0. CONSTRAINT OVERRIDE — BUILD-ONLY (2026-06-23, hard)

**User directive:** *"We cannot buy / adopt anything off-the-shelf; we can only write it ourselves."*

**Interpretation (assumption — to confirm):** open-source **libraries** we write code against (Playwright library, LangGraph, Anthropic SDK) are *"writing"* and allowed. Adopting a turnkey third-party **server / SaaS product** is *not* allowed. If even OSS libraries are off-limits (pure from-scratch incl. browser CDP), scope changes drastically — see GAP-ARCH-002 / open question.

**Consequence:** the synthesis's single most-praised decision — *BUY the official Microsoft `@playwright/mcp` server* — is **REVERSED**. We **BUILD** our own TypeScript Playwright execution server (`pw-executor`). All three design judges flagged a hand-built Playwright server as the biggest "language-tourism" cost; that warning is **acknowledged and accepted** as the unavoidable price of build-only sovereignty. The MCP-over-stdio transport (ADR-002) **stays** — MCP is an open protocol we implement ourselves; we build the *server*, we do not buy it.

---

## 1. Context

### Purpose
A production-grade, standalone autonomous UI-testing agent that (1) explores an unknown web app on its own, (2) decides what flows to test, (3) freezes a deterministic, replayable test plan, (4) repairs broken locators when the DOM changes, and (5) emits engineer-consumable artifacts (reports, traces, exported Playwright specs, regression baselines). It is the differentiator over the existing `qa-automation-engineer` subagent, which only *writes* tests — Sentinel *discovers and maintains* them.

### Actors
| Actor | Role | Interface |
|-------|------|-----------|
| CI pipeline | Runs deterministic replay; consumes exit codes 0/1/2/3 + JSON/JUnit reports | `agentctl run --ci` |
| QA / dev engineer | Triggers explore runs, reviews flagged heals + human gates, approves baselines, consumes `.spec.ts` | `agentctl` (interactive) |
| Home-lab operator | Runs the long-lived service on K3s/ArgoCD, watches Grafana cost/health | Helm + ArgoCD (M5) |
| The agent itself | Autonomous LLM explorer | Opus 4.8 (plan) / Sonnet 4.6 (heal) — **defaults**; planner/heal are provider-agnostic per-role via `LLM_BACKEND*` (Anthropic or any OpenAI-compatible), ADR-019 |

### Scope
- **In scope:** autonomous exploratory testing; locator self-healing with confidence gating + human-in-loop; explore-once / replay-many CI determinism; short- + long-term memory; per-run token/cost budgets + tracing; artifact emission; headless CI + long-running service; home-lab K3s/ArgoCD target.
- **Out of scope (v1):** being a Claude Code `.md` subagent (explicitly excluded); multi-tenant SaaS; auto-merging healed plans into protected branches without review; load/perf testing; mobile-native (non-web); cross-browser beyond Chromium at MVP (Firefox/WebKit deferred); assertions about business correctness beyond observable UI state.

---

## 2. Components / Areas

### Overview
```
                  ┌──────────── Go (control-plane / spine) ────────────┐
  CI / engineer ─►│ agentctl (CLI) → orchestrator (run FSM, gRPC srv,   │
                  │   budget ceiling, subprocess supervision)          │
                  │ store-gateway (SOLE writer, main SQLite-WAL)        │
                  │ control-api (HTTP facade, incl. /metrics)           │
                  └───────────────────────┬────────────────────────────┘
                              gRPC proto3 (UDS/TCP)  ◄── phased in @ M2
                  ┌───────────────────────┴────────────────────────────┐
                  │            Python (brain — LangGraph)               │
                  │ StateGraph(10 nodes) · perception · healing-engine  │
                  │ checkpointer → SEPARATE SQLite file (not main DB)   │
                  └───────────────────────┬────────────────────────────┘
                              MCP / JSON-RPC 2.0 over stdio
                  ┌───────────────────────┴────────────────────────────┐
                  │  TypeScript (hands — BUILD)  pw-executor (our own)   │
                  │  Playwright Chromium · a11y snapshot · trace        │
                  └─────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘
```

### Component table
| Component | Lang | Responsibility | Key tech |
|-----------|------|----------------|----------|
| **agentctl** | Go | Single CLI/CI binary. Two halves, both DERIVED rather than restated here (principle 5): the local subcommands — the `switch os.Args[1]` dispatcher in `cmd/agentctl/main.go` (gate `TestUsageListsEverySubcommand`, ADR-088: `usage()` must name every one), and thin clients over control-api routes — the `apiVerbs` table in `cmd/agentctl/api.go` (ADR-107, gate `api_projection_test.go`; the table *is* the implementation, so it cannot drift). The authority for a human is `agentctl --help`. Exit codes come from the catalog `brain/events.json` → `exit_codes` (ADR-113, which also carries the `fault` axis): **0** passed · **1** the test found a problem (fault `app` — a result, not a breakage) · **2** golden/visual regression · **3** needs a human: plan or configuration · **4** the TOOL itself failed — NOT a finding about your application (ADR-087, fault `tool`) · **5** the tool failed but what it found was SAVED (ADR-131, fault `tool`) · **-1** the run never started or was killed by a signal (a verdict value in the catalog and in `results.exit_code`; no negative status reaches the shell). Works non-TTY + interactive. | stdlib `flag` — deliberately no CLI framework and no Viper: a new dependency has already run into the air-gapped build (cf. ADR-109 — bcrypt rejected over transitive `x/net`; ADR-111, same wording); gRPC client; the YAML RunConfig is parsed by the brain (`brain/runconfig.py`, pyyaml), not by Go |
| **control-api** | Go | HTTP facade for UI/CI (localhost-bind + bearer-token + CORS): `POST /v1/runs` (spawn explore/`replay`/`baseline`/multi-turn), SSE `GET /v1/runs/{id}/events`, WS `/v1/stream` (AG-UI), an OpenAI-compatible shim, the scenarios/tests/chats/results/metrics/**config** domains via store-gateway, `GET /readyz` + `GET`/`PUT /v1/config`. Long-lived control-plane (ADR-049). | net/http, gRPC client to store-gateway, ADR-032/040/041/043/047/048/062 |
| **orchestrator** | Go | Run-lifecycle FSM (PENDING→RUNNING→HEALING→PAUSED→PARTIAL→DONE\|FAILED\|ABORTED), gRPC server (RunControl + EventStream), supervises Python brain subprocess (5s health-ping, restart-on-crash, SIGTERM on per-step deadline). Enforces **Go-side hard budget ceiling** (reconciled vs the brain's in-process counter — NOT a per-call round trip). Does not touch SQLite. | gRPC, goroutine supervisor, context deadlines |
| **store-gateway** | Go | **Sole writer** to the main SQLite (WAL). Legacy trust/heal layer via `PersistenceService`; **M13 `StoreService`** — 6 domains (runs · scenarios/tests · chats · results · metrics · **config** ADR-062) + legacy tables `healed_locators`/`golden_snapshots`(+`mac` HMAC #24)/`healing_audit`/`step_failures`; owns migrations; exposes read RPCs. (The LangGraph checkpointer uses a SEPARATE DB file — so single-writer is *actually* true.) | SQLite WAL, gRPC, ADR-007/049/050/062 |
| ~~**report-service**~~ | — | **Removed 2026-08-09, having never run a single day in any deployment (ADR-119, QA-REPORT-SERVICE).** Built at M8 (ADR-021): served per-run `report.json`/`report.html` from `heal-report.json` + concatenated `/metrics`. Wired into nothing — absent from the `Dockerfile`, every `docker-compose*.yml`, and `install.sh`; 0.0% test coverage. `/metrics` (the aggregate Prometheus scrape) now lives in **control-api** (`cmd/control-api/metrics_agg.go`): a `run` label injected on every series at aggregation time, `# HELP`/`# TYPE` per family, honest `runs_included`/`runs_omitted` counters — not the byte-for-byte concatenation this component did, which would have produced duplicate series. `/report/<id>` HTML/JSON loses nothing: this binary never served it either — `agentctl report` already produces those files, served like any other run artifact (`GET /v1/runs/{id}/artifact`). | — |
| **brain** | Python | LangGraph StateGraph (10 nodes: perceive·ground·plan·act·verify·heal·checkpoint·takeover·scenario·report). Owns ALL LLM calls through the provider-neutral `LLMBackend` (`brain/llm.py`: `AnthropicBackend` \| `OpenAICompatBackend`, per-role `make_backend`; defaults Opus 4.8 plan / Sonnet 4.6 heal), falls back to heuristic / L1–L6 when no key/SDK. Spawns `pw-executor` + binds its MCP tools. gRPC client to orchestrator + store-gateway. Owns explore/replay switching, coverage-based convergence, `plan_hash`. | LangGraph StateGraph + checkpointer, MCP client (VERIFY pkg), Anthropic / OpenAI SDK |
| **healing-engine** | Python | The heal node: bounded re-grounding hierarchy (cache → L1–L6 no-LLM rotation → LLM a11y → gated set-of-marks), grounded confidence model with verify-before-accept, append-only `healing_audit`. Hosts `agentctl calibrate` logic. | Playwright locator strategies via MCP, structured-output LLM |
| ~~**perception**~~ | — | **This component does not exist and never did (removed by ADR-093).** The row promised a Python module computing `completeness_ratio` — `ls brain/perception.py` finds nothing and `grep -rni completeness` over `*.py/*.ts/*.go` returns zero. Perception lives in the executor: `browser.interactives` (the control inventory), `browser.snapshot` (ARIA snapshot), `browser.perceptionAudit` (the visibility measurement). This promise is what produced ADR-092: the registry listed the metric as the mitigation for `GAP-RISK-005`, so it was "restored" — and restored wrong. | — |
| **brain/llm.py** | Python | Provider-agnostic `LLMBackend` abstraction (Protocol): `AnthropicBackend` (native) \| `OpenAICompatBackend` (ChatGPT/DeepSeek/Qwen/Gemini-compat/OpenRouter/Ollama/vLLM); `make_backend(role)` picks a backend per role via env (`LLM_BACKEND[_PLANNER\|_HEAL]`, `_MODEL`, `_BASE_URL`, `_API_KEY`, `_VISION`) → `None` when key/SDK absent ⇒ heuristic / L1–L6 fallback. The LLM path is best-effort; vision gated by `supports_vision`; defaults Opus 4.8 (planner) / Sonnet 4.6 (heal). (ADR-019) | Anthropic / OpenAI SDK, `typing.Protocol` |
| **pw-executor** | **TS (BUILD)** | **OUR OWN** Node service exposing Playwright primitives (navigate, accessibility snapshot, click/type/etc., screenshot, trace control, locator resolve/probe, set-of-marks overlay) to the brain over an MCP (JSON-RPC 2.0) stdio interface **we implement**. Runs as a child subprocess of the brain. | Playwright (lib, pinned), MCP server impl (ours), stdio JSON-RPC |
| **proto** | shared | protobuf3 single source of truth for Go↔Python. Services (derived via `grep '^service ' proto/*.proto`): **`RunControl`** (`proto/runcontrol.proto`), **`PersistenceService`** (`proto/persistence.proto` — the legacy heal/trust layer) and **`StoreService`** (`proto/store.proto` — the M13/ADR-050 domains + `config` ADR-062). `EventStream` from `docs/DESIGN_RECORD.md` was never built and is not a service: events ride the `ReportEvent` reply, and reach the outside as control-api SSE/WS. Introduced @ M2. ⚠ **The stub gate is PARTIAL and inert in CI (measured 2026-08-10):** no codegen in CI and no `.proto` hash assertion — `grep -rni proto .github/workflows/` returns zero; stubs are regenerated by hand and committed. The only check is `internal/store/pb/regen_test.go` (ADR-109): it regenerates the **Go** stubs for `store.proto`+`persistence.proto` and diffs them byte-for-byte, but `t.Skip`s when `.venv` or `protoc-gen-go{,-grpc}` is absent — and in the `build` job `go test ./...` runs BEFORE the venv is provisioned, while no workflow installs the plugins, so it skips silently. Not covered at all: `runcontrol.proto` and every Python stub. Open as `GAP-RISK-008` / `[PROTO-STUB-GATE]`. | protoc by hand (`grpc_tools.protoc` from the venv + `protoc-gen-go`); no proto job in CI |

### Key interactions / boundaries
**Two live wire protocols; a third boundary deliberately eliminated.**

1. **Go ↔ Python — gRPC proto3** (bidi streaming, UDS single-host / TCP for K3s). *Why:* compile-time typed contracts (drift = build failure), server-push of budget/gate events without polling, deadline propagation = per-step timeout. **Phased in @ M2** — M0/M1 use plain subprocess + env vars (`TARGET_URL`, `RUN_ID`, `RUN_MODE`, `ARTIFACT_DIR`). Rejected: REST/JSON (no compile-time schema, no clean streaming/cancel).
2. **Python ↔ TS — MCP (JSON-RPC 2.0) over stdio**, to `pw-executor`, bound via LangGraph's MCP tool integration. *Why:* native LLM tool-call protocol (zero adapter), stdio avoids CI port-allocation flakiness, subprocess lifecycle owned by the Python parent (SIGTERM cascade), EOF is a clean failure signal. **BUILD-only note:** we implement this server ourselves (ADR-001).
3. **TS → Go (artifacts) — ELIMINATED.** Playwright traces written to a shared artifact dir; the brain receives the path in the MCP response and relays it to Go over the existing gRPC channel; control-api serves them as a whitelisted artifact (`GET /v1/runs/{id}/artifact`, ADR-099) or they are read directly from the run directory — there is no separate HTTP service for this anymore (`report-service` removed, ADR-119). `.spec.ts` generated by Go from `RunState`, not pushed from TS. Fewer wires = fewer failure modes.

**Failure isolation (real):** TS/MCP crash → brain detects EOF, restarts subprocess once, re-navigates to checkpoint `page_model.url`, re-enters the node (no work lost — checkpoint precedes the action). Python crash → orchestrator detects gRPC stream termination, marks FAILED, persists partial state, leaves checkpoint intact (`agentctl run --resume`). Go crash → brain reconnects with backoff; main DB durable; budget degrades safely to the in-process counter.

---

### Run lifecycle, stores & resource model (M8 + R2 + ADR-004/007/046/048/049)

**Control-plane vs run-unit (ADR-049).** Long-lived: `control-API` (UI/spawn), `store-gateway` (DB), `orchestrator` (budget/takeover). The ephemeral **run-unit** = `brain` (LangGraph) + `pw-executor` (Chromium) — spawned for ONE run/turn and exits; memory across runs/turns is ONLY via the checkpointer.

**Two DISTINCT stores (often confused):**
| Store | Component | DB | Writer | Holds |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| **store-gateway** | Go gRPC single-writer (ADR-007) | `state/locators.db` (SQLite `0600`) | Go only (brain = gRPC client) | `healed_locators` · `golden_snapshots` · `healing_audit` · `step_failures` — the trust/heal layer |
| **checkpointer** | LangGraph saver (ADR-004/017/048) | `state/conversations.db` (chat, by `conversation_id`) OR per-run `ARTIFACT_DIR/checkpoint.db` (explore) OR Postgres (`CHECKPOINT_DSN`) | brain directly | graph state: intra-run + conversation + (R3) interrupt/resume |

Separate files by design (ADR-004): the "Go store-gateway = single writer of the MAIN DB" invariant holds. **M13** extends the store-gateway to 6 domains (`runs`·`scenarios`/`tests`·`chats`·`results`·`metrics` + `config` ADR-062).

**Browser: per-run in the CLI, a SHARED SERVICE in the default compose (ADR-110/112).** Native `agentctl run` and the compose CLI services (`sentinel`, `demo` — both given an empty `PW_CDP_ENDPOINT`) launch their own Chromium per run: `initialize` → work → `traceStop` + `shutdown`/`close` at the end. But `docker compose up` with NO flags brings up the long-lived `browser` service (it carries no profile), and control-api gets `PW_CDP_ENDPOINT=http://browser:9223` by default — the `${VAR-fallback}` form with a SINGLE dash substitutes until the variable is explicitly emptied (verify with `docker compose config`, not by eye). So **every run started from the interface takes the CDP branch into ONE shared browser**. The variable reaches the executor intact: control-api → the `PW_` prefix allowlist in `agentctl::filteredEnv` → brain → pw-executor.
- **Replay/baseline** — the browser participates FULLY (executes steps against the live AUT) → teardown at the end.
- **Authoring chat:** the explore walk (with a browser) is only on **cold turn-1** (spawn+close WITHIN turn-1); **warm refine turns do NOT call the browser** (the `scenario` node authors over the persisted `site_map`).
- **The opt-out is an explicit empty string:** `PW_CDP_ENDPOINT=` (which is why the `${VAR-fallback}` form was chosen) restores the per-run browser; in the default stack that is the only way to get our own deterministic launch.
- **The CDP branch adopts someone else's CONTEXT, but not their tab** (ADR-128): `browser.contexts()[0] ?? await browser.newContext()` in `pw-executor/src/server.ts`, and the page is ours — `context.newPage()`. The user's session is reused; their open tab is not. Two concurrent runs get two pages and two `targetId`s, so the live view answers each with its own frame (measured: 9 882 and 10 105 bytes, different `url`s). A run's pages are its own plus the popups whose `opener()` is one of ours; a tab the human opened does not belong to the run and is not visible to `browser.tabs`.
- **Teardown does NOT close the browser** (the `attachedOverCDP` flag in `pw-executor/src/server.ts` is read in cleanup and in the `SIGTERM` handler) — by design (ADR-037): the process outlives the run, and closing it would kill either the user's session or the shared service the next run needs.
- **Our determinism anchors do NOT apply on the CDP branch:** viewport/DSR, `PW_IGNORE_HTTPS_ERRORS` and `storageState` are set only in the own-launch branch, and the executor logs `STORAGE_STATE ignored in CDP-attach mode`. Consequence for the default stack: the pre-loaded M9.1 session silently does nothing, and a golden replay started from the interface runs in OBSERVATION mode, where screenshot bytes are not guaranteed (ADR-037, `docs/DETERMINISM.md`).
- **`PW_CDP_ENDPOINT` pointed at the user's own Chrome** (`--remote-debugging-port`) is the same mechanism — the live/takeover path (R3 + #47); only whose browser it is and who turned it on differ.

**⚠ Stale-site-map (refine trade-off, GAP-M9-19):** a warm refine authors over the `site_map` AS IT WAS at turn-1. If the AUT changed between turns, refine won't see it → a new explore is needed (new conversation / cold turn). **To foresee:** staleness-detect (compare the current page's a11y hash with the persisted one) OR an explicit "re-explore" button in refine OR a `site_map` TTL → planned for M13/M14.

**Brain process (per-turn):** `agentctl`/`control-API` spawns the brain (`RUN_MODE` + env) → `main()` dispatches → (chat) resume `conversation_id` from the checkpointer → graph `perceive→ground→plan→act→verify→…→scenario→report` (or straight to `scenario` on a warm turn) → each node-step `rc.report()` → `orchestrator` returns `Control{abort|takeover}` → artifacts + stdout (→ control-API SSE/WS) → exit `0/1/2/3`. **(R3) takeover:** `Control.takeover` → LangGraph `interrupt`+persist → the human drives (CDP) → return → a new brain invocation `resume`s from the interrupt point.

**Resource model (measured 2026-06-30):** brain cold-import ≈ **0.75–1.0 s / ~60 MB RSS** (ephemeral, freed on exit); the browser ≈ **1–3 s + hundreds of MB** (cold turn-1 / replay only). Spawn ≪ the LLM authoring call (several seconds) ⇒ startup is not the bottleneck. **Deliberate trade (ADR-046):** process-per-turn-resume (via the checkpointer) was chosen over a resident brain — for simplicity, crash-isolation, air-gapped, conflict-free concurrency; cost ≈ 1 s import/turn. **Optimization (if M9-LIVE profiling shows a bottleneck):** a warm pool of resident brain workers OR lazy-importing grpc/anthropic — deferred.

**Context budget:** per-LLM-call is bounded (site_map menu `[:8000]` chars, `max_tokens` 200 per-step / 800 authoring). **⚠ The conversation history grows unbounded (GAP-M9-20):** the R2a `messages` channel + `_user_turns` pass ALL user turns, and `conversations.db` accumulates everything → for long conversations the prompt/checkpoint bloats. Planned for **M13** (chats domain): cap the last N turns + a running summary + retention.

---

## 3. Decisions (ADR Log)

> **`ARCHITECTURE.md` (Russian) is the registry of record; this is its translation.** A decision is
> written there first, and this half carries the same set of ADRs in the same order — not by
> anyone's diligence but because `tests/test_bilingual_content_offline.py` fails the build when the
> two diverge. The drift this note used to carry a warning about is closed: the halves held 128 and
> 90 entries when `[DOC-ARCH-EN-DRIFT]` was opened, and they hold the same set today.
>
> ⚠ The count of TODAY is deliberately absent from that sentence, and the first draft had it —
> «they hold the same 128 now» — which went stale on the very next ADR, three paragraphs above a
> line telling the reader not to restate the count in prose. A number in prose is a promise
> nobody re-measures; the gate is where the count belongs.
>
> The old wording lived here long after it stopped being true, which is the reason the parity is
> gated rather than promised. Do not restate the count in prose — derive it:
> `grep -cE '^\| ADR-[0-9]+ \|' ARCHITECTURE.md ARCHITECTURE.en.md`.

| ID | Date | Decision | Status | Context / rejected alternative |
|----|------|----------|--------|--------------------------------|
| ADR-001 | 2026-06-23 | **BUILD** our own TS Playwright execution server (`pw-executor`) over an MCP stdio interface we implement | **Accepted (constraint-driven; supersedes synthesis)** | Build-only directive (§0). We own the tool schema (stable) at the cost of owning Playwright-API-churn maintenance. **Rejected:** BUY official `@playwright/mcp` (disallowed by constraint — was the synthesis's top pick) |
| ADR-002 | 2026-06-23 | MCP over stdio for the Python↔TS boundary | Accepted | Native LLM tool-call protocol; LangGraph binds it w/o adapter; stdio avoids CI port flakiness. **Rejected:** gRPC TS server; Python-Playwright in-process (violates polyglot lock, loses Node-native trace) |
| ADR-003 | 2026-06-23 | gRPC proto3 for Go↔Python, **phased in @ M2** (M0/M1 = subprocess+env) | Accepted | Compile-time contracts, server-push, deadline propagation. **Rejected:** REST/JSON; gRPC-from-day-1 (premature) |
| ADR-004 | 2026-06-23 | LangGraph StateGraph backbone; checkpointer in a **SEPARATE** DB file from store-gateway | Accepted | Free checkpoint/resume/conditional-heal-edges/human-pause; separate file makes "Go sole writer of main DB" true. **Rejected:** bespoke asyncio loop; shared checkpoint+store DB (two writers) |
| ADR-005 | 2026-06-23 | a11y tree = primary perception; set-of-marks visual = fallback gated by `completeness_ratio<0.30` AND a measured PoC | Accepted | ARIA roles/names are semantic, re-skin-stable, cheap, yield usable selectors. **Rejected:** screenshot-primary (cost, fragile); full-DOM snapshot (token blowout) |
| ADR-006 | 2026-06-23 | Explore-once / replay-many with `plan_hash` HARD-ABORT on replay + immutable golden baselines (operator-command update only) | Accepted | The frozen plan is the only trustworthy reproducibility guarantee (no provider determinism even at T=0). **Rejected:** seeded/T=0 LLM as the determinism mechanism; auto-regenerate plan on staleness (P2's fatal flaw); HAR replay |
| ADR-007 | 2026-06-23 | Go store-gateway = single writer of one main SQLite (WAL); Postgres + AsyncPostgresSaver deferred to M5 behind explicit trigger | Accepted | Zero-ops, cp-backupable, single-writer + concurrent-readers matches access; per-job SQLite for CI parallelism. Postgres-compatible schema. **Rejected:** Postgres day-1; Python direct DB access |
| ADR-008 | 2026-06-23 | Grounded, calibrated confidence: per-strategy priors + empirical discounts + MANDATORY verify-before-accept live-DOM probe + post-heal verification + scheduled calibration | Accepted | Every LLM/visual candidate re-probed live (confidence zeroed if absent); `calibrate` recomputes precision/recall vs human-verified; cold-start threshold raised to 0.90. **Rejected:** raw LLM self-report vs magic thresholds with no calibration path |
| ADR-009 | 2026-06-23 | Model split: Opus 4.8 for explore/plan, Sonnet 4.6 for healing | Accepted | Planning quality drives the differentiator (runs once/explore); healing is bounded + structured + in the replay hot path (latency/cost). **Rejected:** uniform Opus (5–8× cost); local model (VERIFY home-lab GPU sufficiency if revisited) |
| ADR-010 | 2026-06-23 | Exploration terminates on a MEASURABLE coverage target (fraction of discovered interactive elements exercised + empty nav frontier); budget = backstop | Accepted | Closes the cross-cutting hand-wave: an LLM "done" flag bounds, it does not converge. **Rejected:** LLM `exploration_complete` flag alone; fixed-depth-only cap |
| ADR-011 | 2026-06-23 | Pluggable planner: `HeuristicPlanner` (default, offline, deterministic, zero-cost) + `LLMPlanner` (Opus 4.8, optional via `--planner llm`, falls back to heuristic) | Accepted | Makes the M1 explore-gate verifiable offline / in CI without network or LLM spend, and doubles as the budget-exhaustion graceful-degradation path (consistent with §8). LLM stays the primary "smart" explorer when a key is present. **Rejected:** Opus-only plan node (untestable offline, costs tokens per smoke run, blocks CI) |
| ADR-012 | 2026-06-23 | M2 delivered heal-engine-first: deterministic L1–L6 rotation + verify-before-accept + confidence gate + minimal replay, with an **interim brain-local SQLite store**; Go store-gateway+gRPC+proto (M2b) and MCP-SDK transport deferred | Accepted | Self-healing is M2's value and is testable offline; gRPC/store-gateway is infra best introduced separately. Heal needs a stale-locator trigger → minimal replay pulled forward (without the M3 trust layer). Interim local store is a **documented temporary deviation** from ADR-007 (single-writer), restored at M2b. **Rejected:** full M2 bundle at once (high integration risk, untestable in the gated/offline env) |
| ADR-013 | 2026-06-23 | Heal and golden-diff **coexist**: a healed step still executes AND its page is still golden-diffed, so a drift that heals via testid also raises an a11y golden regression (exit 2). Golden baselines are **page-keyed by URL basename** (cross-base comparison). M3 gRPC orchestrator stays in M2b | Accepted | Healing = test robustness (keep running); golden-diff = change detection (flag the change). They answer different questions and must both fire. Page-basename keying lets a plan explored on site/ be diffed against site-v2/. **Rejected:** treating a heal as suppressing the regression signal (would hide real app changes) |

Bilingual via `data-lang`; `<option>` labels are built in JS (spans inside `<option>` do not work — the M12 bug). The `node --check` CI gate now covers every `docs/*.html`. **Boundaries fixed by the adversarial review:** `sampling` is **filtered out** of the backend dropdown (the MCP host supplies the model — a plain `agentctl run` would silently degrade to the heuristic); a live schema is accepted **only in full** (`modes`/`planner`/`backends`/`roles`/`fields`/`llm`), otherwise the snapshot stays and the UI says so (an older control-API served `{modes,planner,fields,note}` → an empty `LLM_BACKEND=` under a green "valid" banner); `applyPreset` **always** rewrites the per-role models (otherwise `claude-opus-4-8` survived in `LLM_MODEL_PLANNER` after switching to Ollama); a preset's `label` renders as-is (product names) while its `note` is English provenance data and is **not rendered as UI chrome** (the bilingual claim covers chrome, not data); `.dockerignore` now lets `docs/backend-presets.json`+`prices.json` into the `webui` image (their `fetch` used to 404) — asserted structurally in `tests/test_m11_4_offline.py`. | Accepted | "Schema-driven" and "air-gapped" pull apart; snapshot+override delivers both, and the test gate turns hardcode drift from a matter of discipline into a CI failure. **Rejected:** requiring a control-API (kills offline generation — the page's whole purpose); a hardcoded form with no snapshot (exactly the drift ADR-059 warns about); a `<script type="application/json">` snapshot (the CI extractor would pull it into the `node --check` bundle and break the gate). **60→61 ADRs.** |
| ADR-062 | 2026-07-10 | **A `config` domain in the store-gateway + `/readyz` (implements ADR-059 PR-5, closes the M11.5 arc)** — a sixth `StoreService` domain (`ConfigRecord`/`ConfigKey`/`ConfigList`; `PutConfig`/`GetConfig`/`ListConfig`/`DeleteConfig`), table `config(key,value_json,updated_at)`, portable `ON CONFLICT` upsert under `s.mu` (ADR-007) — the ADR-050 pattern. **Tiered config (ADR-049):** standalone = a file (`brain/runconfig.py`, untouched), service = this domain; the control-API reads the `setup` document at start and the wizard writes it (`PUT /v1/config`; both the write and the read are token-gated — the document names internal hosts and models). **Secrets are refused, not stripped:** the rule has ONE definition, `internal/configguard`, imported by BOTH the gateway and the control-API. Member names are checked recursively (nested objects and arrays); the `token`/`key` families match on word boundaries, or legitimate `max_tokens`/`total_tokens` would be refused. A non-object document (a bare `"sk-live-…"` string) is refused separately: it carries no member names, so a name-based guard would pass straight through it. The guard must live in the gateway because that socket is reachable by any same-UID process holding `STORE_TOKEN` — an HTTP-only check would be a suggestion, not a boundary. **`/readyz`** (unauthenticated, beside the `/healthz` liveness probe): three checks — `store` (an RPC to the gateway) · `llm` (`GET <base_url>/models`) · `config` (a document is present); `200` when nothing is in `error`, `503` otherwise, k8s-shaped. **An unconfigured dependency is `skipped`, not `error`:** the default standalone deployment (`docker compose up`, no store, no `LLM_BASE_URL`) is a legitimate working install, and a readiness probe that never went green there would be worse than useless. **Boundaries fixed up front rather than discovered later:** the result is cached (3 s TTL) and single-flighted, or an unauthenticated `/readyz` becomes an amplifier aimed at the LLM endpoint; redirects are refused (`CheckRedirect`), only `http`/`https` are spoken, and each probe is bounded at 2 s; the `detail` strings (socket paths, internal hostnames, gateway errors) go **only** to an authenticated caller, or `/readyz` is a free oracle for the internal network; the network I/O takes its own mutex and **never** `s.mu`, or a probe would stall `/v1/runs`; and `PUT` invalidates the memo, or `/readyz` would keep answering `503` for 3 s after the config was saved. The CORS preflight now advertises `PUT`/`DELETE` — without it the wizard's browser-side `PUT` could not be sent. **Boundary:** the stored config is the persistence substrate + the `/readyz` signal; it is NOT yet materialized into `agentctl run` (a spawn uses the process env, `os.Environ()`), and wiring stored→run-env is deferred (an env-vs-store precedence question) — "Save" changes what survives a restart and what `/readyz` sees, not run behaviour. **Hardened by an adversarial review (6 findings, each reproduced and fixed before merge):** (1) `Secretish` missed `api-key`/`api key`/`api.key` (a hyphen/space broke the `api_key` substring) → the name is canonicalized (separators→`_`) before matching, while `max-tokens`/`base-url` stay clean; (2/3) `getConfig` collapsed an RPC error and not-found → `GET /v1/config` answered a false 404 when the gateway was unreachable, hiding a real config → a three-way result (found/not-found/error): 502 on a gateway error, 404 only on a genuine miss; (4) `readiness()` held `s.ready.mu` across the whole outbound I/O → `PUT` blocked behind a slow probe (reproduced under `-race`: invalidate stalled ~1.2s) → an epoch-guarded single-flight, I/O outside the lock, the config probe bounded at `readyProbeTimeout`; (5) the byte bound lived only in the HTTP layer → moved to `configguard` and enforced at the gateway (the real boundary). The userinfo-in-base_url angle was confirmed already closed. | Accepted | "Onboarding in ≤10 minutes" requires the config to survive a restart and an orchestrator to be able to tell "the process is alive" from "the process can serve". One rule-package instead of two copies turns guard drift from a matter of discipline into a compile error. **Rejected:** storing keys in the domain and "stripping" them (silent stripping leaves the operator believing the key was stored and honoured); the guard in the HTTP layer only (the socket is a second door); `/readyz` as an alias of `/healthz` (useless in k8s); a strict "no config -> `503` even in standalone" (breaks the QUICKSTART path). **61→62 ADRs.** |
| ADR-063 | 2026-07-23 | **Config-driven LLM for runs — materialize the UI/persisted LLM connection into the `agentctl` spawn env (closes the ADR-062 deferral)** — a run no longer takes the LLM only from the control-API process env. `spawnRun` builds the env in layers, precedence **process env > per-run (`POST /v1/runs` body field `llm`) > persisted (`/v1/config` `llm`, service tier)**: `resolveRunEnv` (`cmd/control-api/llmenv.go`) fills only the `LLM_*` it does not already set, never overriding the process env. `agentctl filteredEnv()` already forwards the `LLM_` prefix to the brain, so the allowlist is untouched. The hub `#build`/`#chat` (`docs/index.html`) now send `llm` in the run body; per-run input is validated (`backend`∈enum · `base_url` via the shared `validateLLMBase` — http(s)/no `user:pass@`/block link-local, the same validator `probeLLM` reuses). **Secret invariant preserved:** an `api_key` is never accepted via the API/UI (`configguard.FindSecretKey` per-run, `configguard.Validate` persisted); a real cloud key stays in the process env; a local `openai` (Ollama) gets `LLM_API_KEY=noauth`. Persisted path = service tier (compose `store` profile + `CONTROL_API_STORE_ADDR=unix:/app/state/store.sock`); standalone works per-run without a store. Tests: `llmenv_test.go` + `llm_materialize_test.go` (per-run/env-wins/persisted via a real gateway/400 validation). The instruction is simplified: control-api starts with just a token, the LLM is set in the UI. | Accepted | The UI must actually configure the LLM for runs, not only generate env/yaml (self-contained co-pilot). **Rejected:** the UI beats the process env (a surprise for an operator whose env is baked → env-wins); accepting a key via the API/UI (breaks the ADR-060/062 secret invariant → noauth default for local, env for cloud); a per-run `base_url` without validation (a token-gated SSRF surface → the shared validator with `probeLLM`). **62→63 ADRs.** |
| ADR-064 | 2026-07-23 | **Three UI deployment modes + a runtime control-API token** — the modes are named and the third is implemented: **1 headless** (no UI: CI/scripts/an MCP host — today's default, nothing is registered) · **2 split** (the `webui` service on :8088 + the API on :8090 — two origins, a CORS allowlist is required, the token is pasted into Settings; unchanged) · **3 single-service** (`CONTROL_API_SERVE_UI=1` — control-API serves the UI from its **own** port: one process, one port, same-origin → the allowlist can be emptied outright, `CONTROL_API_CORS_ORIGINS=`). **The token is no longer something to invent first:** `resolveToken` (`cmd/control-api/token.go`) — precedence **`CONTROL_API_TOKEN` env > `CONTROL_API_AUTOTOKEN=0` (fail-closed read-only) > persisted `state/control-api.token` > 32 fresh `crypto/rand` bytes**; the write is atomic (temp+rename, 0600), it is reused across restarts, and an unreadable/foreign file is NEVER overwritten (fall back to an in-memory token + a warning). **Bootstrap:** handing the token to whoever does a `GET /` would mean "reached the port = holds the token" and would gut ADR-032 — so a **single-use TTL nonce** is minted at startup, the `/?bootstrap=<nonce>` link is printed only on the operator's terminal, and the page trades it for the token exactly once (`GET /v1/ui-token`, `subtle` compare, same-origin guard, `no-store`, burned on replay/expiry/5 wrong guesses), keeps the token in tab memory and strips the nonce from the URL. Assets are embedded (`docs/embed.go`, `package webui`) so a release binary serves the UI with no checkout; the `go:embed` pattern list is an **explicit allowlist**, plus a runtime `uiPathAllowed` filter for the disk source (`CONTROL_API_UI_DIR`) and a blanket refusal of directory listings: `docs/` also holds gitignored INTERNAL-ONLY material that a wildcard would bake into a maintainer-built binary while CI looked clean (`TestEmbeddedUIHasNoInternalDocs`). An unknown `/v1/*` answers a JSON 404, not `index.html`. The DOM gate grows a 13th check — a real Chromium against a real mode-3 process. | Accepted | The two most common first-run stumbles — "invent a secret before starting" and "wire CORS between two origins" — are removed without weakening the security model: auto-generation gives the SERVER a token, not everyone who can reach the port. **Rejected:** injecting the token into the served HTML (port reachability would equal token possession; in a container the bind is `0.0.0.0`, so a local-bind gate does not save it); `all:.` in `go:embed` (would embed the INTERNAL-ONLY docs); gating on `isLocalBind` instead of a nonce (the compose bind is always `0.0.0.0`, so the gate would be falsely strict and useless); a bare `"/v1/"` guard (`net/http` panics at registration next to `GET /`). **63→64 ADRs.** |
| ADR-065 | 2026-07-25 | **Human-readable events: a catalogue and two streams instead of one log** — a run printed 218 lines, 4 of which addressed a person: 178 (82%) were `@@AGUI` protocol frames, 34 were the same healing-stub line, and 23 were a Node stack trace from a closing pipe that read like a product crash. Meanwhile the run was **stuck in a loop** and the log never said so. The distinction that drove the design (Alex): **human-readable messages and levelled logs are not the same thing**, and merging them yields neither. So one catalogue, `brain/events.json`, serves **two streams**: **A — the narrative** (`events.jsonl`; what is happening; strictly ordered, **no levels at all** — filtering a story by severity destroys it; sourced from the 8 AG-UI event types, which already carry "step 2 of 40" and the healing strategy but had neither human text nor anywhere to appear, so those 178 "noise" lines were the unread story of the run) and **B — diagnostics** (`run.jsonl`; why it broke; levels/categories/modules, filterable; sourced from 64 codes covering 72 `log()` sites). Plus `run.log`, the raw stream 1:1, so nothing is lost. **The one crossing is `degrades: true` (20 codes)**: a path where the run continues, exits 0 and looks successful while the LLM never participated or the scenario came out empty; such an event must surface on the verdict (`{ru,en}_verdict`) rather than settle into a log. Both incidents of this milestone came from that list (`no Anthropic key`, then a wrong model tag surfacing as `provider unavailable`). Implementation: `brain/eventlog.py` (`log(code, **fields)`; level/category/phase come from the catalogue, not the call site; the wire is one line that is readable AND parseable with no mode flag: `[warn\|llm] llm.no_anthropic_key: …`; English on the wire — it greps cleanly and survives a Windows codepage, with the UI supplying Russian) · `brain/embed.go` (`package eventcatalog`, go:embed — a Go file inside a Python directory on the same principle as `docs/embed.go`, so no second copy exists) · `cmd/control-api/logsink.go` (a second consumer of `lineWriter`; the ring buffer and the live timeline are untouched) · `logs_api.go` (`GET /v1/runs/{id}/logs` — token-gated, filters `lvl` (minimum)/`cat`/`mod`/`code`/`q`/`after`/`limit`, and `recorded:false` distinguishes "nothing recorded" from "nothing matched"; `GET /v1/events-catalog` — unauthenticated like `/healthz`: it is the shipped bilingual message list and carries no run data). Completeness is **machine-checked**: `tests/test_event_catalog_offline.py` is a two-way gate (a new `log()` line with no code fails the build; so does a code pointing at deleted code), plus RU/EN on every entry, `degrades`⇒verdict hint in both languages, closed vocabularies, and `phases` ≡ the real `graph.py` nodes. Live result: **110 raw lines → 7 rows**, with the loop collapsed to `×8` and visible **in real time**. | Accepted | A tester who knows nothing of the internals must understand what happened, and must see a stuck run. **Rejected:** one levelled stream (a story cannot be severity-filtered and remain a story); `@@LOG {json}` on the wire (a CLI operator would stare at JSON) and two lines — readable plus JSON (double the volume); Russian on the wire (Windows codepage plus unportable grep); **collapsing repeats at write time** — built, then rejected on the evidence of a live run: holding a record back to count its repeats kept a stuck run out of its own log for the whole duration of the loop, and real repeats arrive ~5 s apart, so no deadline could combine live visibility with collapsing → moved to the read side, where both properties hold at once; a second catalogue copy under `internal/` guarded by a byte-identity test (one copy, with nothing to drift from, is strictly better). **64→65 ADR.** |
| ADR-066 | 2026-07-25 | **Navigation by what a person is doing, not by which module owns the code — a vertical rail, eight views, one token** — the old structure grouped by code ownership, which produced three defects at once. (1) **The product's main action was buried:** launching a run (§8 "RunConfig builder") sat under the **Settings** tab beside four calculators; of that tab's eight sections exactly one was a setting. (2) **The Tests tab was a dumping ground** of seven unrelated things in one row — launching, watching, three stores, two analytics — with nowhere for logs to go, which is where the question "is it really logical to file logs under tests?" came from. (3) **`docs/chat/` was an orphan page:** zero inbound links, while the nav item labelled "Console" (`index.html:200`) switched a tab on the SAME page — a button reading "Console" did not open the console. The root cause was not a missing link: `/chat/` **duplicated** the "Author" subtab, and therefore kept its own `#capitok`, so the token had to be pasted twice, the one-time bootstrap nonce being redeemable by a single tab. **Decision:** a vertical rail (Alex's directive: like Gemini/Anthropic) and eight views ordered by how a session actually goes — **Chat · New run · Live · Library{Scenarios,Runs,Conversations} · Results{Verdicts,Metrics} · Logs · Tools · Settings**; `/chat/` is absorbed into the Chat view and remains a redirect, so the documented URL and bookmarks keep working. One token **by construction** rather than by a syncing mechanism: the chat stopped being a separate page, so `capiTok()` reads the single input — no BroadcastChannel, and no weakening of ADR-032/064. **Implemented by toggling existing nodes, not moving them:** every section kept its id and markup, so run launching, replay, baseline, the library, the charts and the logs are untouched — a 2700-line page cannot be safely re-laid-out by hand, but it can be re-grouped by a `data-view` attribute (plus `data-subpanel` for the two composite views). Addressing: `#v=<view>` is a direct link, and legacy anchors (`#connect`, `#build`, `#cost`) resolve to the view that now contains them **via the DOM** rather than a hand-kept map that would rot; `hashchange` is honoured, since a hash-only change is a same-document navigation and nothing would otherwise re-read it. Precedence: **an address beats a remembered view** — the reverse sent every deep link to whatever was open last. The gate `scripts/hub-dom-check.mjs` grew to 16 checks, 6 of them navigational. | Accepted | A tester should find things by their own task, not by which module owns the feature. **Rejected:** keeping two pages and syncing the token via BroadcastChannel/localStorage (treats the symptom, keeps two copies of the chat, and drags the secret into storage against ADR-032); physically moving DOM nodes into new containers (in 2700 lines of inline JS that is a re-plumbing of unpredictable radius — all the logic is addressed by id); rewriting the M12/M14/M15/COPILOT contract docs to the new structure (they record THEIR milestones and would become false about them — only the operator instructions were updated). **65→66 ADR.** |
| ADR-067 | 2026-07-25 | **Three log sources and step correlation — whose log is this, and where did it break** — a tester watching a step fail could not tell "the tool mis-clicked" from "the application threw", and the tested site's console was never collected at all. The **source** axis is derived from the category (a record never carries it, so the two cannot disagree): `tool` (run·plan·heal·hitl·llm·record·system·browser) · `application` (new category `app`) · `testing` (new `test`). **Application channel** — 7 codes emitted by `pw-executor` (`page.on('pageerror'\|'console'\|'requestfailed'\|'response'\|'dialog')`), attached to the initial page AND every popup, in the SAME wire format brain uses so the boundary parses it with no new code; capped at 500 with an explicit `app.log_capped`, so a truncated capture can never read as a quiet application. **Testing channel** — 10 codes; the per-step outcomes were raw `print` calls, so the one line saying WHICH step failed was landing in the diagnostics as `system.unclassified`. **Step correlation with no protocol change:** both streams share one ordered pipe, so the boundary remembers the last `step.progress` frame and stamps subsequent records; a report-phase summary is deliberately not stamped ("Explore finished … step 3" would read as a fact about step 3). The gate holds a foreign emitter to the same two-way rule and compares the English templates byte-for-byte — the UI recovers placeholder values by matching them, so a drift would silently degrade the UI to English. New fixture `l7-appfaults.html` genuinely misbehaves. | Accepted | A tester's first question is "is this my application or your tool?", and answering it must not require reading the raw stream. **Rejected:** changing the executor protocol to carry a step number (the boundary already knows it from an existing frame); collecting `info`/`log` console output from the site (the application's own chatter would bury the two levels that matter); storing the source on the record beside the category (deriving it makes disagreement impossible). **66→67 ADR.** |
| ADR-068 | 2026-07-25 | **A filter language, and two registers for reading the same events** — Alex's requirement: "technical logs and human logs … by category, so one can see where and which step went wrong". **Register** is a toggle over the SAME rows rather than two tables: *plain* = the catalogue phrase in the reader's language plus the step number; *technical* = a full RFC3339 timestamp, the event code, module, step, source and the server's raw English — what gets pasted into an issue. Filters, order and scroll position are shared, so switching never loses your place, which two subtabs could not offer. **Filter language:** what transfers from Wireshark is NOT the language (that one is about packets — `tcp.port`, protocol layers; we have none of those fields) but three of its properties: filter by FIELD instead of hoping a word appears; live validity shown by border colour (an expression is invalid on almost every keystroke, so the error must be quiet); and "apply as filter" by clicking a cell — which is how the syntax gets learned without docs. A hand-written recursive-descent parser (~250 lines, 9 fields `lvl src cat mod code msg step n ts`, operators `== != >= <= > < ~ contains in {…}`, connectives `&& \|\| ! ( )`) — a library is impossible: the self-contained page's CSP forbids fetching one and the air-gapped bundle would break. **The expression is the only thing that filters;** the dropdowns and checkboxes WRITE clauses into it, so whatever a click did is always visible as text, editable, saveable and pasteable into a bug report. Sync is one-way by design: hand-editing switches to an honest "custom expression" state and disables the controls, rather than two parsers fighting over one string. An invalid expression does NOT blank the view — the last valid predicate keeps filtering while the message says what and where. **The source axis** (ADR-067) is derived from the category, so on a row it is redundant ("приложение приложение") and was removed; it remains a filter, where being coarse is the point. Brain's console summaries (`====`, `EXPLORE COMPLETE`, `# 1 navigate`) carry the same facts as the `test.*` events, so a new `system.console_table` pattern classifies them `debug`: hidden by default, the CLI terminal untouched. The gate grew 16→22 (the grammar is exercised INSIDE the shipped page through an explicit `window.lgCompile` seam, not against an extracted copy that could drift). **REVISION 2026-07-25 (rev.2) — the register was misread; the decision above is deliberately NOT rewritten:** "technical logs and human logs" did not mean two WORDINGS of one event, it meant two different SUBJECTS — the business logic under test (what the application does, and how the steps exercising it end) versus the tool doing the testing (brain, LLM, planner, healing, driver, control-api). That distinction already EXISTED in the product — the ADR-067 source axis — merely named differently and one level finer than the question being asked. So: **(1)** an **audience** layer now sits above the sources (`brain/events.json` → `audiences`: `business` = application+testing, `tool` = the tool; the catalogue gate requires audiences to partition sources EXACTLY — a source in two audiences, or in none, would make the coarse filter lie about its coverage). The "Source" dropdown became "Logs" with `<optgroup>` grouping in which the group heading is itself selectable, and in the filter language a `src` value may name an audience: `src == business` expands to the union of the sources it contains, so `==`, `!=` and `in {…}` agree instead of disagreeing about the same word. **(2)** The register toggle collapsed into a **"detailed"** checkbox: a row always reads as a sentence in the reader's language, and the box ADDS the machine identity (full timestamp, event code, module, source) rather than replacing it. Two registers were a fourth dimension nobody asked for, and they also made `msg` mean two things — a different string depending on a toggle. One register, one answer: `msg` is the visible phrase, and finding an event by its server-side identity is what `code` is for (exact, identical in both languages, printed on every row under "detailed"). The view gate went 22→23: the additivity of "detailed" (the old check only demanded that the two registers read DIFFERENTLY, which any pair of unrelated wordings satisfies) and a set-theoretic audience check (`business` = `application ∪ testing`, `in {business}` = `== business`, an audience plus its negation = everything). Mutations: 5 on the catalogue, 7 on the view — all caught, and two of them only because of a second fixture: the first draft of the audience check ran against `l2.html`, which BEHAVES and therefore emits no application records at all, so `src == business` and `src == application \|\| src == testing` agreed on zero and the "compare the audience name as a plain string" mutation passed. The gate now spawns a second run against `l7-appfaults.html` and ASSERTS NON-EMPTINESS before asserting equality: an identity over empty sets checks nothing. | Accepted | One run has two readers — a tester and a developer — and switching between their languages must not cost the filters or the position. **Rejected:** Wireshark's language wholesale (its fields are about packets); two subtabs instead of a toggle (find a line in one, hunt for it again in the other); vendoring a parser (CSP + air-gap); two-way sync between controls and text (a classic source of disagreement — an honest "custom" state instead); blanking the view on an invalid expression (it is invalid most of the time while typing); showing source and category on the same row (source is a function of category, so it prints one word twice). **67→68 ADR.** |
| ADR-069 | 2026-07-25 | **Stopping a run, and an honest store marker — the interface must be able to say what state the system is in** — two M9-LIVE-UX findings of one class: the UI could not tell the truth. (1) **A run could not be stopped.** A goal-mode run against a real site walks for minutes and a stuck one until agentctl's own timeout; the only recourse was killing the container, losing every other run's artifacts. `POST /v1/runs/{id}/cancel` (token-gated, `validRunID`) kills a **tree**, not a process: control-api → agentctl → the Python brain → the Playwright executor → Chromium. So a run gets its OWN process group at spawn (`setProcGroup`, with platform files `procgroup_unix.go` / `procgroup_windows.go` on the `peercred_*` pattern): on Unix one signal to `-pgid` reaches all of it, on Windows `taskkill /T /F` walks the tree (there a surviving Chromium would hold the trace file open, and an open handle also blocks deleting the artifact directory). SIGTERM first with a 3 s grace — the executor closes its trace and the brain writes what it has, because the reason to cancel is usually to look at what happened so far; SIGKILL only if the tree stays. **A cancelled run is state `canceled`, not `failed`:** a killed process exits −1, indistinguishable from a crash, so the deliberate stop is recorded by a flag the waiting goroutine reads. Cancelling an already-finished run answers 200 "already stopped" rather than an error: a second click, or one that raced the exit, should not need interpreting. Mutation-proven that the group is load-bearing: a binary signalling only the top process leaves 2 orphans of 3 (the brain and the executor); the fixed one leaves 0. (2) **Silently empty lists.** In the standalone tier `scenarios/tests/chats/results/trends` answered an empty 200, and an empty 200 means BOTH "nothing saved yet" and "this deployment saves nothing"; Alex read it as "the library does not load", the correct reading of an interface that says nothing. A 501 is wrong here (an empty list is a valid answer when a store exists), so the fact travels with the data: `store: false` plus a `store_reason` that names the remedy (`--profile store` + `CONTROL_API_STORE_ADDR`), and a banner in the UI. | Accepted | An operator must be able to distinguish "I stopped it" from "it broke", and "empty" from "not working". **Rejected:** signalling only the agentctl process (2 orphans of 3 — measured); SIGKILL immediately (it would lose the trace people cancel in order to read); answering 501 for an empty list (emptiness is legitimate with a live store); exposing store presence as its own endpoint (a client would forget to ask — which is how the original ambiguity arose). **68→69 ADR.** |
| ADR-070 | 2026-07-25 | **A per-element retry budget: exploration stops hammering what will not act** — a live run on 2026-07-23 showed the same click 34 times in a row, roughly every 5 seconds, until `max_steps`: a whole run's budget spent on one button, and reported as exploration. The root cause is an asymmetry in `graph.py::act` — success marks the element `interactive_exercised` and so removes it from the candidate set, while failure marked NOTHING, so `plan` proposed the same element on the very next step. Nothing changed between attempts by construction: the explore graph's `heal` node is a stub (healing belongs to replay, ADR-047), so a third attempt could only produce the same exception. **Decision — a failure count PER ELEMENT** (`interactive_failed: dict`, semantic_id → how many times acting on it raised) with a threshold `_EXPLORE_FAIL_LIMIT` (env `SENTINEL_EXPLORE_FAIL_LIMIT`, default **2**, not 1: the first failure is worth a retry — an overlay mid-animation, a control that enables a beat later — while the second says the page is not going to change its mind). The catalogued `plan.element_blacklisted` is written exactly on the crossing, once rather than per attempt, or the log would recreate the very noise this removes. The same budget covers **navigate candidates**: a link that cannot be followed looped in exactly the same way. **A second, cheaper layer — perception reports `disabled`** (the attribute OR `aria-disabled`: the attribute is only valid on form controls, so a `<div role=button>` can only say so through aria). `pw-executor` REPORTS it and does not filter on it: perception describes the page, the brain decides. A disabled element is not offered as a candidate but **stays in `interactive_seen`** — it is part of the page and is usually enabled later in a filled form, and dropping it from perception would report coverage over a smaller page than the one under test. `plan_hash` is unaffected: it hashes the STEPS, and no step carries this field (`state.py::canonical_plan_hash`). When candidates run out with something still unreachable, `plan.unactionable_elements` is written once with the coverage percentage — at exactly the point the reader asks "why 50%, I can see ten buttons". **A side effect that had to be worked through:** two M14 tests (ADR-055) built their run of consecutive misses on retrying ONE element forever, and the fix broke them. Their claims ("a successful verify resets the streak", "threshold 0 never arms HITL") remain true — the FIXTURE was rewritten so consecutive misses come from DIFFERENT elements, which is the honest model of the world after this change. Both claims were re-checked by mutation and still bite. The DOM gate's repeat-collapsing check went the same way: that loop was what gave it a ×N row, and the new `plan.element_blacklisted` line lands between identical records and breaks the consecutive run. The repeat moved to where it belongs — the APPLICATION (`l7-appfaults.html` now prints the same error 8 times running): an app stuck in a broken render loop is the real case for collapsing, whereas the tool repeating itself was a defect. The check also gained an invariant no fixture change can quietly make vacuous: no two ADJACENT rows may read identically. | Accepted | Exploration should spend its budget on the page, not on one control; and it should not quietly report "reached max_steps" where the truth is "one button would not act". **Rejected:** retry with exponential backoff (a pause changes nothing — heal is a stub in explore, so it is the same loop, slower); dropping `disabled` from perception entirely (coverage would be measured over a truncated page, and a form's submit enables later); counting a blocked element as covered (coverage would stop measuring what was actually exercised, and the failure would become invisible); raising the threshold to 3 to make the M14 tests pass (that is fitting the product to a fixture — their scenery broke, not their claims). **ADR 69→70.** |
| ADR-071 | 2026-07-26 | **Interface drift is a first-class verdict outcome, not a counter; re-bind is separated from re-ground** — Alex's framing: "tests must REPORT a problem with the interface, not adapt to it; fixing test problems caused by DOM elements — yes, adapting to the result — no". Checking the code showed the healing mechanics are in fact right and its **reporting** is not: a run that needed five heals reported `exit 0` and a bare `healed: 5`, so a green build was **indistinguishable** from a green build under an interface that had moved. Worse, the counter merged two different events. **Decision — the drift class is derived from the FROZEN plan:** if the strategy that worked is present in the step's `alternatives[]` (captured at freeze time) it is a **re-bind** — the same element by another key, the legitimate test repair healing exists for; if the strategy was not there, it was produced at heal time from the page AS IT IS NOW (`css` from `_llm_reground`, `visual` from set-of-marks) — that is a **re-ground**, and nothing in the pipeline verifies element identity. Deriving the class from membership in `alternatives[]` rather than from a hardcoded strategy list is deliberate: the real question is "was this key frozen with the plan?", and an authored or imported plan may legitimately carry a `css` alternative — in which case using it IS a re-bind. **The report names what, where and how:** the element (human handle + `semantic_id`), page, step, strategy, confidence, outcome (`auto_healed`/`flagged`/`cache_hit`) and the **before→after** locator — `drift.elements[]` in `heal-report.json` plus an "Interface drift" table in the HTML report. Three catalogued events: `heal.drift_rebind` (info), `heal.drift_reground` (**warn + `degrades`**, because it is the only path where the test could have bound a different element) and `heal.drift_summary`. **Verdict:** the state `pass_with_drift` is set WHENEVER drift occurred and travels on the AG-UI `verdict` frame — a reader need not open a report to learn "passed, but the interface moved". **The exit code stays 0 by default:** healing exists so a cosmetic DOM change does not stop a pipeline, and reddening out of the box would teach teams to switch the feature off. `SENTINEL_FAIL_ON_HEAL=N` (0 = off) reddens the build at N or more drifted elements. | Accepted | A tool that silently adapts to a changed interface stops being testing. The mechanism to speak already existed — the `flagged` outcome and the `healing_audit` table — but it spoke into a file rather than into the verdict. **Rejected:** a sixth exit code (the `0/1/2/3/-1` catalogue is a published contract and a sixth value would break consumers that switch on it); `exit 2` on threshold (its label reads "golden/visual regression", which a re-bound locator is not, so reusing it would make the label lie; `exit 1` — "the test found a problem" — was chosen because the test did find something: the interface changed); reddening on drift by default (every renamed testid would break a build); storing the drift class in the heal cache (the cache is keyed on the page hash, and the class is recoverable from the strategy without it). Surfaced incidentally during implementation: a ternary `log("a" if x else "b")` **does not pass the catalogue gate** — correctly, since the catalogue cannot vouch for a code it does not see as a literal. **ADR 70→71.** |
| ADR-072 | 2026-07-26 | **The tested application's own faults reach the verdict — the EMITTER counts, because the brain cannot see them** — a run could report `exit 0` PASSED while the page threw exceptions and answered 5xx throughout. A live run on 2026-07-26 against `l7-appfaults.html` showed it literally: three steps passed, `exit=0`, **13 application faults, 12 of them errors** — and not a word on the verdict. **The fact that determined the design:** `app.*` events are printed by the executor to stderr, and `brain/executor.py:21` does `stderr=sys.stderr` — it **inherits rather than pipes**. So the brain never saw them; the only parser was control-API's log sink, which counts AFTER the process has already exited with its verdict. Hence the **emitter** counts: `pw-executor` keeps a per-code tally in `appLog` (a counter already lived there for `app.log_capped`) and returns it through a new `browser.appFaults` RPC; the brain asks at report time. The method deliberately **does not require a live browser** — by report time the page may be closed, and the tally of a run that never launched is a correct answer, not an error. Fail-open by construction: an older executor without the method, or a dead one, yields an empty tally — a run must not fail because we could not count how badly the AUT behaved. **The gateable subset is `errors`, not `total`:** a thrown exception, a console error, a failed request, 4xx/5xx. A console WARNING and a dialog are reported but not counted as errors — gating on warnings would make the feature unusable on any real application, which means it would be switched off. **Verdict:** `pass_with_app_faults`, ranked above drift (ADR-071), because a tester who sees both wants THEIR bug first while drift is our maintenance. `SENTINEL_FAIL_ON_APP_ERRORS=N` (0 = off) reddens the build, and a red build **names its cause** — `problem_app_faults`, not a generic `problem`, or the reader goes hunting for a failed step that does not exist (a weakness I found in my own implementation while testing the gating live). A real step failure keeps the generic word: then the step IS the story. | Accepted | The product exists to find problems in the application; a count of its faults is a RESULT, not diagnostics. **Rejected:** counting in `logsink.go` (the data is there, but by then the process has exited — it can report, not gate; and in pure CLI mode there is no sink at all); piping the executor's stderr through the brain (would make the brain responsible for relaying all output, with ordering and deadlock risk, and would break the "executor logs go straight up" property); gating on `total` (see above); reddening by default (every live application has console warnings). `app.faults_summary` is marked `degrades`; the tally failure is a separate `system.app_faults_unavailable` with category `system`, because it is OUR counter failing and filing it under the "application" source would lie to the audience filter (ADR-068 rev.2). **ADR 71→72.** |
| ADR-073 | 2026-07-26 | **JUnit XML — the machine contract every CI expects** — before it, integrating with a pipeline meant "read the exit code". GitLab, Jenkins, GitHub Actions and TeamCity all consume JUnit XML to render a test list with names, durations and failure text, and none of them can do anything with a bare exit code. Nothing needed computing: the whole report already sat in `heal-report.json`, only the shape was missing. **THE MAPPING IS THE DESIGN**, because JUnit has four outcome shapes and we have more: step ok → `<testcase>` with no child · step failed → `<failure>` · **quarantined step → `<skipped>`** (ADR-013 suppresses its contribution to exit 1; showing it as passed would hide a known flake and as failed would cry wolf — `skipped` is JUnit's word for exactly this) · golden regression → `<failure type="regression">` · **re-bind → `<system-out>`, re-ground → `<system-err>`** (both steps PASSED so neither is a failure, but "we chose a new selector and did not verify it is the same element" is precisely what a reviewer must see without opening another artefact, and stderr is CI's standard channel for "passed, but read this") · **application faults → `<system-err>` on the SUITE**, not on a step (the emitter cannot attribute a console error to a step, and pretending it can would be a lie) · integrity abort → **`<error>`** on a synthetic case (exit 3 means nothing executed, so there is nothing to attach to; zero tests would read as "nothing to run", and `failure` is JUnit's word for a failed assertion whereas `error` means the harness itself broke). `failures`/`errors` in the header are counted **from the emitted children** rather than from the report's own tallies, so the header can never disagree with the body. Output is deterministic (the timestamp comes from the report, not the clock) — this is a CI artefact and a diff of two runs must show what changed in the RUN. Written alongside `report.html`/`report.json`/`metrics.prom` rather than behind a flag: a reporter nobody enables is a reporter nobody has. | Accepted | Missing JUnit is a visible sign of immaturity that costs more than many features: without it we are not considered. **Rejected:** hiding it behind a flag (see above); Allure (needs a runtime and a plugin — against self-containment); nesting application faults inside steps (the emitter does not know the step); showing quarantine as passed or failed (see the mapping); taking header counts from the report (the header would drift from the body — a mutation confirmed it). **ADR 72→73.** |
| ADR-074 | 2026-07-26 | **The token travels between our own pages over a same-origin channel — "one tab" stops being a condition of working** — by ADR-064's construction the nonce is single-use, and the startup banner prints a ROOT link only (`cmd/control-api/main.go`), so a `/setup/` opened second had nowhere to get a token and soft-refused to save. The wizard's code was never at fault: its bootstrap is byte-for-byte the hub's and works when handed a nonce — it was simply unreachable. **The fix is a request/response relay over `BroadcastChannel('sentinel-capi')`**: a page NEVER announces a token it holds; it only answers a page that asks, and it only asks when its own field is empty. No storage is involved at all — the token still lives in tab memory, and a channel is a browser object rather than storage, so ADR-032/061/064's "the bearer token is never persisted" holds literally. The answering side keeps the channel open for the tab's lifetime DELIBERATELY: closing it after one exchange would mean a wizard opened ten minutes later gets silence, which is precisely the situation being fixed. The guards are the existing bootstrap's: `http:`/`https:` only, and only when the constructor exists (it throws on `file://`, and an uncaught throw is a red gate). Fixed alongside it: the reason the defect went unnoticed — **nothing anywhere linked to the wizard** (`grep -in setup docs/index.html` returned zero). The link now sits beside the token field it needs, with one back to the app. **Relation to ADR-066**, which states "no BroadcastChannel" and lists this mechanism as rejected: that clause was about TWO COPIES OF THE CHAT and about dragging a secret into storage. The wizard duplicates no hub view and no storage is touched — ADR-074 narrows the clause to its actual reason rather than quietly outrunning it. **What the relay widens, stated plainly:** while a tab holding the token is open, any same-origin document can ask for and receive it. On the serving origin that origin IS the control-API, which hands the token to its own pages by design; a hostile document there would mean the API is serving hostile content, and the token would be the second question. On Pages / `file://` / a split deployment the relay never installs. The hub gate grew a check with a NEGATIVE control: a tab inherits the token from an open sibling AND fails to get one when no sibling holds it — without the second half the first proves nothing. | Accepted | A single-use nonce plus a memory-only token left the second page unusable. Rejected: `localStorage` (breaks ADR-061) · a second one-time link in the terminal (5-minute TTL — a wizard opened later is empty again) · folding the wizard into a hub rail view (strategically right, but a manual move of ~1100 lines, i.e. the redesign epic, while the token breaks work today). |
| ADR-075 | 2026-07-26 | **A file tier for config: the standalone deployment gets what the code already promised it** — `PUT /v1/config` with no store-gateway answered 501 reading "this deployment keeps its config in a file (standalone tier)", while **no such file existed** and the 501 returned before the body was even read: the wizard saved into nothing and reported it as a soft refusal. The readiness detail pointed at `brain/runconfig.py` — a YAML the OPERATOR passes and the server never writes. **The fix:** `state/config.json` (0600, atomic temp+rename) as a real medium when no store is configured; the document, the key and the validation are IDENTICAL across tiers — only the medium differs — so a deployment that later gains a store-gateway is not configuring a different product. Validation (`configguard.Validate` + `validateLoggingSection`) moved BEFORE the tier decision: the old order refused a good document and a malformed one identically. The file is read by `getPersistedLLM`, `loadStartupConfig` and readiness alike — otherwise ADR-063's layer-3 promise would hold only where a store happened to be wired. **`s.store == nil` is split into the two different situations it conflated:** no address configured = the operator chose standalone, and the file IS the config; address SET but the gateway silent at boot (`main.go` is fail-open and never re-dials) = writing a file would be exactly the silent degradation this milestone closes, because the gateway returns, wins the next read, and the saved settings vanish with no message. That case answers an honest 503 and leaves no file to be shadowed later. The response names the `tier`, and the wizard names the medium — "saved" without it was the shape of the old lie. A missing file in standalone stays `skipped` rather than `error`: running purely from the process env is a legitimate deployment and a 503 would call it broken (the service tier treats `rec == nil` as an error for the opposite reason — pointing at a gateway declares that a stored config is expected). | Accepted | The code promised a file tier that did not exist. Rejected: an honest refusal alone (the wizard still could not save) · falling back to a file on any nil store (silent loss of settings once the gateway returns). |
| ADR-076 | 2026-07-26 | **The verdict the run itself pronounced reaches the store and the hub — with no schema change** — ADR-071/072 taught brain to distinguish `pass_with_drift` / `pass_with_app_faults` / `problem_drift` / `problem_app_faults`, but the Go side derived `ResultRecord.verdict` INDEPENDENTLY from the exit code (`verdictEnum`), so the Results domain and the hub saw only the coarse four and the whole distinction died at the process boundary (a comment in `brain/replay.py` named the debt outright). **No schema is needed:** `ResultRecord.verdict` in `store.proto` is ALREADY a `string`, and `MetricPoint` is a generic `name/value`, so the counts `drift_total`/`drift_rebind`/`drift_reground`/`app_faults_total`/`app_faults_errors` ride the existing metrics domain as new points. Zero stub regeneration, zero SQLite migration — and `protoc` is not installed while the store has only `CREATE TABLE IF NOT EXISTS`, so the alternative would have cost a migration for data the artifact and the metrics already carry. **The run's word wins, but through a whitelist:** `verdict` is a free string in a file on disk, and passing it through would admit a value into the domain that no reader can render; an unknown state — including one from a NEWER brain — degrades to the exit code, the same rule the Logs view applies to an unknown event code (ADR-065). Points are emitted only when the run actually produced the block: explore/goal runs carry no heal-report at all, and "there was no drift" is not the same fact as "this mode cannot report drift"; zeros from the second would pollute a series such a run has no business appearing in. Pass-rate arithmetic is untouched: `pass` remains an exit-code fact, because a run that passed with drift still passed. In the hub, drift and application faults are shown SEPARATELY even when only one of them named the verdict: they are different news for different people — drift is our maintenance, a fault is their bug — while a verdict can only be one word. | Accepted | The ADR-071/072 distinction did not survive the process boundary. Rejected: `drift_json`/`app_faults_json` fields on `ResultRecord` (protoc + Go/Python stub regeneration + ALTER TABLE with no migration framework — to duplicate what the artifact and the metrics already carry). |
| ADR-077 | 2026-07-26 | **Degradation that actually happened reaches the verdict — the catalogue knew, nothing read it** — `brain/events.json` has always marked which codes mean LOST QUALITY (no LLM key, a spent budget, locators re-grounded from the live page) and has always carried a `{ru,en}_verdict` sentence saying what that means FOR THE RESULT. Neither field was ever read: a run that finished without an LLM produced a verdict, a `report.html` and a `junit.xml` indistinguishable from a clean run, and the only trace sat in a log file nobody opens when the build is green. Wave 1 warned only BEFORE a run (`bLlmGuard`) and by construction could know nothing about a key that turned out to be rejected or a budget spent mid-run. **The fix carries the same 19 codes to three surfaces:** (1) the hub reads `degradations` from `GET /v1/runs/{id}/logs` — NOT from the report, because a report exists only for replay/baseline while the commonest degradation of all ("no key -> heuristic") happens on the goal/explore runs that have no report at all; (2) `brain/eventlog.py` tallies the codes that fired and `replay.py` writes `report["degradations"]` beside `app_faults`, the same way ADR-072 introduced application faults; (3) `report.py` renders a block and `junit.py` adds `<property name="degradations">` plus `system-out` on the SUITE (not a failure: the run passed — it passed with less than it was meant to have). **The load-bearing detail is that the tally is taken BEFORE the level filter.** Whether the run degraded is a fact about the RUN; whether the reader wanted warnings is a fact about the VIEW. Recording after the filter would mean `SENTINEL_LOG_LEVEL=error` produced a cleaner verdict for the same run — the mechanism against silent degradation silently degrading. A mutation proved it: moving the tally past the filter SURVIVED at first, because the test varied the environment without resetting `_levels()`'s memo, so the "quiet" run had never been quiet. `logSink.degradations()` was deleted along the way — a dead in-memory twin with no production caller whose comment promised exactly what the HTTP path now does; its dedup-and-order assertion was not dropped but moved to the test of the endpoint that actually serves the verdict. | Accepted | The catalogue carried both the flag and the sentence; nothing read either. Rejected: sourcing degradations from the report (explore/goal have none — i.e. most genuinely degrading runs) · keeping the sink's in-memory copy (a twin that lives only as long as the process). |
| ADR-078 | 2026-07-26 | **A `file://` target the container cannot see warns BEFORE the spawn — with the ones it can see listed beside it** — live runs went out as `file:///D:/Projects/sentinel/...`, the operator's own HOST path, died on `ERR_FILE_NOT_FOUND` with exit 1, and said nothing about which problem the test had found. The only validation was `validTarget`, a SCHEME check for which such a path is impeccable. **A warning rather than a block, and on the client rather than the server:** a natively-run `agentctl` legitimately targets host paths (the README documents `file://$PWD/testdata/m0.html`), so a hard 400 would break correct use; what is wrong is not the shape of the path but the mismatch between it and WHERE the run will execute — and only the operator knows that. The sentence is reused WORD FOR WORD from `bExtractCause`, which already explained this same failure AFTER the run: a warning before and an explanation after, phrased differently, would read as two different problems. **There are two fields, and that is the heart of the fix:** `#b-target` and `#ch-target` are separate run paths, and fixing one would have left the chat path dying exactly as before. Plus a `<datalist>` of the nine bundled fixtures by their IN-CONTAINER paths — a `<datalist>`, not a `<select>`, because a real http(s) target remains the normal case.
