The list is read from `testdata/fixtures/` rather than guessed: there is no `l6.html`, only `l6-newtab.html`, and an invented entry here would be the same silent no-op an invented CSS class is. | Accepted | The scheme was checked, reachability was not. Rejected: a hard 400 server-side (breaks native runs) · `<select>` instead of `<datalist>` (closes the field to real targets) · fixing only the #build form (leaves chat broken). |
| ADR-079 | 2026-07-26 | **A run becomes self-describing: a replay freezes the plan it executed, and artefacts name the model that produced them** — both defects were found by a CLEAN END-TO-END RUN on `main`; neither was reachable by reading. **(1) A replay could never be replayed.** `resolveFromRun` looks for a frozen plan inside the run's OWN directory and a replay wrote only its report there, so `has_plan` was false forever and the re-run controls greyed out permanently — even though the plan was perfectly well known, having arrived via `from_run`. The fix is `executed-plan.json`: the plan this run ACCEPTED and ran, frozen into its own directory. The name is deliberately NOT `plan.json`: in an explore run that name means "the plan this run PRODUCED" and `persistResult` reads `coverage_achieved` out of it, so reusing it would attribute the original explore's coverage to every replay of it — buying a shorter name with a silently corrupted metric. It is written immediately AFTER the integrity gate and before the first step: a run that hard-aborted on a `plan_hash` mismatch executed nothing, and putting its rejected plan one click from a re-run is exactly what ADR-006 refuses to do. The copy sits LAST in `replayInputs`: a run that produced its own plan should replay that, not a copy of someone else's. As a side effect the artefact directory became self-sufficient — hand someone a replay's directory now and they can re-run it. **(2) The model id reached nothing.** The transcript showed `qwen3:14b` and 551 tokens while `plan.json` carried `models: {plan: null}` and `scenario.json` carried neither `models` nor `tokens`. The cause: `graph.py` read `.model` off the EXPLORE planner, which is heuristic by default and has no such attribute at all, while the head that actually called the LLM has one. The consequence travelled: `persistResult` received an empty model, `cost_usd` came out 0 (correct for a local model) and — more importantly — the `model` label on EVERY metric point came out blank, and that label is the seam a commercial cross-project rollup groups on (ADR-056). Both roles are now recorded (`explore`/`author`) while `plan` keeps naming the single spender for pricing: both heads resolve through the same `make_backend("planner")` and therefore the same model. `scenario.json` gained `models`+`tokens` because a scenario is the artefact people HAND EACH OTHER, and one that cannot name its model is not reproducible by whoever receives it. | Accepted | Found by running, not by reading. Rejected: copying the plan as `plan.json` (corrupts the coverage metric) · storing a reference to the source run instead of a copy (needs a `RunRecord` field → proto + migration, and introduces a reference chain that would have to be walked recursively) · freezing the plan before the integrity gate (offers a re-run of the plan the gate just rejected). |
| ADR-080 | 2026-07-26 | **A heal applied without full confidence stops being invisible, and "a re-ground is never accepted silently" becomes a rule rather than arithmetic** — `docs/SELF_HEALING.md` §194 described the 0.60–0.84 band as "apply optimistically **+ set `review_required=true` + show in the report's healing-audit section**". Only the first third was ever built: `review_required` appeared in the repository ZERO times, and `flagged` appeared in neither `report.py`, `junit.py` nor the UI. The tool repaired itself with a locator it did not vouch for and produced a verdict, a report and a JUnit file indistinguishable from a clean run. **Behaviour does NOT change** — optimistic application was chosen deliberately (ADR-005/017); only the silence does. The fact rides the **ADR-077 degradation channel** rather than a new verdict word: a run can drift AND fault AND heal unverified at once while the verdict is ONE word, so a fifth state would breed combinatorics; `degrades` already reaches the hub badge, the report block and the JUnit `<property>`. It is counted from `drift.elements`, where every applied heal already carries `outcome` and `confidence` — no second bookkeeping to drift out of step with the first. **The subtler half:** "a re-ground is never accepted silently" held ONLY by arithmetic — css scores `PRIORS["css"] × 0.90 = 0.585` against `FLAG = 0.60`, a margin of 0.015, and the test pinned the number 0.585. Raising a prior or softening the discount to 0.95 would have pushed an unverified locator into the band that EXECUTES (`replay.py` applies `auto_healed`/`flagged`/`cache_hit` alike) with nothing failing. It is now an explicit cap in an extracted `_gate`, and the test **creates the future the rule guards** — raising a prior to 0.99 and demanding the re-ground still not become `auto_healed`; a mutation showed that without such a test, deleting the rule is caught by nothing, because no re-ground reaches `AUTO` today. Thresholds moved to `SENTINEL_HEAL_AUTO`/`SENTINEL_HEAL_FLAG` (garbage falls back, as with every other env threshold), and the `PRIORS` docstring says plainly: these are PRIORS, not probabilities; there is no confidence model and no calibration. Three mitigations that never existed were removed from `GAPS.md` (`GAP-RISK-002` "threshold 0.90 until N labelled records", `GAP-RISK-005` `completeness_ratio`, `GAP-RISK-008` the proto-hash CI gate), and `GAP-RISK-002` was reworded from "cold start" to "no confidence model exists". | Accepted | The design was one-third implemented, and the missing two thirds were exactly the parts that tell a human. Rejected: a new `pass_with_unverified_heal` verdict state (combinatorics against drift and app faults with a single-word verdict) · ceasing to act on `flagged` (reverses ADR-005/017; green runs turn red) · leaving the thresholds alone and fixing only the docs (the re-ground rule stays an arithmetic coincidence). |
| ADR-081 | 2026-07-26 | **Write-side redaction of the tested application's output; log retention is opt-in rather than silent** — ADR-067 gave seven `app.*` codes the job of carrying what SOMEONE ELSE'S application printed into `runs/<id>/logs/` (that is the point: "is my app misbehaving or the tool?" is otherwise unanswerable), and ADR-072 made that stream VERDICT-BEARING, so we write more of it. Nothing redacted it: the sink stored the message exactly as it arrived, protected only by `0700` on the directory and a bearer token on the read endpoint, and any `tar runs/<id>` or log pasted into a bug report carried a `console.log` with a session token, a URL with `?token=`, a 5xx body, the text of a dialog. **One choke point** — `logSink.write`, from which all three files (`run.log`, `run.jsonl`, `events.jsonl`) descend — so a secret cannot reach disk by another route; EVERY line is redacted rather than only `app.*` ones, because our own diagnostics quote URLs and request bodies, and a redactor that trusted the source would be trusting the very thing it exists to check. **What is NOT matched matters more than what is:** only NAMED secrets and one structurally unambiguous shape (a JWT). There is deliberately no entropy or "long hex/base64" rule, though GAPS proposed one: our own audit trail is made of exactly that shape — `run_id=868db3496d004a1c`, `plan_hash`, `dom_hash`, sha256 goldens — and a rule broad enough to catch an unknown token would eat the identifiers these files exist to preserve, silently. Better a redactor with stated limits than one that quietly destroys the evidence. **The vocabulary is not redefined** — it is `configguard.Secretish`, the same function that refuses a secret-shaped config member, so the two cannot disagree about the word `token` (and `max_tokens`/`total_tokens` stay counters). **A scanner rather than a regex, and that is a correction rather than taste:** a regex match CONSUMES its span, so `[app] console: session_token=SECRET` matched as the outer pair `console: …`, `console` was not credential-shaped, the pair was returned untouched — and the scan resumed PAST the secret. Same with `https://host/cb?token=SECRET`. The gate caught both, and both are the kind of miss that makes a security control worse than useless: it reports success while the credential is on disk. The hand-written scanner visits every separator independently. A mutation then showed that a guard inherited from the regex version ("value must not start with `/`") had become both unnecessary and harmful — it let `api_key=/opt/creds/id_rsa` through; removed, with a test. **Retention (`SENTINEL_LOG_KEEP`/`SENTINEL_LOG_TTL_HOURS`) defaults to OFF**, unlike traces: a trace is a bulky by-product, the logs ARE the diagnosis, and an upgrade that silently deleted a user's evidence would be a worse failure than the gap being closed. Redaction removes the credentials; retention is disk hygiene the operator opts into. The distinction is load-bearing: a TTL that quietly ran would look like security while doing nothing about the secret already written. Only `logs/` is removed — `plan.json`, the reports and `executed-plan.json` stay, so a swept run remains auditable and replayable. | Accepted | We wrote other people's secrets into our own files and handed them out. Rejected: an entropy heuristic (eats `run_id`/`plan_hash`/goldens — our audit trail) · a second secret-name vocabulary beside `configguard` (it would drift) · redacting only `app.*` (our own lines quote their URLs) · retention on by default (deleting a user's evidence unasked). |
| ADR-082 | 2026-07-27 | **Element identity on a re-ground: a predicate, not a score** — "verify-before-accept" (`healing.py` step 6) established exactly one thing: the candidate resolves to ONE element. The comment beside it said honestly what that does NOT establish — "one match is one match, not the right one" — and with ADR-071 the product learned to NAME a re-ground without verifying it. **There IS something to compare against, and that was found by measurement rather than by reasoning:** a step's primary locator carries `role`+`name` in 1026 of 1026 steps across every artifact in the repository, so the check needs no new field in the plan, no `plan_hash` break and no new RPC. (The backlog's wording, "compare against the frozen `semantic_id`", is unimplementable literally: it is a truncated SHA-1 and is irreversible.) **Three outcomes and not one new number:** `verified` (the role and the normalized name matched) · `contradicted` · `unverifiable` (the plan froze no name, or the tier did not report what it found). There is deliberately no threshold — the `PRIORS` in that same file are admitted to be unmeasured (GAP-RISK-002), and a scored measure of identity would be a SECOND uncalibrated number beside the first; a property, by contrast, is checked by a mutation, whereas a number is checked by nothing. **The check is strictly stronger than the probe that produced the candidate:** `buildLocator` passes `{name}` to `getByRole` without `exact`, so Playwright matches the name case-insensitively and BY SUBSTRING — "Pay" is satisfied by "Pay now". Equality is not, and that is exactly the class of wrong binding being closed. **What `contradicted` does (Alex's decision of 2026-07-26): degradation, not refusal** — the heal is applied, because a renamed control is the commonest drift and repairing it is why the product exists, but it is applied VISIBLY: its own code in the catalogue, `degrades: true`, a "before→after" line in the drift report and a sentence on the verdict. `unverifiable` is fail-open ON THE DATA: punishing an old plan for a decision it never made is unfair, but passing it off as verified is not allowed either. **The text tier no longer authors a selector** and picks an INDEX into the list the executor has already reported (`browser.interactives`) — the same grounding pattern as `planner.py` (`candidates[idx]`, ADR-022/027): this was the only place in the product where an LLM invented a selector, and simultaneously the only one whose identity is structurally unverifiable (a CSS string cannot be read to learn what it found). Coverage is not lost, and that was checked against BOTH producers of plans: in an explore plan an element outside the executor's perception could not have reached the plan either (the map is built from that same `browser.interactives`); in a plan RECORDED by the recorder `css`/`xpath` do occur (`record_bridge.py` derives the strategy from the key of the locator actually observed), so such an element CAN be in a plan — but the old tier could not heal it anyway: it scored 0.585 against FLAG 0.60 and went to `needs_review`, i.e. the step was not applied. What narrowed is unreachable coverage, not reachable coverage. Confidence is the prior of the resulting locator's TYPE × the same 0.90 discount that was already being applied, then the ADR-080 cap; no new constants introduced. **Two defects surfaced along the way and were fixed:** the css tier NEVER applied (0.585 against FLAG 0.60) and at the same time SUPPRESSED the visual tier — the only one actually applied — because `heal` tries visual only `if not chosen`; and `cache_hit` returned BEFORE `_gate`, so a re-ground accepted optimistically once was thereafter replayed at the full stored confidence, past the cap and past any verification. **`is_reground` is brought to ADR-071's definition** (membership in the frozen `alternatives[]`) instead of the literal `{css, visual}`: one and the same heal could be a re-bind in the report and a re-ground at the gate, and there was no test that the two agree. **The residual hole is named plainly:** two controls indistinguishable by role and name will pass the check — that is the "is it the same INSTANCE" question, and it is closed only by the surroundings; what did change is that the case is now COUNTABLE (`probe_count >= 2` reaches the heal context), and that is the future labelled signal. | Accepted | The tool repaired a test with a new key and said nothing about the element possibly being a different one; importing other people's tests (`PROD-IMPORT`) lands on exactly this mechanism. Rejected: **comparing crops** (`screenshotHash` takes the whole page without `clip`, `golden_snapshots` has no element-level measurement, explore runs with no store at all, and we do not yet trust our own per-page visual goldens — plus an unavoidable perceptual threshold) · **comparing the surroundings** (the only thing that tells two identical controls apart, but it requires a new field in the step with a `plan_hash` break, an ancestor walk in the executor and NECESSARILY a scored threshold — after this ADR, not instead of it) · **`browser.describe`** (the index tier removes the need; it also sidesteps the fact that `-32000` is the executor's only error code, while on the MCP transport an unknown method returns a SUCCESSFUL result) · **refusing a re-ground below the threshold and handing it to a human** (replay has no interruption and resumption — today that is silently the same as "the step failed"). |
| ADR-083 | 2026-07-27 | **One vocabulary of locator strategies; a prior from the plan can only LOWER confidence** — the vocabulary has two producers (`graph.py` for explore, `record_bridge.py` for the MV3 recorder) and one consumer (`healing.py`), and each kept a private copy. The copies drifted apart: the recorder wrote `text`, explore wrote `text_role`, and `healing.PRIORS` knew only the latter — so the text alternative of a RECORDED plan resolved to the unknown-strategy default of 0.5, **below FLAG 0.60**, i.e. it was found, discarded and never applied. Silently: no event, no line in the report, the step simply failed. The remedy is structural rather than spot: `brain/strategies.py` is the single source of the names, the priors and the "locator key → strategy" mapping, plus an offline gate that forbids a literal of the priors anywhere else in `brain/`. The module is deliberately import-light: a cheap import is exactly why `record_bridge` once started a copy of its own, and a solution that forced a choice between correctness and import weight would breed the same problem again. **The old spelling is an ALIAS, not a separate strategy** (`text` → `text_role`): plans recorded by the earlier recorder already exist and must go on being healed; the alias is NOT added to `PRIORS`, because two keys for one thing IS the divergence mechanism. `is_reground` canonicalizes BOTH sides of the comparison: canonicalizing one would declare a recorded plan's own key a re-ground and mark every such run as unverified drift. **The second half is the `prior` field:** both producers wrote it and NOBODY read it — so an importer carefully ranking the locators of somebody else's suite was ranking into the void. It is now read, but downwards only: `min(table, plan)`. Reading it as-is is not allowed — `testid` is worth 0.95 against AUTO 0.85, so any file could raise its own prior and smuggle a weak locator into the band that is applied silently, and an imported plan is by definition someone else's. Garbage (not a number, outside [0,1]) falls back to the table rather than failing the run — the same rule as for the confidence thresholds. | Accepted | This directly blocked `[PROD-IMPORT]`: a transpiler of other people's tests ranks locators, and its ranks would have gone to the 0.5 default without a single message. Rejected: **importing `healing` from `record_bridge`** (drags the LLM backends into an import-light bridge — precisely the reason for the original copy) · **treating `text` as a separate strategy with its own prior** (two names for one thing IS the source of divergence) · **reading the plan's prior as-is** (grants someone else's file the right to raise its own confidence) · **ceasing to write `prior`** (throws away the only channel through which an importer can express caution). |
| ADR-084 | 2026-07-27 | **Artifacts holding somebody else's data: the session file at 0600, the trace only for a run that is not green** — two places where the CUSTOMER's data (not our own secrets) sat in our files for no reason. **(1)** `browser.saveStorageState` writes an authenticated user's cookies+localStorage — that is, a LIVE session — with the process umask, typically `0644`. The asymmetry was obvious the moment anyone looked at it: we set the run directory to `0700` explicitly because PII is POSSIBLE there, while the file where tokens are GUARANTEED was not protected at all — and `STORAGE_STATE_SAVE` is an arbitrary path that typically lands next to the project rather than inside `runs/`. Now `chmodSync(path, 0o600)` immediately after the write, best-effort: a filesystem with no POSIX modes (a Windows share) must not fail the run — the state is saved either way. **(2)** `trace.zip` was saved ALWAYS. It carries the live DOM of the application under test (including `input.value`) and request bodies, and there is nothing to redact it with — Playwright has no mask API. So every green CI run left a copy of somebody else's application state on disk for an investigation nobody was going to make. The path is now passed to `browser.traceStop` only when `exit_code != 0`; with no path Playwright DISCARDS the buffer and the bytes never reach the disk at all — strictly better than writing and deleting, because no window remains in which the file exists. The threshold is `exit_code != 0` specifically, not "a step failed": a golden regression yields 2 without a single failed step, and that is exactly the case a human will want to look through frame by frame. `SENTINEL_TRACE_ALWAYS=1` restores the former behaviour for debugging a run that passes but behaves oddly; `PW_NO_TRACE=1` still outranks both. **A criterion that checked something other than what it was named for was fixed along the way:** explore judged itself successful by `trace.exists()` — asserting a BY-PRODUCT instead of the result (that the browser worked is better proved by `len(steps) >= 5`) — and after this ADR every SUCCESSFUL explore would have reported failure. | Accepted | We kept other people's data longer and wider than the work requires. Rejected: **writing the trace and deleting it after success** (a window remains in which the file is on disk, plus a needless write of a large file) · **gating on "a step failed"** (misses a golden regression — the most interesting case to investigate) · **keeping the trace always and relying on `SENTINEL_TRACE_KEEP`** (count-based pruning deletes the OLD, whereas the point is that a fresh green run should leave nothing at all). |
| ADR-085 | 2026-07-27 | **A setting that cannot be found does not exist for the person it is meant for: 14 operator settings surfaced into the wizard FROM THE SCHEMA** — the code reads 56 environment variables, while `config-schema` surfaced 15 fields, 6 of them about the LLM. Everything else was configurable only by `export`, i.e. discoverable only by reading the source — the same class `[DOCS-REGISTERS]` describes for the registries. The ones surfaced are those that change a run's BEHAVIOUR or the LIFETIME of its artifacts: retention (5), self-healing thresholds (4), build gates (3), human involvement (2). **There is one source** — `settingsSchema` in `handleConfigSchema`; the wizard renders the whole section out of it, so the next setting appears in the UI with no HTML edit, and `TestSetupWizardSchemaSnapshotMatchesHandler` keeps the snapshot from drifting apart. **`hint: {ru, en}`, not `note`** — and this is a correction to our own project rather than an ornament: `note` is deliberately NOT shown in the interface, because it is English while the wizard is bilingual, and showing an English string in a Russian UI is code-switching (the rule is already written into the preset code, ADR-061). A setting whose whole point is to be UNDERSTOOD cannot arrive in one language. **`min`/`max`/`step` travel WITH the field** instead of being set by branches on the key name: previously any new field fell into the `else` with `step=1000` — sensible for a token budget, meaningless for hours and for a 0..1 confidence. **Only CHANGED values are exported**, so that every line of the generated config means "I decided this" rather than "it came out that way". **A gate on the honesty of the numbers:** `TestSettingsSchemaDefaultsMatchCode` checks the schema's default against the literal where the variable is actually read — two numbers for one decision drift apart silently, and in the UI a wrong default is indistinguishable from a right one until somebody compares the behaviour with the form. The comparison is NUMERIC: Go prints `0.6` where Python writes `0.60`. | Accepted | We were asking the user to configure things whose existence they could learn only from the source. Rejected: **showing `note`** (code-switching, forbidden by ADR-061) · **leaving `min`/`step` in the wizard's branches** (a new setting would arrive with a meaningless step, and only the person who used it would notice) · **exporting every setting** (the config stops telling a decision from a default) · **surfacing all 56 variables** (`RUN_ID`, `PLAN_FILE`, `MCP_TRANSPORT` are invocation parameters, not settings; surfacing them would mean offering a human the decisions the agent makes). |
| ADR-086 | 2026-07-27 | **A collapsible hint on every field (the OPNsense idiom)** — the wizard's 17 hand-written fields explained NOTHING, while the settings surfaced by ADR-085 showed their explanation always, and both extremes are equally bad: a form where every field carries a paragraph is as unreadable as a form that explains nothing. The decision: the explanation is collapsed behind a small marker beside the label + a single "show all hints" toggle whose state is remembered. Collapsed BY DEFAULT deliberately — a reader who already knows the field should not have to scroll past its description; the text is nevertheless always present in the DOM, so reaching it costs nothing. `<details>`, not a div with a class: keyboard and screen-reader behaviour is taken from the platform rather than written by us. **Bilingual by construction** — BOTH variants are rendered as `data-lang` spans, and the choice is made by the body class that already governs the whole page; assembling the string through `tr()` at creation time would freeze the language current at that moment, and the language switch would leave these blocks behind (the first version did exactly that). The text is written about the CONSEQUENCE ("what happens if you change this") rather than restating the label: a hint that repeats the field's name is the same silence, only wordier. | Accepted | A setting with no explanation is an environment variable with a box drawn around it. Rejected: **showing everything always** (the form stops being readable) · **hiding it in a hover tooltip** (unreachable from the keyboard and on touch devices) · **building the text through `tr()`** (freezes the language of the moment of creation). |
| ADR-087 | 2026-07-27 | **Exit code 4 — "WE broke": a tool crash stops passing itself off as a finding in the application** — an unhandled exception in our own code returned `1`, and `1` in our own catalogue means "The test found a problem… this is a result, not a tool failure". That is, EVERY internal crash was presented to the user as a defect in THEIR application — exactly the substitution principle 8 exists to prevent, and the one this product can least afford: its value lies in answering the question "whose fault is this?". `4` is introduced (`💥 tool_failure`, "this is OUR failure, not a finding about your application — attach the output to a bug report") together with the event `fatal.internal_error`, which says so in words; a new code was chosen rather than the existing `3`, because `3` = "needs a human: the plan or the configuration does not add up" — also our side, but a different one: configuration there, a defect here. **Four catalogue entries were lying about their exit code as well**: `fatal.plan_missing`, `fatal.plan_missing_export`, `fatal.heal_report_missing` and `fatal.executor_cmd_unset` declared `exit: 3` while the code returned `2` — so a missing `plan.json` reached every reader of the exit code as "🖼 Golden/visual regression", and a human went off to look at the page instead of at their own configuration. The catalogue was right, the code was wrong. **The gate that did not exist:** the vocabulary check proved that an exit code EXISTS, not that the process returns it; `check_exit_promises_match_the_code` compares an entry's promise with the nearest `return` at the emission site. **And a third thing, without which the first two are dead:** the offline suite in CI ran off a HAND-WRITTEN list of 31 names rather than a glob — so a newly added gate ran only if somebody had not forgotten to write it in there as well, and a gate that does not run is indistinguishable from one that passes. Discovery is now by `tests/test_*_offline.py` + a floor of "fewer than 25 found is an error", because a glob that finds nothing must fail loudly rather than report success over an empty set. | Accepted | The product answers the question "did my application break, or the tool?" — and in the most important case it answered wrongly. Rejected: **reusing `3`** (mixes a tool defect with a configuration that does not add up — different actions for the reader) · **keeping `1` and explaining in prose** (the exit code is read by CI, which does not see prose) · **fixing the catalogue to match the code** in those four entries (the catalogue described the intent correctly: a missing plan is "needs a human", not "the page moved") · **leaving the test list in CI** (the same disease one level up: a registry that has to be remembered). |
| ADR-088 | 2026-07-27 | **A capability that cannot be found does not exist for the person it is meant for — a sweeping audit along five angles** — the question "where is LiteLLM, then?" exposed a whole class: code that is written, works and is unreachable. `usage` listed 4 subcommands out of 7, and the missing ones were exactly those most needed: `report` (the ONLY producer of `report.html`/`report.json`/`metrics.prom`/`junit.xml`), `export-spec` (migration to `@playwright/test`, which a competitor sells as a feature) and `calibrate`. A subcommand absent from usage exists for whoever reads the `switch` in `main.go` — and that is not the user. The gate `TestUsageListsEverySubcommand` DERIVES the truth from the dispatcher and fails on any divergence. **The registries stopped asserting things nobody had built:** an audit of six "declared mitigations" showed that ~12 of ~30 mechanisms do not exist — there are NO contract tests (zero grep hits, even though `TOOL_METHODS` declares 19 methods against 17 zod schemas, and nothing failed); heal-storm has no attempt limit, no step deadline and no auto-skip — precisely the three that would bound time and cost; `dom_subtree_hash` stores a hash of the WHOLE page, so the schema tells its reader the opposite of the truth about how brittle the heal cache is. Every line now says what was looked for and what was found. **~45 buried capabilities were found in total** (a full Helm chart, an OpenAI-compatible `/v1/chat/completions`, CDP-attach, takeover, MCP-server mode, installation without a checkout; of the 9 runtime presets, only two have a real service behind them) — filed under `[PROD-DISCOVERY]` GROUPED, one fix per group, because fixing them one at a time is exactly how they accumulated. | Accepted | We were asking the user to use things whose existence they could learn only from the source. Rejected: **surfacing everything indiscriminately** (some of it is buried deliberately — the recorder, the maintainer build, the expert toggles: they need a line in the right place, not a shop window) · **fixing them one at a time** (that is how they accumulated) · **fixing the registry to match the code** where the registry described the correct intent. |
| ADR-089 | 2026-07-27 | **A run started from the interface finally produces reports, and "finished" now means they are already there** — `agentctl report` was the ONLY producer of `report.html`, `report.json`, `metrics.prom` and `junit.xml`, and nobody called it for a run started from the UI: control-api spawned only `run` and `baseline update`. **Measured before the fix: 192 `runs/control-*` directories, of which those carrying a `metrics.prom` — ZERO.** The product's main path produced neither the human surface (the only place where identity verdicts, per-step outcomes and "before→after" pairs are visible) nor the machine one (`junit.xml`, for which ADR-073 was done) — even though all four are on the download allowlist and were returning 404. **A real race was closed:** the terminal state was published BEFORE generation, so a client that saw `state: done` and immediately requested `report.html` got a 404; a run that has declared itself finished must also have finished what it promises. **The report inherits the run's environment** rather than resolving its own: it is part of that run, and `PROM_PUSHGATEWAY` decides whether the metrics leave at all. **A failed report does not rewrite the outcome** (the verdict has already been issued and is correct), but it is not silent either — `test.report_failed`, and with it the event catalogue reaches the Go layer for the first time (`EMITTERS` now knows `control-api`): before that, EVERY line from the Go binaries arrived in the UI through the catch-all `system.unclassified` at level `info`, i.e. a warning and "the browser has started" carried the same severity. | Accepted | The product promised artifacts for CI and did not produce them on the main path, while `docs/OUTPUTS.md` claimed the opposite. Rejected: **generating them inside brain** (`report` has its own mode and its own input — the run directory) · **publishing the state before the report** (the client races the generation) · **resolving the environment afresh** (the report would see a different world than the run) · **treating a failed report as a failed run**. |
| ADR-090 | 2026-07-27 | **Our own CI templates did not consume our machine contract — and did not create it either** — `junit.xml` has existed since ADR-073 under the wording "a machine contract any CI consumes", yet both templates in `docs/ci-templates/` (the first thing a new user copies) archived `runs/` wholesale and published nothing: the results stayed invisible in the pipeline's interface. A contract nothing is connected to is a file. Worse: **there was nothing to publish either** — both templates drive the CLI, where `agentctl report` has to be called explicitly, while the ADR-089 wiring lives in control-api and covers only runs started from the UI. So even a correct publish step without generation would have remained decoration; this was found by our own gate, not by reading. Now: GitLab gets `reports: junit: runs/*/junit.xml`, Jenkins gets a `junit` step with `allowEmptyResults` (an explore run legitimately produces no `junit.xml`, and reddening over the absence of a file that should not be there is worse than not publishing), and both generate the report first, and do so in `always`/unconditionally: the report of a FAILED run is precisely the one people open. **There was no gate on the templates at all** — now there is `tests/test_ci_templates_offline.py`. ⚠ The first version of its `allowEmptyResults` check turned out to be vacuous: it searched for the word across the whole file, and my own comment explaining the option contains it — the check was satisfied by its own documentation. What must be asserted is the CALL LINE. | Accepted | We built a contract for CI and did not connect it in our own templates. Rejected: **publishing without generating** (there is nothing to publish) · **calling `report` only on success** (the failed one is exactly what is needed) · **going without `allowEmptyResults`** (reddens an explore run for the absence of a file it never has). |
| ADR-091 | 2026-07-27 | **A runtime preset says honestly whether a service stands behind it** — the wizard offers nine presets, while a real service in our `docker-compose` exists for exactly TWO (`ollama`, `litellm`); the other seven are placeholders whose address you have to replace yourself, and the only place this was said was the `_note` field inside a JSON nobody opens. That is, seven of the nine dropdown entries silently meant "there is more work ahead of you". This is the LiteLLM defect one level down: the capability exists, the way in does not. Every preset now has its own bilingual hint: the real ones carry the START COMMAND (`docker compose --profile litellm up -d litellm`) and the place where provider keys go (`deploy/litellm/config.yaml`); the placeholders carry an explicit "PLACEHOLDER"/"ЗАГОТОВКА" mark and what exactly to replace. A preset's hint is rendered as a separate paragraph rather than in ADR-086's collapsible block: this is not an optional detail about a field but the difference between a working and a non-working choice. **The hub did not mention the router at all** — it now names both real services and points to the wizard. **The gate ties the hints to `docker-compose.yml` rather than to memory:** a hint promising `--profile X` for a service that does not exist sends the user off to run a command that will not work — worse than silence; the converse is checked too (a real service must not call itself a placeholder). The compose file changes, and the hints will not follow it on their own. | Accepted | The dropdown offered nine options, seven of which silently required additional work. Rejected: **removing the presets with no service** (they are useful as placeholders — the address is your own anyway) · **leaving the explanation in `_note`** (only someone who has opened the JSON reads it) · **a collapsible block as on the fields** (this hint is not optional). |
| ADR-092 | 2026-07-27 | **Measure the blindness first, treat it afterwards: `browser.perceptionAudit` and the visible fraction in the plan** — the registry promised `completeness_ratio` as a MITIGATION of the a11y blind spots, while `grep -rni completeness` returned ZERO. The product reported coverage 1.00 for pages it sees half of, and nothing said the denominator was incomplete. **Coverage and visibility are different quantities, and only one was being measured:** coverage answers "how much of what was SEEN was exercised", visibility answers "how much was there". They multiply. The new RPC counts both sides in one pass: the numerator is what `browser.interactives` yields (7 selectors, the light DOM of the top frame), the denominator is deliberately WIDER (`[onclick]`, `[tabindex]`, `contenteditable`, clickable ARIA roles) plus a walk of the OPEN shadow roots and all frames via `page.frames()`. The denominator is generous on purpose: we are measuring how much we MISS, and a denominator that occasionally overstates is more honest than a narrow one that flatters. **The report is a BREAKDOWN:** "65% visible" is not actionable, "8 controls in a shadow root, 1 in a frame" tells you what to do; `opaque` is separate — canvas, CLOSED roots and unreachable cross-origin frames, boundaries that cannot be crossed, and the browser's refusal is itself a finding. **The plan gets `worst_ratio`, not the mean:** a mean hides one half-visible screen behind nine complete ones, and it is precisely that screen which makes the plan incomplete. The field sits BESIDE `steps`: otherwise every plan's `plan_hash` would break because of a measurement that describes the page rather than the test (verified — 106 plans are valid). **It is measured ONCE PER PAGE:** a live run against `l5.html` printed one finding five times, and a finding repeated until it becomes wallpaper is a finding nobody reads. An older executor yields `ratio: null` and a reason, but NEVER a flattering number. **Live:** `l5.html` 15 of 23 (0.652), with 8 controls in the shadow DOM out of sight; `l1.html` 19 of 19. ⚠ **THE NUMBERS ARE WRONG — CORRECTED BY ADR-093.** The numerator was counted by `document.querySelectorAll` inside `page.evaluate`, while the perception it described is `page.$$eval`, i.e. Playwright's engine, which does PIERCE open shadow roots. The measurement was comparing perception against a rewritten copy of itself. `l5.html` is in fact 23 of 23; the eight "invisible" controls did not exist — the product saw them and clicked them. The line is kept verbatim: the decision log is append-only, and the error "I measure with something other than what I do it with" is worth more than a tidy table. | Accepted | We promised coverage over a picture whose completeness we did not know. Rejected: **widening perception straight away** (without a number, "the blind spot is closed" is an assertion with no proof — exactly the error that put `completeness_ratio` into the registry as something non-existent) · **the mean** (hides the worst page) · **measuring inside a step** (breaks `plan_hash`) · **a narrow denominator** (flatters) · **staying silent when it is unavailable** (reads as full visibility). |
| ADR-093 | 2026-07-27 | **Measuring a capability must INVOKE that capability rather than reproduce it** — ADR-092 was reporting on a perception the product does not have. `browser.interactives` calls `page.$$eval`, that is, Playwright's selector engine, and it **pierces open shadow roots as a matter of course**; the audit, meanwhile, counted the numerator through `document.querySelectorAll` inside `page.evaluate`, which does not pierce. Two different machines over one page — and the divergence landed exactly where it matters. **Verified against the product itself rather than against a copy of its logic** (driving the built `dist/server.js` over stdio): on `l5.html` `interactives` returns **23** elements, including all 8 shadow-DOM controls, `probe{role:button,name:'Red'}` gives 1 and `click{name:'Apply color'}` gives `clicked:true`, while the audit in that same session prints `seen 15/23, ratio 0.652, unseen.shadow_dom 8`. The product saw and clicked precisely the controls it was reporting as invisible, and `ratio<1.0` raised `perception.partial` with `degrades:true` — **a false degradation reached the artifacts** (`runs/25e5ec3bf51b7616`, `runs/8e37398fae2546b7`). **The remedy is structural, not arithmetic:** the selector literal had been copied THREE times (`interactives`, `setOfMarks`, `PERCEIVED` in the audit) — copies ARE the divergence mechanism; there is now a single `PERCEPTION_SELECTOR`, and both sides of the fraction are counted with `locator()`, i.e. by the same engine. The manual shadow walk remains ONLY for the sake of `opaque` (counting roots), because the engine has already crossed the open ones. `browser.links` deliberately keeps its own `a[href]`: that is the navigation frontier, not an inventory of controls. **The breakdown keys were RENAMED, not redefined:** `light_no_role`+`shadow_dom` → a single `outside_selector`. A key whose meaning changed quietly lets every consumer go on reading it and go on being wrong; a key that has disappeared forces you to look at each of them. Where exactly a missed control lives — the light DOM or an open root — is not something the operator is asked: our selector fails to name it in both cases. **A second finding, out of a mutation:** `visibility:hidden` yields a FULL-SIZE bbox, while `setOfMarks` filtered by bbox alone — so the vision tier was handed a numbered mark over an empty spot. Both perception surfaces are brought to one definition of `visible` (Playwright's definition: a non-empty box and not `visibility:hidden`; `opacity:0` is deliberately NOT hidden — Playwright clicks it, and diverging from our own executor is not allowed). On `l5` the text tier saw 23 and the visual one 16, and neither of them knew it. **A third:** `display:none` in the check was a DEAD branch — for an element inside a hidden ancestor `getComputedStyle` returns its own `display:block`, since it does not look at ancestors; the box is the only thing that collapses. The branch was deleted, because the mutation that erased it survived. **`visible` travels to the brain in three states:** `None` = an older executor said nothing, and silence must not read as "invisible", or the candidate set empties and explore ends with zero coverage without a single error. The consumer checks `is False`. An element in a hidden panel is not offered (do not burn ADR-070's budget) but REMAINS in `interactive_seen` — the denominator describes the page, not our convenience. **The gate is the one that did not exist:** `tests/test_perception_engine_offline.py` brings up the REAL `dist/server.js` and asserts `audit.seen == len(interactives.elements)`, that is, a property rather than a constant; the old gate stubbed the executor and by construction could not see that the number was wrong. Three fixtures, because on a flat page the defect is invisible: with no shadow root the two machines agree. The new `l8-blindspots.html` is a page assembled out of what we do not see (`[onclick]`, `[tabindex]`, contenteditable, canvas, a CLOSED root, virtualization), so that the boundary is measured rather than declared. 17/17 mutations are caught; two survivors found defects in MY code, not in the test. | Accepted | A tool that lies about its own blindness is worse than a tool with a drawn boundary — and we were lying in BOTH directions: reporting as invisible the controls we were clicking, and staying silent about seven we were showing the planner without being able to click. Rejected: **redefining `shadow_dom` in place** (consumers go on reading it and go on being wrong) · **editing ADR-092's line** (the log is append-only; the class of the error is worth more than a tidy table) · **fixing only the numerator** (three copies of the literal would reproduce the divergence at the next edit) · **filtering the hidden ones in the executor** (perception would start lying about the page's composition, and a hidden panel opens tomorrow) · **widening the selector at the same time** (measured: zero gain across all 9 fixtures, the 4 pages of `testdata/site` and `docs/index.html` with its 206 elements — Alex's decision: the fixture first, the data afterwards). |
| ADR-094 | 2026-07-27 | **The field was called `role` and was not a role: one ARIA role for the whole system** — `browser.interactives` emitted `getAttribute('role') \|\| tagName`. For an ordinary `<a>` that held `"a"`, and the role `a` **does not exist** in ARIA, so any locator built from that field found nothing and could not find anything in principle. Downstream, two consumers repaired the conflation DIFFERENTLY, and each of them wrongly in its own way: `graph.py` re-derived the role from the tag, beginning its ladder with `if tag == "button"` — thereby INVERTING the ARIA rule under which an explicit `role` attribute overrides the tag's implicit role; `healing.py` did not repair it at all and took the field as-is. **One misnamed field — two independent defects in two modules.** **The damage was MEASURED, not estimated** (the real brain functions against the real executor across the whole fixture corpus, split by cause, because "a control off-screen is not in the accessibility tree" is correct behaviour and lumping it in would have overstated the figure): **48 non-resolving locators**, of which **42 came from the conflation** and all of them on the SELF-HEALING path (every link in the corpus), 6 being tabs on the plan path. Separately, 36 expected ones (20 hidden, 16 ambiguous with `count: 2` — the ADR-082 hole, not taken into scope). Afterwards — **0**. **A symptom that had lived for months:** ALL seven explore runs against `l5.html` in `runs/` had `coverage 0.0, exercised 0`, including the two on which ADR-092 was written. The `[role=tab]` support introduced by M9.4 had NEVER once worked: `getByRole('tab',{name:'Overview'})` = 1, `getByRole('button',{name:'Overview'})` = 0. Live after the fix — 0.00 → **0.53**, nine clicks, verdict `ok`. **The remedy is at the root, not in the ladder:** the executor emits the REAL ARIA role (the explicit attribute wins; otherwise the implicit one from the tag, and for `<input>` from `type`), the brain READS it, the ladder in `graph.py` is deleted entirely, and `healing.py` is fixed for free — it was reading that field already. A better ladder would be a second place where the role is decided; the defect is that there are two places. **The map of implicit roles was MEASURED with Playwright's engine rather than copied out of the specification,** and the two diverge: the specification leaves `color`/`date`/`time` without a role while the engine gives `textbox`; `file`/`image` → `button`; `select[multiple]` → `listbox`, not `combobox`. The engine is the authority, because it is what `getByRole` asks; a map taken from the specification would have been correct and useless. **The candidate set was widened to `{button, tab}` precisely so THE SET WOULD NOT CHANGE:** a mis-typed tab had been a candidate before (an unreachable one), and narrowing to `{button}` would quietly have thrown four controls per page out of the denominator — coverage would have risen because the page shrank. Measured: `interactive_seen` = 15 before and 15 after, `exercised` 0 → 9. We did NOT widen it further: `checkbox`/`radio` are clickable, but explore never offered them, and adding them would have smuggled a behaviour change in under the guise of a fix. **A third place where the role was INVENTED:** the candidate hardcoded `"role": "button"` and printed "click button 'Overview'" for a tab — the locator beside it was already correct, which is why the defect did not catch the eye: it looked like a click that had timed out rather than a lying caption. **The gate asserts a PROPERTY:** every declared role must resolve with Playwright's engine, across the whole corpus, with a floor on the sample size. A table of expected roles would have been written by the same reasoning that produced the defect, and would have agreed with a wrong implementation that shares its assumptions. The new fixture `l9-roles.html` — one control per role. 11/11 mutations; two survivors found defects in MY tests: the check "the role-less one did not reach the model" looked at falsiness (substituting the tag makes the element truthy, and the assertion passed), while the negative control itself was being discarded by a DIFFERENT check — the hidden input had no anchor, so it never reached the role code at all. | Accepted | The product saw controls and could not click them, and self-healing — the central promise — was entirely non-working for links. Rejected: **fixing only the ladder** (closes 6 of 48 and leaves the main cluster) · **fixing it separately in `healing.py`** (a third place where the role is decided) · **a map from the specification** (diverges from the engine on 5 types) · **widening the candidates to every clickable role** (a behaviour change under the guise of a fix) · **normalizing `listbox`→`combobox` the way the ladder did** (a role is a role; conflating them is the same loss of information as conflating a tag with a role). |
| ADR-095 | 2026-07-27 | **A frame is the AXIS "where to look", not a seventh strategy** — the frame boundary was the only blind spot of `GAP-RISK-005` that was both real and closable: `page.$$eval`, `page.locator` and `getByRole` do not cross it (a property of the selector engine, not an oversight), while `frameLocator` does, cross-origin included. **The key measurement that shaped the construction:** `FrameLocator` carries ALL SIX tiers of `buildLocator` (`getByTestId`/`getByRole`/`getByLabel`/`getByText`/`locator(css)`/`locator('xpath=')`), and `click`/`fill` work through it. So `frame` answers the question WHERE, orthogonal to the question HOW — and `strategies.py`, the `PRIORS` table, `pick_confidence` and the vocabulary gate on locator keys are not touched at all. A seventh strategy would have demanded a prior there is nothing to derive from: the six existing ones are already acknowledged as unmeasured (`GAP-RISK-002`), and a seventh uncalibrated number beside six is not frame support, it is one more invention. **`buildLocator` grew by exactly one line:** `root = frame ? page.frameLocator(frame) : page`, then the same six-way dispatch. The same in `exporter.py`. **Frame addressing goes by descending STABILITY, not convenience:** `iframe[name=…]` (the author chose the name, it survives a re-layout) → `iframe#id` (usually survives) → `iframe >> nth=N` (survives nothing, and a plan carrying such a selector SAYS OUT LOUD that it depends on document order). **The key is present ONLY on a control inside a frame** — not `null`, but absent: `canonical_plan_hash` hashes every field of every step, and a present-but-empty key would have shifted all 106 stored hashes for the sake of a fact about the PAGE rather than about the TEST (verified by literal equality of descriptors on a frameless page). **The frame enters the control's IDENTITY, not just its address:** two payment frames each with a "Pay" button are two controls, and without this they would collapse into one `semantic_id`, coverage would count one where there are two, and the second would read as already exercised. It is appended to the anchor, so a top-frame control hashes exactly as before. **Depth is capped at 1 DELIBERATELY:** `frameLocator` can chain deeper, but the chain would have to be carried in the step, compared when checking identity and emitted by the exporter — paying everywhere for a shape that is rare. A nested frame is counted in `opaque.frames_nested` while its control stays in `unseen.iframe`: "we decided not to" and "we could not" are different sentences for the operator. **The audit's numerator now walks THE SAME roots**, otherwise it would under-count at exactly the moment perception grew — the ADR-093 defect in reverse. **⚠ A stated boundary:** `browser.setOfMarks` stays top-frame only, because a mark is a BOX, and a box inside a frame lives in that frame's coordinate system — the number would land somewhere other than the control. The consequence is named out loud: a heal that reached the visual tier cannot re-ground a control inside a frame. **Live:** explore over `l10-frames.html` — verdict `ok`, coverage 0.60, steps carry `{"role":"button","name":"Pay now","frame":"iframe[name=\"payment\"]"}`; replay of the same plan — 4 of 4, `healed=0 failed=0`. 14/14 mutations; three survivors found holes in MY OWN checks: the "name beats id" ordering was untestable (no fixture frame had both), the locators `graph.py` builds were looked at by NO test at all (that is the planning path the whole thing was built for), and the audit mutation did not compile and therefore was not a mutation. | Accepted | Importing third-party suites (`PROD-IMPORT`) will hit frames immediately: payment forms, OAuth, embedded widgets. Rejected: **a seventh strategy** (a prior there is nothing to derive from) · **a selector chain for arbitrary depth** (paid everywhere, met rarely) · **addressing by the frame's URL** (query parameters change from run to run) · **silently skipping nested frames** (a boundary that is not stated reads as its absence) · **marking frames in `setOfMarks` offset by the owner's box** (inner scrolling makes the offset wrong; a smaller reach declared out loud is more honest than a larger one that sometimes lies). |
| ADR-096 | 2026-07-28 | **A control we can see is a control we can NAME** — `name` was computed as `aria-label \|\| textContent`, and an `<input>` has no text content: its accessible name comes from the associated `<label>`, which we never read. **Eleven labelled form fields across the corpus arrived with an empty name, failed brain's "no anchor" check and were SILENTLY dropped from the page model.** On `l3.html` — a fixture that exists precisely to validate a form — 5 controls of 9 reached the model while the audit printed `ratio 1.00`: we were not blind to them, we discarded them AFTER seeing them, and every number downstream described a smaller page. Found while preparing the UI part: you cannot display "we see and act on: N" if N silently excludes addressable controls. **`<select>` was worse than empty:** a select's `textContent` is the concatenation of its OPTIONS, so the theme picker on `l5.html` was called "System default Light Dark" while the accessibility tree calls it "Theme:". A wrong name is not a reduced version of a missing one: it yields a locator that looks precise and finds nothing. **The fix is to read where the browser reads:** `aria-label` → `aria-labelledby` → `element.labels` → `placeholder` → `value` (on submit/reset/button) → text (except `<select>`) → `title`. `labels` was taken deliberately instead of `querySelector('label[for=…]')`: it covers BOTH spellings, and the wrapping form is what the `l5` checkboxes use. **The control's own subtree is cut out of the label:** a `<select>` inside its own `<label>` contributes its options to that label's `textContent`, and a naive read gives "Theme: System defaultLight" against the accessibility tree's "Theme:" — and the naive name resolves to ZERO, because `getByRole`'s name matching looks for the given string INSIDE the real one, and a superset is not a substring. **The authority is the engine, as in ADR-094:** this is a CLAIM, and the gate proves it by requiring the engine to resolve every reported name. Measured: 102 of 102 visible controls. **⚠ The ADR-094 gate did not catch this because it SKIPPED elements with an empty name** — that is, exactly the broken ones. A check that steps over its own subject is not a weaker check, it is a check about something else. **The measured result:** model losses 12 → 1 (the remaining one is a deliberate `input[type=hidden]` with no role); `l3` 5 → 9; every recovered field resolves. 7/7 mutations; **two survivors pointed at holes in the CORPUS, not in the code** — both fallback branches (placeholder, and "a select is not named by its options") were unreachable because every control in the corpus has a label; both were then MEASURED against `ariaSnapshot` (a placeholder does name; an unlabelled select has NO name at all) and pinned down in `l9` §3b. | Accepted | The tool discarded controls it is perfectly able to address, and did so silently — while the UI part this ADR unblocks is obliged to show honest numbers, or it will repeat the very error it was undertaken to fix. Rejected: **the full W3C accessible-name algorithm** (large, and it would have had to be verified by the engine anyway — simpler to declare a subset and prove it) · **asking `ariaSnapshot` per element** (authoritative, but that is N round-trips per page; on `docs/index.html` there are 206) · **letting the UI show the discarded ones as "we cannot address these"** (that would be a LIE: we can, we just never read them) · **merging `name` and `text` into one** (`text_role` matches the page's text, not the accessibility tree; merging would make the fallback strategy a copy of the primary one). |
| ADR-097 | 2026-07-28 | **A measurement nobody read — and three categories instead of one number** — ADR-092 measured a page's visibility, ADR-093 made the measurement truthful, and neither of them made it VISIBLE: `plan.json` carried `perception.worst_ratio`, and its only reader in the entire repository was a test. The Go decoder `planCoverage` dropped the block while parsing, `report.html` read an entirely different artefact, and the hub printed `coverage NN%` beside it without a hint that the denominator might be a fraction of the page. The product reported a ratio without saying what it was a ratio OF. **Three categories chosen from what is TRUE rather than from what sounds complete:** "we see it and can act on it" (in the model, on screen, not disabled) · "we see it but cannot act" (perceived and reported, but off-screen, disabled, or with nothing to address it by) · "we do not see it at all" (outside the selector or beyond a frame boundary). **The categories SUM to the audit's denominator, and that is asserted on all 13 fixtures:** a breakdown that does not add up is decoration, and without such a check one category can silently absorb another. `opaque` is kept SEPARATE because those zones cannot be counted at all — a canvas may carry one button or ten — and a guess inside the ratio would be a flattering number in a pessimist's coat. **There is NO "vision-only" category, although the work began with that wording:** `browser.setOfMarks` is bounded by THE SAME selector, i.e. vision sees no more than the text tier, and before ADR-093 it saw seven controls FEWER. A category empty by construction would be an interface making exactly the claim we have spent the whole day repairing registries to prevent. **The split between "we see" and "we can act" is made by the BRAIN, not the executor:** the executor reports facts (`visible`, `disabled`), and what they mean for planning is not its question. Both quantities are taken from one moment on one page: computing them apart is exactly how the audit and perception came to describe different pages. **"Not measured" renders DIFFERENTLY from "everything is visible" on every surface** — ADR-092's own rule, repeated where the number is now shown; a `null` that reads as 100% would be a NEW instance of the defect, produced by the code that closes it. For the same reason `WorstRatio` in Go is a POINTER: `nil` means "not measured", which is not the same as "measured zero". The `visibility` metric is emitted only when a measurement was taken — a series collecting a zero from every replay would say the tool had gone blind, whereas replay simply does not ask. **`report.py` now reads `plan.json`:** the audit travels the explore path while the heal report is written on the replay path, so a report assembled from one cannot mention the other. A missing or corrupt plan is not an error (an imported plan replayed on another machine has no audit), but a corrupt one is SAID OUT LOUD: a silently omitted section is indistinguishable from a run with nothing to report. **A minimum threshold was introduced to BOTH DOM gates:** the Python suite had one, these two did not, and a deleted check silently lowered the denominator while leaving the step green; a gate that cannot fail is indistinguishable from one that passes. **An explicit TEST SEAM** `window.__gate` for the pure function `perceptionBlock`, named as a seam: the alternative was a third stubbed run with a control-api restart for the sake of one `<details>` — machinery disproportionate to a formatter, and one that checks the wiring instead of the wording. 11/11 mutations. | Accepted | A number nobody sees does not exist for the user; and "coverage 1.00" beside a half-seen page is exactly the reassuring figure this whole line of work was undertaken to remove. Rejected: **multiplying visibility by coverage** (a third number that can no longer be decomposed back) · **folding `opaque` into the denominator** (it cannot be counted, and a guess is worse than an absence) · **showing "vision-only"** (the category is empty by construction) · **computing the split in the executor** (what `visible` means for planning is the brain's question) · **a section of zeros for replay** (it asserts the tool saw nothing, when it never asked). |
| ADR-098 | 2026-07-28 | **A saved trace is a redacted trace, or there is no trace at all** — ADR-084 narrowed the WINDOW (`trace.zip` survives only a non-green run); this ADR closes the CONTENTS. **The item's design turned out to be half wrong, and that came out of measuring a live archive rather than reading the format.** The item said "apply the ADR-081 scanner"; measured: `Secretish("value")` = **false** — and rightly so, a search box has a `value` too — so the scanner reaches everything except the main thing: the text the tool TYPED IN itself. This is not a naming problem but a structural one, and it is solved structurally. **Three structural rules, the second and third of which were found by RUNNING the first and examining what survived:** (1) the arguments of input verbs (`fill`/`type`/`press`/`insertText`/`setInputFiles`) are redacted UNCONDITIONALLY — Alex's decision; the alternative, "redact if the neighbouring selector is credential-shaped", is GUESSWORK over someone else's markup, and a field labelled "PIN", or not labelled at all, would have passed silently · (2) `{"type":"log","message":"  fill(\"…\")"}` — the driver retells the call itself; matched by `callId`, NEVER by text · (3) `__playwright_value_` in the DOM snapshot — Playwright writes the field's live state there because the DOM does not serialise `value` as an attribute; this is its own name (one of ~30 `__playwright_*` in this version, and the only one carrying free text), not a guess. **The text pass was made STRUCTURE-AWARE, and that too was found by a check rather than by reasoning:** a raw string scanner turned `"cookies":[]` into `"cookies":[REDACTED]]`, and two lines of a real archive stopped parsing. A trace that will not open is WORSE than a trace with a secret in it — the latter at least reports something. A JSON line is now walked as a document (the key name consults the vocabulary, string leaves get the Bearer/JWT regexes), while the raw scanner remains for `resources/*.html` and `trace.stacks`. **One implementation:** `redactSecrets` moved out of `cmd/control-api` into `internal/redact` — a second consumer appeared, and a copy would have been exactly the divergence a package boundary is chosen to prevent (the same device as ADR-093 for the selector and ADR-094 for roles). **Pixels are not redacted at all** (Alex's decision): redacting them means OCR plus masking — unreliable and expensive — and a redactor that half-works on screenshots is worse than one that honestly declined. The lever is `SENTINEL_TRACE_SCREENSHOTS=0`, with a single insertion point at `tracing.start`. The gate asserts this as a DECISION: the image must come out byte-for-byte the same. **Fail CLOSED:** a trace that could not be redacted is DELETED. That is not a degraded artefact, it is a leak, and keeping it because the cleanup failed would turn the cleanup inside out. All three unsuccessful outcomes carry `degrades: true` and reach the verdict; the successful one is SILENT (a warning on every run stops being read — the same reason ADR-092 speaks once per page). The emergency `SENTINEL_TRACE_RAW=1` announces itself EVERY time. **The insertion point is where the trace is SAVED** (`_stop_trace`), not in report generation: the report is built later, and a replay launched directly never reaches it at all — a redaction that depends on how you launched is the worst possible property for a protective mechanism. **⚠ THE WINDOW REMAINS AND IS NAMED:** Playwright writes the archive itself and gives no hook between "the bytes have landed" and "we can read them", so the raw file lives for the duration of one subprocess. ADR-084 COULD close its own window (discarding the buffer is a supported option); this one cannot, and the honest half of the answer is to say so. **Live:** the tool is present → the trace is saved and there is no secret in the text; the tool is absent → the trace is deleted and the event reaches the verdict. 11/11 mutations; two survivors were holes in the FIXTURE (there was no NON-input call carrying a `text` field, and the fake image contained nothing the text pass would have changed, so "byte-for-byte equal" passed even when the pixels were run through the scanner). | Accepted | The trace is the only artefact that receives someone else's input in full, and until now it was written as-is. Rejected: **redacting in TypeScript** (a second implementation of the notion "secret") · **redacting pixels** (OCR+masking: unreliable, expensive, and a half-redacted image is more dangerous than a refusal) · **folding the redaction into `agentctl report`** (a whole run too late, and past a direct replay) · **failing open — keeping an unredacted trace when redaction fails** (preserves parseability at the price of a leak; the opposite was chosen) · **a raw string scanner over JSON** (measured: it breaks the archive). |
| ADR-099 | 2026-07-28 | **The run directory acquired a lifetime — and the real leak was not where it was being looked for** — the item proposed introducing a NEW axis, `artifact_retention`. Measurement found two facts that changed the shape of the work. **The first: the `retention` family ALREADY EXISTS** — five settings in the schema plus `sweepTraces`/`sweepLogs` with their own tests; a second policy over the same files would have been exactly the divergence this code spends its day removing, so the run directory JOINED the family (`run_keep`/`run_ttl_hours`, the same group, the same template) instead of getting a vocabulary of its own. **The second, and it overturns the item: `runs/` held 606 MB, of which 570 MB was `checkpoint.db`** (284 files, up to 9 MB), which NO sweeper touched: `sweepTraces` owns traces, `sweepLogs` owns logs, and nobody owned the directory. Traces weighed **0 MB** (ADR-084 already discards them on green), logs 4 MB. That is, the item was about traces and media while 94% of the disk was taken by something nobody had thought about. **The checkpoint is deleted in `finally`**, not on the happy path: a crashed run is exactly the case where nobody will come back for the files, and at the same time the one where they are largest. Deleting is safe BY CONSTRUCTION rather than by decision: the thread is keyed to `run_id`, unique per run, and that is precisely why a multi-turn chat keeps a SEPARATE shared store (`_conversations_store_path`) — both halves of the claim are pinned by a test so they cannot drift apart. The `-wal`/`-shm` pair is deleted too: removing only the `.db` would leave half of it behind. **Directory sweeping is OFF by default** — like logs and unlike traces, for the same reason: the directory holds the plan, the reports and the executed plan, that is, the evidence a person comes back for; deleting it on an upgrade nobody asked for is worse than an unbounded disk. A bulky by-product may be capped by default; a record of what was done may not. **The newest run is NEVER swept**, even when both knobs say delete: a person who has just launched one and found nothing learns that the tool eats its own output, and correct arithmetic does not repair that. **`trace.zip` is opened for download** (Alex's decision): until now it was the only artefact unreachable without access to the server, i.e. debugging a failed run was unavailable to exactly the person the product is for. It is opened KNOWING what is inside: ADR-098 redacts TEXT and deliberately does NOT redact SCREENSHOTS, and the warning about that sits next to the whitelist entry — a reader who does not know this will conclude the redaction covered everything. It is served as `application/zip` and always as an attachment. **Artefacts are not kept in the database** — this was true and unasserted, which is a way of ceasing to be true; it is checked against the schema (there are no `bytes` fields), because the schema is what a future feature would have to change first. ⚠ **Found but NOT done:** the hub itself reads `plan.json`/`scenario.json`/`heal-report.json` every time a run is opened, so an `until_downloaded` mode built as "delete on GET" would destroy the run ON VIEWING — an explicit marker for "a human is taking it away", distinct from "the interface is rendering", is needed. 8/8 mutations; one survivor was the session's sixth instance of "assert the cell, not the document": the check looked for the substring `"trace.zip": true`, and a commented-out line contains it — replaced with a BEHAVIOURAL Go test that asks the server for the file. | Accepted | The product silently consumed disk and never said so; and debugging a failure required access to the machine. Rejected: **a new `artifact_retention` axis** (a second policy over the same files) · **sweeping directories by default** (deletes someone else's evidence on an upgrade) · **sweeping the newest run too** (a tool that has eaten what was just created loses trust before it gets to explain) · **`until_downloaded` as "delete on GET"** (the hub reads artefacts to render them — the mode would destroy a run on viewing). |
| ADR-100 | 2026-07-28 | **An inventory of someone else's text in the database, and a purge that is INVOKED rather than one that just happens** — ADR-098 redacts the trace, ADR-099 gives the run directory a lifetime, and both redact ON WRITE; over what was written BEFORE them, write-side redaction is powerless, and in SQLite the text of someone else's site had been accumulating the whole time there was no policy. **The item's register of suspects diverged from the measurement in both directions, and this is the third such case in the session.** It named `healing_audit.original`/`healed` and did not name **`healed_locators.value`** — the same page text, but in the LIVE CACHE, which every run reads and which never expires, whereas the audit is at least an append-only history. It called `chats.summary` a summary from the model — it is a deterministic 80-character string (`graph.py:213`), and today it is UNREACHABLE altogether (`ChatProjector` requires `STORE_ADDR`, which `agentctl` does not set for `--mode chat`). It called `runs.error` "the classic accidental leak" — it is the CLEANEST column in the area. There turned out to be three database files rather than one, and `brain/store.py::_SCHEMA` does not declare the M13 domains at all. **The load-bearing distinction is INHERENT text versus ACCIDENTAL, and it decides feasibility rather than wording:** `healed_locators.value` = `{"role":"button","name":"Confirm payment"}`, that is, the caption on the button IS what the button is found by; scrubbing it leaves "any button" and breaks self-healing. Almost all foreign text here is inherent (there are no accidental leaks in the heal domain at all — searched for separately), so a purge means DELETING ROWS ON AN EXPLICIT COMMAND rather than overwriting fields, and for inherent text the only things available are a lifetime, mode `0600` and an honest record that "the database contains the text of someone else's site by design". **The one NON-inherent leak is named but not closed here** (Alex's decision — do not mix an inventory with a product change): `scenarios.steps_json[].value` stores the entered password in clear text, and the sharpest way to see it is this — `collect-live-run.sh:255` ALREADY scrubs it when a scenario leaves in a support bundle, while the database does not; one policy, two outcomes, because the policy lives in a bash script rather than on the write path. The root is not in the database: the LLM step schema (`planner.py:66-79`) has `value` and has no `secretRef`, which the writer's path carries through perfectly well → `[SEC-SCENARIO-SECRETREF]`. **`agentctl purge-store` is invoked only explicitly** — it is in no sweep, and the bar here is higher than for file retention (automatic, but switched off): a swept trace is reproducible by running again, a self-healing history is not, and an automatic purge would make "the tool tidied up" and "the evidence was erased" indistinguishable. **Two policies, both legitimate, neither of them a default** (Alex's decision): without `--vacuum` the rows leave the queries while the BYTES REMAIN; with `--vacuum` the bytes disappear TOGETHER WITH recoverability, which is contraindicated for some organisations — the deployer chooses. That the bytes remain was MEASURED on the product's own driver (`modernc.org/sqlite` v1.53.0, `secure_delete=0`), not assumed; measuring with the `sqlite3` CLI would have meant measuring a copy. ⚠ `VACUUM` BY ITSELF DOES NOT CLEAN `-wal` — the command checkpoints before and after, or "the bytes are gone" would be a lie; a failed `VACUUM` (it requires free space the size of the database) does not fail the call but is reported as the partial truth "deleted but not overwritten". The report is counters, never contents (the `redact-trace` rule), and it always names the CAPABILITY BEING GIVEN UP: purging `healing_audit` blinds `agentctl calibrate`, which reads exactly that. The inventory gate is BEHAVIOURAL in both languages — it opens a real `store.New`/`LocalStore` and queries the resulting database rather than asserting anything about the text of the source (a surrogate that mutations pass straight through). | Accepted | Write-side redaction does not help what was written before it, and an inventory built on an unverified register would have sent the purge to the wrong place. Rejected: **redacting locators** (would break self-healing while protecting nothing) · **an automatic purge** (destroys the distinction between "we tidied up" and "the evidence was erased") · **`VACUUM` by default** (destroying recoverability is not our decision) · **purging `config`** (live configuration, not history) · **closing `steps_json[].value` right here** (a product change in the authoring schema, a separate ADR). ⚠ **Stated as a question, not introduced as a requirement:** is a STRICT NON-ERASURE mode needed — a ban on any deletion for the sake of preserving evidence. It is not measured and there is no backlog item, so introducing it would mean inventing a requirement; but staying silent is not possible either, since `--vacuum` already gives the deployer a lever in the opposite direction. |
| ADR-101 | 2026-07-28 | **Field help reached the page that actually gets published** — ADR-086 built a collapsible explanation and bounded itself in its own words: "17 hand-written fields of the WIZARD". From the inside that reads as a finished feature; from the outside it does not, and Alex's question laid it bare: **`docs/index.html` — what GitHub Pages serves at the root — had `fhelp`=0**, so a reader of the published interface saw not a single hint and concluded the mechanism did not exist at all. Meanwhile the run panel's labels are literally environment variable names (`coverage_target`, `max_steps`, `plan_budget`, `storage_state`), that is, exactly the shape ADR-086 was undertaken for ("a setting with no explanation is an environment variable with a border round it"). **The mechanism was COPIED, not reinvented:** the same `<details class="fhelp">`, the same "?" marker, the same single "show all" toggle with its state remembered — two implementations of one device drift apart. One deliberate improvement over the original: the marker's accessible name is two `data-lang` spans and NOT an `aria-label` assembled through `tr()`, because `tr()` freezes the language of the moment of creation — the trap ADR-086 itself names for the hint's text and itself walks into for the hint's label. **The text speaks about the CONSEQUENCE and is taken from the code, not from the field's name**, and three of its statements CONTRADICT what the name suggests, which is precisely why they earn their place: the budget does NOT fail the run (`brain/budget.py:3-11`) — the planner silently falls back to the heuristic and self-healing to the deterministic strategies, which without an explanation reads as "it got worse for no reason" · a missing or corrupt `storage_state` does NOT fail the run (`pw-executor/src/server.ts:384,387`) — it quietly proceeds UNAUTHENTICATED · `max_steps` is a loop guard, and reaching it ends the run INCOMPLETE (`brain/graph.py:349-350`). The gate asserts these three facts by name rather than "a hint exists": a hint that restates the label is the same silence, only wordier. The CI threshold was raised 26→35 so that the loss of a check cannot pass quietly. 6/6 mutations (not collapsed · one language · the toggle opens only the first · state not remembered · restating the label · `div` instead of `details`). ⚠ **The calculators are deliberately untouched** — they have their own prose from ADR-033/034, and a second hint beside the first is noise. | Accepted | A mechanism that reached half the interface reads as absent to whoever opened the other half. Rejected: **writing the hints again for the hub** (two implementations of one device drift apart) · **`aria-label` through `tr()`** (freezes the language, ADR-086's mistake) · **covering the calculators as well** (they already have prose, a duplicate = noise) · **a gate for "a hint exists"** (passes on a restatement of the label). |
| ADR-102 | 2026-07-28 | **The LLM could author a login only by writing the password in — `secretRef` added to the authoring schema** (SEC-SCENARIO-SECRETREF, the one NON-inherent leak from the ADR-100 inventory). `scenarios.steps_json[].value` stored the entered value — a password, a card number — in clear text; the writer's path already carried `secretRef` (the name of an environment variable instead of the value), while the LLM schema had `value` and had no `secretRef`, so a model authoring a login could only enter the password literally — and `collect-live-run.sh:255` was already scrubbing that literal on the way into a support bundle while the database kept it. Added: `secretRef` to both LLM schemas (`_SCHEMA_STEPS`, `_SCHEMA_DRAFT`) + an instruction in the prompts. **It stays fill-only, because that is the product's existing end-to-end contract:** the executor resolves `secretRef` only for `browser.fill` (`pw-executor/src/server.ts`), the recorder routes it only for fill (`record_bridge.py`), and `_verb_step` honours it only for fill. So a `secretRef` on any other verb is REJECTED into `unmatched` rather than carried through and silently lost by `_verb_step`: a silently dropped `secretRef` would read as "the secret is protected" while the field stayed empty — the same failure, "a silent drop passing itself off as protection", that this entire arc exists to avoid.
