The precedence is safe and unchanged: a `secretRef` and a literal on the same fill step keep the reference and discard the literal, not the other way round. The gate is BEHAVIOURAL in Python — through real schema objects (`jsonschema`) and the real `ground_scenario`/`reconcile`, not an assertion about the text of the source. 5/5 mutations (the very first was a dud — the regex did not fire — and that is PROVEN by `changed:False` rather than taken on trust). ⚠ **The honest remainder:** the schema gives a well-instructed model a safe path but does not compel it — a goal whose text literally contains the password can still lead to a literal; only an operator who refers to the secret through an environment variable in the goal itself can close that, and the schema does not guarantee it. | Accepted | The leak was not in the database but in the fact that a safe authoring path existed for the recorder and was absent for the LLM. Rejected: **extending `secretRef` to `type`/`select`** (a cross-language change to executor+zod, wider than the task; fill-only is the existing contract) · **silently ignoring `secretRef` on non-fill** (a drop passing itself off as protection) · **asserting about the shape of the source** (mutations pass straight through). |

A distinguishing primitive `?download=1` was introduced: the hub sets it ONLY on a genuine download action, never while rendering. | ADR-106 | 2026-07-28 | **Test versioning: history, diff, rollback — what `plan_hash` never gave** (PROD-VERSIONING, core + live wiring). `plan_hash` answered "the same plan?", but never "how does it differ from the previous one", kept no history, and re-authoring erased the previous test without a trace — a load-bearing gap for a product that promises to MAINTAIN tests. `brain/revisions.py` is the authoritative FILE-BASED append-only revision store: authoritative because correctness must not depend on a network service (an air-gapped installation may have no git remote — that is a declared differentiator; git = an optional adapter on top, not the store). Append-only BY CONSTRUCTION: a revision body is named by its own `plan_hash` (the same plan is idempotent, a different one is a new file), the history is a growing JSONL log. **A rollback does NOT delete** — it RE-ADDS the previous revision as a new head, so "put it back the way it was" is itself recorded and the intermediate history stays intact. The step diff names added/removed steps and CHANGED FIELDS, so "how does it differ" is answered step by step instead of by a hash mismatch. Wired in live: freezing a scenario with `SENTINEL_TEST_ID` writes a revision (a named test), without it nothing is written (an ad-hoc run is not versioned, so run-scoped ids do not litter the history). `scenario_id` is reduced to its base name (no traversal out of the store). 4 mutations. | Accepted | A product that promises to maintain tests must be able to answer "why did it change" and "put it back the way it was". ⚠ **NOT done (follow-up):** writing a revision on the promote path with a STABLE `scenario_id` from the store (today it keys off `SENTINEL_TEST_ID` at authoring time) · golden history per revision · a UI/CLI surface for diff/rollback. Rejected: **git as the version store** (binary goldens make noise on every baseline; an air gap loses the git remote — tying correctness to the network = losing the differentiator) · **keying by target_url at freeze** (conflates different tests against the same target). |

The claim is left as written; the correction is ADR-141]** (`none`/`app`/`tool`/`test`/`config`) over a closed vocabulary. The ownership rule is DERIVED, not enumerated: a record that declared an `exit` ends the run by definition, so it is the one obliged to name the fault — a hand-written list would have missed the next such code. The decision is taken in `cmd/control-api/fault.go`, and it is an ORDER of questions rather than a table: never started → ours · stopped by a human → nobody's · a terminal code from the catalog → its answer · only then the exit code; an unknown exit code → empty rather than "tool", because an unexplained exit is itself a finding. **A green ending has no fault** — that rule stands BEFORE the terminal code, otherwise a degradation the run survived (the fallback to the heuristic planner, the most common one) would hang an accusation on a passing run; what was lost along the way is answered by `degrades` and the `pass_with_*` states, while this axis answers who broke a NON-GREEN ending. **A live run found a hole in the first version:** a goal run whose endpoint answered 404 emitted `plan.scenario_error_empty` (`degrades`, `src=tool`, with the 404 in the text), grounded zero steps and exited with 1 — and the verdict charged it to `app`, because exit 1 on its own means "the test found a problem in the application". The product KNEW whose problem it was, and the badge said the opposite. So the fault is also named by the five codes that end a run in every sense except announcing the number (`plan.*_empty`, `plan.output_unparseable`). The field travels through proto → the stubs of both languages → `results.fault_domain` → `/v1/runs`, the SSE frame and AG-UI → the run's badge and the results list. 13 mutations, 13 killed. Verified live against ollama `.170`: with no model — exit 3 and `tool` with the reason named; with `qwen3:8b` — exit 0, `none`, 6 grounded steps out of 30. | Accepted | Alex's decision of 2026-08-03: a SEPARATE field, not new words. `problem` stays the OUTCOME, the domain says whose fault it is, the hub composes the phrase out of the pair. Rejected: **new verdict words** (combinatorial growth: `problem`×4 domains, then `regression`×4) · **both the words and the field** (two sources of truth about one fact — exactly the `lvKindOf`-versus-catalog wound) · **a new exit code for the refusal** (contradicts the already accepted `exit 3` and would break readers of the exit code) · **backfilling `none` into old rows** ("we never decided" is not the same as "there is no fault"). |
| ADR-114 | 2026-08-04 | **A failed step says WHY, and the distinction is made by the CODE, not by a field** (HEALTH-004, PR-1b). `brain/replay.py` builds a rich record for every step — the exception text, the assertion condition with expected and observed, the outcome of the heal — while what went into the stream was `step` and `type`. Which means that in the very stream a person searches, "the application returned the wrong thing" and "the executor died and we never reached the page" were ONE line; the detail sat in `heal-report.json`, which replay/baseline have and goal/explore do NOT — that is, for the most common runs it existed nowhere. **The distinction is made at the level of the CODE:** `test.step_failed` (category `test` → source `testing` → audience `business`) against the new `test.step_unresolved` (category `browser` → `tool`). A field cannot do it — the audience is derived from the category, and a field would not move the record between filters. **The boundary is drawn by the TRANSPORT, not by a word in the message:** `ExecutorTransportError` was introduced, raised where the channel genuinely failed (the subprocess closed · the reply is unreadable · our own RPC deadline expired · the MCP session never came up); the executor's error reply stays an ordinary `RuntimeError` — it is alive, it reached the page and it is reporting about THAT. `_fault_of` asks the TYPE: matching the driver's strings ("Timeout", "ECONNRESET") is a surrogate for the question "did the transport fail", it agrees today and breaks silently at Playwright's first rewording; a gate pins this with two timeouts whose English is nearly identical and whose owners are opposite. The domain is written AT THE POINT of failure (7 branches) rather than reconstructed afterwards. **What the live check found:** (1) the reason for the most common failure — an exhausted heal — was still not recorded, because the first version read only `error` and `assert`, and that path neither throws nor asserts: 3 of the 8 failures in a real re-run printed the placeholder; the information had been in the record all along; (2) the server promised `agentctl` a `--src` filter by AUDIENCE name and compared the string exactly, so `src=business` answered 200 and an EMPTY list — and an empty list reads as "this run has no records about the application", i.e. as a statement about the run rather than about the query. 11 mutations, 11 killed — two of them survived the first pass and both turned out to be defects in the gate (the fake threw the transport type DIRECTLY, bypassing the real boundary; the assertion about the value was satisfied through a DIFFERENT channel). | Accepted | HEALTH-004 backlog item 2: "in the very stream a person searches, 'the application returned the wrong thing' and 'we could not find the button' are one and the same line". | ADR-115 | 2026-08-04 | **The catalog starts speaking about the APPLICATION** (HEALTH-004, PR-1c). Three parts. **(1) Interface drift is a fact about the application.** `heal.drift_*` is emitted by the heal, so the derived source made them tool records, and the "business" filter hid the product's only message that the interface under test had changed. A NARROW source override was introduced (`src` plus a mandatory `src_why`; the gate fails the set on an override with no justification): the category stays `heal` — drift is a notion of self-healing, and a filter on `heal` is obliged to show it — while the source becomes `application`. Moving the CATEGORY was rejected: it would have taken the genuine heal diagnostics with it, and `heal.budget_exhausted` ("we ran out of budget") would have become the application's fault; that is pinned by a separate check. **(2) Partial grounding stopped being mute.** A goal run that bound 3 references out of 10 reported a counter and exited green — exactly the shape "we exited green having silently lost quality". Now there is `plan.partially_grounded` (`degrades`, so it reaches the verdict), and the exit code does NOT change: a goal is a direction, not a specification, and demanding that every reference be bound would make the mode unusable. The code's domain is `tool`: the cause is mixed (the page may not contain those controls, the model may have named them badly, our matcher may have failed to bind them), and the honest answer with a mixed cause is "our authoring came up short". **(3) Speed did not exist.** "The request failed" was in the catalog, "the request is slow" was not — even though Async Wait is the most frequent source of flakiness in the literature. | ADR-118 | 2026-08-08 | **Readiness is not remembered: on demand AND pre-emptively** (HEALTH-006 PR-A, Go only). ⚠ **A measurement narrowed the task and cancelled its most dangerous part.** The backlog demanded "a reconnect instead of a remembered miss", from which a mutable `s.store` followed — and that field has **33 unsynchronized readers** across seven non-test files, while `-race` exists NOWHERE in CI, i.e. no existing gate would have seen the race. Measured live: with a client already built, killing the gateway moves `store` to `error`, and its return moves it back to `ok` **without restarting control-api**. So `grpc.ClientConn` heals itself, and what remembered the miss was not the connection but `newStoreClient`, which CLOSED it and returned nil. The decision: the client is returned TOGETHER WITH the error and kept — the caller learns that the gateway did not answer (and writes that to the log), while the same client picks the gateway up the moment it appears. No redial loop, no mutable field, no race. As a side effect **the two reasons for `s.store == nil` became cleaner**: nil now means "no address declared" and nothing else — exactly the distinction `configfile_test.go` keeps load-bearing. **The pre-emptive half:** `runReadinessProber` with a DECLARED interval of 15 s (measured: the store probe is a local unix RPC, `probeLLM` and `probeBrowser` are network probes on the `readyProbeTimeout` budget; 15 s is no more than four outbound calls a minute against an endpoint nobody asked us to poll, and "known before it was asked" still holds at any human scale; the interval is LONGER than `readyCacheTTL`, so a tick refreshes the memo that ordinary callers use as well). **TRANSITIONS are logged, not ticks:** a record per tick would produce ~5700 lines a day stating that nothing had changed and would bury the single line about the store going away — precisely what the levels in ADR-116 were built to prevent. The rule is factored out into the pure function `componentTransitions`, so it is asserted without waiting out the interval; the first observation does not count as a transition. **Publication goes THROUGH `readiness()`**, not straight into `readyState`: a direct write would overwrite `invalidateReadiness()` after a config PUT and would bring back a stale verdict — the very race `epoch` exists for. **The browser probe** closes an exception that called itself a GAP: `depends_on: browser: service_healthy` made the component mandatory at startup and unobservable afterwards. It goes through `s.client()` (`liveClient` deliberately has NO overall timeout, because `/live/mjpeg` is an open stream and a probe through it would hang `/readyz` itself), an unset `CONTROL_API_CDP_LIVE` gives `skipped` rather than `error` (otherwise every single-container deployment is 503 forever), and it reads `/live/status`, whose contract is 200 with a reason, so a live but idle browser is `ok`. The `MAX_UNPROBED` ratchet was lowered 3→2 in the same commit, as its construction requires. | Accepted | Rejected: **a mutable `s.store`** (a race across 33 readers, invisible to every gate, in exchange for behaviour the connection provides by itself) · **a redial per request** (`ownerOfRow` and `accountsExist` sit on the hot path the hub polls every 2 s, with `storeCallTimeout` at 3 s — a dead store would add up to three seconds to every poll) · **a log line per tick** (see the volume) · **the browser probe through `liveClient`** (with no overall timeout it hangs `/readyz`) · **moving the dependencies into `/healthz`** (it would destroy the liveness contract: the orchestrator would RESTART a healthy process whose store merely happens to be down). |
| ADR-117 | 2026-08-08 | **A log record's text is RENDERED from the catalog rather than assembled** (DEBT-GO-EVENTLOG). Measured: `brain/eventlog.py::log(code, **fields)` takes the catalog entry and substitutes the fields — a second statement about the format does not exist, there is nothing to drift apart, and an uncatalogued code honestly emits `eventlog.uncatalogued`. In Go the text was assembled by concatenation while the catalog lived separately, and that is **exactly what produced the six drifted codes in PR-B**: the hub pulls the values out of the EN sentence BY the EN template in order to redraw them in Russian, and on a mismatch it silently falls back to English, one line at a time. A screenshot found it; the gate could not. The decision: `internal/eventlog.Render(code, fields)` — one renderer for three emitters (control-api, agentctl, the browser service). **Three things that are NOT carried over from Python and are written down here so that nobody "fixes" them back:** ① **the level stays an argument** — `service.api_call` is `debug` on a read and `info` on a mutation, `service.api_refused` is `warn` on 401/403 and `error` on 5xx, whereas the catalog declares one level per code; reading the level off the entry would silently demote every 5xx to `warn` and every mutation to `debug`, and `svclog` filters ON WRITE with a default of `info` ⇒ mutations would stop being written at all; ② **redaction moved onto the FIELD** (`redact.Value`): `redact.Line` scans an assembled sentence for the `name=value` shape, and over a template from the catalog it would be scanning OUR prose — the wording of `service.token_source` already had to be steered away from the `token: ` shape once; when the name and the value are separate the NAME is authoritative, and that is strictly stronger than before; ③ **`En` is decoded, `Ru` is not** — this reverses half of the ADR-064-era decision that "bilingual text is the browser's business": the caution was right about `ru` and wrong about `en`, because the server must own exactly one sentence — the one it rendered. **The signature is shaped so that a hand-assembled sentence has NOWHERE to go:** `journalEvent/journalSubject` take fields, not a `msg`. **The wording gate was KEPT even though the task said to remove it:** its former assertion became a tautology, but the new one — "every message went through the renderer" — does not follow from the construction, because `svclog.Log` still accepts a `Msg` field; deleting it would have removed the only thing standing between main and the next hand-assembled message. The catalog gate was widened along the way: the emitter map held `pw-executor` as ONE file, so the browser service's emissions were outside any check whatsoever, and the catalog walk took `*.go` only. | Accepted | Rejected: **taking the level from the catalog** (see ① — a silent loss of mutations from the log) · **redacting the assembled line** (see ② — a scanner over our own prose) · **moving the code literals into a shared package** (the catalog gate is keyed BY PATH and would have turned all 14 `service.*` into phantoms) · **tokenizing `{reason}` here as well** (a token with no resolver reads worse than prose for both readers; that is [JOURNAL-VALUE-I18N], and naming the fields unblocked it without closing it). |
| ADR-116 | 2026-08-04 | **The service log: everything the tool does is written down — and the volume is taken off by LEVELS, not by selection** (HEALTH-005, core). Measured: `runs/<id>/logs/` is about the RUN and nothing else (`newLogSink` is called from a single place), everything else went to stderr, that is into the container's log: not a file, not a directory, not filterable, not in the UI. Worse: `session.go` and `configfile.go` had **zero** logging lines of either kind, so creating an account, changing a password, granting admin, DELETING an account, editing the global config, rotating the machine token and starting with an unreachable store left **no trace**. The `users` table stores the RESULT (the account exists) and never stored the EVENT (who created it, when, from where). **Alex's directive:** record all service operations, including access and every call to the API, and the first stage — the service coming up, whatever brings it up; and implement levels. **Levels are the answer to volume:** reads `debug`, mutations `info`, a refusal and a touch of SOMEONE ELSE'S row `warn`, 5xx `error`; the default is `info`, so the hub's polling (measured: `/v1/runs/{id}` every 2 s, ~300 records for a ten-minute run) does not bury the line "account deleted", while `SENTINEL_SERVICE_LOG_LEVEL=debug` brings it all back. The filter is on the WRITE side: a log whose noise is damped only by a read filter still pays for it in disk. **The hook sits in `guard`** — the single place all 42 routes pass through, so a route added tomorrow is logged because it is in the table and not because somebody remembered; forty-two manual insertions are exactly how ADR-109 produced the defect it then fixed itself. **The medium is a file, not a store table:** `s.store == nil` is a supported tier (ADR-075); an audit trail absent exactly where single users most often work is not an audit trail, and a store that has fallen over must not take down the record that it fell over. **One format for both streams** (`svclog.Record`; control-api aliases it) — two structures about one wire are the `lvKindOf`-versus-catalog wound. The supervisor is determined BY MEASUREMENT (`INVOCATION_ID`/`JOURNAL_STREAM` → systemd, `/.dockerenv`/cgroup → a container, otherwise by hand), and an unreadable cgroup yields `unknown` rather than a guess. 12 mutations, 12 killed. | Accepted | Alex: "logging needs to be done properly, so that it is easier for us afterwards". Rejected: **a table in the store** (no log in standalone and none with the store down — exactly where it is needed most) · **selection instead of levels** (a log in which an event cannot be found works like no log at all; but the reads cannot be discarded either — access to someone else's data is visible only in them) · **parsing other services' output** (`ollama`, `litellm`, `webui`) into a structure they do not have — they get docker's rotation, and the boundary is declared rather than left unsaid. ⚠ The catalog gate was widened along the way: it read ONE file per emitter, so code in any other file of the same binary was invisible to it — a blind spot in a check whose job is "the catalog cannot promise a message nobody sends". |
| ADR-119 | 2026-08-09 | **The aggregate `/metrics` scrape moves to control-api; `report-service` is removed, having never run a single day (QA-REPORT-SERVICE).** `cmd/report-service/` had been built, packaged and cosign-signed since ADR-021 (2026-06-26), but was invoked by NOTHING: absent from the `Dockerfile` (which builds agentctl/store-gateway/control-api), from all three compose files, and from `install.sh`; test coverage — 0.0%. `GET /metrics` is **root-level, unversioned** (the Prometheus convention; `/healthz`/`/readyz` are root-level too). **Access is `accessAuthed` + an owner filter IN THE HANDLER**, not an `{id}` guard: the aggregate has no single `{id}` for a route guard to key on, so the scoping lives in the handler — the same shape as `handleListRuns` and `GET /v1/service-log`. The machine token and a deployment with no accounts (`owner == ""`) are unfiltered — that is the operator's scrape; everyone else is scoped CLOSED (a store that doesn't answer ⇒ a run isn't attributed ⇒ it isn't shown). **The `run="<id>"` label is injected AT AGGREGATION TIME in control-api** (`cmd/control-api/metrics_agg.go`), not at the source: `brain/report.py::_metrics` writes bare series (`sentinel_run_steps 7`) with no `# HELP`/`# TYPE` and no label — fixing the source would not have reached the 192 run directories already on disk with no run label, and the old byte concatenation of N files would have produced N duplicates of the same series (a broken scrape, not an aggregate). The aggregator groups by family, prints one `# HELP`/`# TYPE` per family (the type is DERIVED from the name — a `_total` suffix means counter, everything else gauge), caps the response at the 500 newest runs and **reports what it dropped** (`sentinel_metrics_runs_included`, `sentinel_metrics_runs_omitted{reason="cap\|unreadable\|too_large\|conflict"}`, `sentinel_metrics_lines_dropped`), caches the walk for 10 s with single-flight, and skips unreadable directories instead of failing with 5xx. **Consequence:** `/metrics` (the labelled aggregate) and the per-run `metrics.prom` artifact (`brain/report.py`, UNlabelled) are now DIFFERENT things — not one format under two names. | Accepted | **Retracts the `report-service` clause of ADR-021** (which introduced it as HTTP `/report`+`/metrics` "only for the long-lived orchestrator/service mode"): that mode exists (control-api has been long-lived since M9.3), but a separate process for two routes nobody ever deployed is not justified when control-api is already long-lived and already carries auth/owner scoping. ADR-021 stands as the record of the decision made at the time — it is not rewritten. `/report/<id>` (per-run HTML/JSON) loses nothing: it was never served by report-service either — reports are produced by `agentctl report` and served like any other run artifact (`GET /v1/runs/{id}/artifact`). **Rejected:** reviving `report-service` as its own process (a second binary, a second compose service, a second auth perimeter for two routes, one of which was never opened) · versioning the route as `/v1/metrics` (the Prometheus convention, and our own `/healthz`/`/readyz`, are root-level) · porting the `metrics.prom` concatenation as-is (N duplicates of one series — not an aggregate). |
| ADR-120 | 2026-08-09 | **Observing a run is ONE set of modes the PERSON chooses; the VLM layer sits on top and does not depend on that choice (LIVE-MATRIX + LIVE-CONSUMERS).** The measurement behind it: observation was governed by FOUR unrelated switches read in four places across two languages — `PW_HEADED`/`PW_HEADLESS` (`pw-executor/src/launch.ts:26`), `SENTINEL_TRACE_SCREENSHOTS` (`pw-executor/src/server.ts:489,1086`), `SENTINEL_LIVE_FRAMES` (`brain/graph.py:45`) — so nobody could answer "what will I see and what does it cost", because no single place knew. Worse: **the same picture is gated by TWO variables in TWO languages**, so switching one off yields a half-observed run that says nothing about the missing half. **(1) The mode set is the user's:** `off` · `frames` · `stream` · `human` (stream + synthetic cursor + `slowMo` + highlight; **CHANGES TIMING**) · `record`. Every mode must state its applicability and its cost in the interface, not merely its name. **(2) The default lives in deployment settings, a run overrides it, and the default is SHOWN** — an invisible default makes "I did not choose" and "I chose exactly this" the same act. The CLI takes the same name as a flag. **(3) The VLM layer is not a mode but a CONSEQUENCE OF HAVING a model** able to read our screenshots and video. It runs whenever such a model is configured **and capture is on**; when a VLM is configured, capture and frame processing are on BY DEFAULT. If the person turned capture off, the model gets nothing — and the product must SAY so, because a heal that silently never ran is indistinguishable from one with nothing to heal. **(4) TWO capture paths, not one compromise.** The human's video ALWAYS carries the cursor — it is THEIR artifact and its name says so. Frames and video for models and goldens are CLEAN: the cursor overlay is drawn INTO the page (the same injection path as set-of-marks), so it is removed AROUND the model's capture rather than abandoned. The golden's byte stability is protected by the same mechanism. **(5) `human` and golden DO NOT MIX: refusal BEFORE START with exit 3**, the shape of `fatal.force_replay_in_ci` / `fatal.secret_would_leak_to_trace` (`brain/__main__.py:719,725`). Slowing down and decorating do not degrade a reference — they make it WRONG, and it surfaces later on somebody else's run. **(6) `PW_NO_TRACE` is NOT in the set and is not driven by a mode.** It is a fail-closed secret guard with TWO enforcement points: `pw-executor/src/server.ts:610` throws when a secret is entered while tracing is active, `brain/__main__.py:723` exits 3 before starting. An `off` that clears it silently removes the guard; one that sets it breaks every login run. | Accepted | Decisions (2), (3), (4) are Alex's directives of 2026-08-09; (5) is his choice among three proposed forms. **Rejected:** DERIVING the observation mode instead of letting the person choose (built and reverted the same day — the observation axis was confused with the GROUNDING axis, where deriving is in fact right) · binding `off` to clearing `PW_NO_TRACE` · one compromise frame serving both human and model · an invisible default · `LIVE-HLS`. **Deferred with a recorded reason:** `stream` should follow a viewer ATTACHING rather than run continuously; the attachment signal arrives with `LIVE-PER-RUN`. |
| ADR-121 | 2026-08-09 | **The live view answers about the RUN, not the SERVICE: a page is claimed by its Chromium `targetId`, and a run that cannot be resolved is refused rather than shown someone else's picture (LIVE-PER-RUN).** With two runs in one browser, `currentPage` (`pw-executor/src/cdp-service.ts`) handed every viewer the newest page — the hub could only say so in words, because the topology could not say it in data: showing another run's picture is worse than showing none, since absence is visible and a wrong picture is indistinguishable from a right one. **The measurement the decision rests on was taken BEFORE the code was written:** the exploration assumed there was nothing to label until the executor created its own page; a direct measurement of `Target.getTargetInfo` disproved that — CDP returns the SAME `targetId` to every client, including an observer that only ADOPTED the page (`connectOverCDP`), so the mechanism also works in CDP-attach mode (ADR-037), where the executor owns nothing. **Mechanism:** `cdp-service.ts`'s six process-wide singletons (frame/session/page/idle/waiters/ack-error-count) become `sessions: Map<targetId, LiveSession>`; once its page exists, the executor posts `POST /live/claim {run_id, target_id}` (`pw-executor/src/server.ts::claimLivePage`), filling `claims: Map<run_id, {targetId, at}>`. Resolving a run (`resolve(runId)`) tells apart THREE refusals instead of collapsing them into one: unclaimed · claimed but the page is gone · no page at all — each with its own `reason`, returned as a 503 rather than the path of least resistance. An unnamed request (`agentctl live frame` with no `--run-id`, every pre-existing caller) stays legal and answers about the newest page as before, but is now EXPLICITLY marked `scoped:false` in the `/live/status` JSON and via `X-Sentinel-Run`/`X-Sentinel-Scoped` headers on the frame itself — a JPEG cannot say that in its body. **The claim is fail-open**: a failed `claimLivePage` does not fail the run (observation must not be allowed to kill work) but is not silent either — the reason is logged. **The identifier is deliberately `targetId`, not page order, URL or creation time**: all three were available and all three are wrong exactly when two runs overlap. **`cmd/control-api/live.go` carried a defect the same measurement found**: `handleLiveStatus` and `proxyLive` built the browser-service URL independently, so `?run_id=` reached `proxyLive` but was dropped on the floor in `handleLiveStatus` — nothing failed, status simply answered about a different page. Both call sites now go through one function, `liveTargetURL`. **`SENTINEL_RUN_ID` is a second defect found along the way**: `agentctl` minted a random `runID` with no link to `--artifact-dir`, the name control-api had already given the run (`runs/control-<id>`) — the claim would have gone out under a different name, and the hub would never have met it. control-api now passes `SENTINEL_RUN_ID=<id>` to the child process, and `agentctl` takes it when set (not `RUN_ID` — a common shell variable whose accidental inheritance would let two unrelated runs claim one identity). **Compose is untouched, and that is established by measurement, not skipped**: `PW_CDP_ENDPOINT=http://browser:9223` is already set for control-api (`docker-compose.yml:216`), the browser's live port is 9224 (`:207`), so the claim address is DERIVED from `PW_CDP_ENDPOINT` (the port swapped for `CDP_LIVE_PORT`, the path for `/live/claim`; the derivation is announced in the log), overridable via an explicit `PW_LIVE_CLAIM` — a second address in compose would be a second way to get it wrong, and the YAML merge key has already swallowed `PW_CDP_ENDPOINT` whole once before. A claim outlives its page by `CLAIM_TTL_MS` (12 hours by default) — that is what makes "the run finished" distinguishable from "the run never started". Gate: `tests/test_live_video_offline.py` grew 4→8 checks (against the real `cdp-service` + real `control-api` + a real run), 6 mutations, 6 killed — one survivor (emptying the list of forwarded headers) was closed: a frame's ownership lives in a header, not the JSON every prior assertion read. | Accepted | Alex's directive — showing another run's picture is worse than showing none. **Rejected:** labeling a page by order/URL/creation time (all three break exactly when two runs run at once) · substituting someone else's page for an unresolved named request (indistinguishable from a correct answer) · a separate claim address in compose (a second way to drift from `PW_CDP_ENDPOINT`, which a YAML merge key has already swallowed once) · a fatal claim (observation must not be allowed to kill work). |
| ADR-123 | 2026-08-10 | **Pluggable adapters stop being a roadmap word: one registry, three kinds, and the built-ins registered INTO it (M9.7, generalizes ADR-045).** **The four measurements that set the scope:** (1) `grep -c 'adapter\|SPI'` over `internal/` and `cmd/` returns **0** — the word lived in the roadmap and in one document, nowhere in the product; (2) `brain/llm.py::make_backend` branched on TWO literal provider names and ended in `log("llm.backend_unknown"); return None` — what ADR-045 made pluggable was OpenAI-COMPATIBILITY, not the provider slot, so a backend with a different SDK required editing the core file; (3) `brain/runconfig.py::_AUTH_ENV` was a four-entry literal dict and `load_run_config` DROPPED every `auth:` sub-key outside it: an out-of-tree auth mechanism could not even RECEIVE its configuration; (4) deploy had no runtime seam at all — Helm `extraEnv` is evaluated before the process starts. Hence the scope: GENERALIZE what exists, adding no product capability. **Mechanism:** `brain/adapters.py` — `register`/`get`/`require`/`names`/`unregister`, kinds `model\|auth\|deploy`, discovery via `SENTINEL_ADAPTERS` (importing the module IS the registration). `ModelAdapter.make(ModelSpec)` receives the ALREADY-resolved environment (role → model/base URL/key/flags), so an adapter never re-implements the `LLM_<KEY>_<ROLE>` > `LLM_<KEY>` precedence and cannot drift from it; `EnvAdapter.env(spec)` is a PURE block→mapping function, while the "explicit flag > file" precedence stays in `runconfig.py::_apply_block` — otherwise an adapter could quietly outrank the operator's own flag. `anthropic` and `openai` are registered as ordinary adapters (`_AnthropicAdapter`/`_OpenAICompatAdapter`), so `make_backend` has exactly ONE provider path: a seam only third-party code travels is a seam nothing in CI exercises. `sampling` (ADR-020) resolves BEFORE the registry: it is a property of HOW the process runs, and a plugin of that name must not be able to displace it. References: **`storage_state`** = the M9.1 login-as-test flow verbatim (ADR-026), **`local`** = `STORE_ADDR`/`OTEL_EXPORTER_OTLP_ENDPOINT`/`CHECKPOINT_DSN` — ⚠ `ARTIFACT_DIR` and `PW_EXECUTOR_CMD` were left OUT by measurement: `cmd/agentctl/main.go` emits both for every run it spawns, so a RunConfig value would lose to the explicit one ALWAYS and the key would be decoration. Each env adapter's `keys` is DERIVED from its table in `__init_subclass__` — a hand-kept companion fails in the direction you cannot see (a stale entry breaks loudly, a missing one is silence, and the key is then dropped by the loader). **Failure is split deliberately:** an unimportable `SENTINEL_ADAPTERS` module RAISES — on the model path that becomes `llm.provider_unavailable` + the heuristic fallback (a run without AI, announced), on the RunConfig path a config error (exit 3); an adapter asked for BY NAME and not registered raises ALWAYS, because a typo in `adapter:` that fell back to "no auth" is a run that looks successful and did not do the thing. **The ADR-056 §2 row 42 licence boundary is CHECKED, not written down:** the gate walks `adapters.names(kind)` and reddens on enterprise-auth vocabulary (Keycloak/OIDC/Vault/SSO/RBAC/SAML/LDAP) — that attaches to the SPI FROM OUTSIDE (`[M-COMMERCIAL-auth]`); Apache is irreversible. The gate `tests/test_adapter_spi_offline.py` is thirteen groups, ALL behavioural (its adapters register into the real registry and the shipped `make_backend`/`load_run_config`/`apply_run_config` are called; no check reads a source file), the declared-sub-key walk is DERIVED with a floor of "≥2 adapters, ≥7 keys"; 13 mutations applied, 13 killed, all compiled. Contract `docs/M9.7_CONTRACT.en.md`, umbrella doc `docs/ADAPTERS.en.md` §1. | Accepted | **Rejected:** entry-point metadata for discovery (requires a pip install into the same environment, while the product routinely runs from a source tree with `uv sync --frozen` — half the deployments could not use it) · a silent fallback to the default adapter on an unknown name (indistinguishable from correct configuration) · an adapter that writes the environment itself (it could outrank the operator's explicit flag) · `ARTIFACT_DIR`/`PW_EXECUTOR_CMD` in the deploy adapter (agentctl always sets them — a decorative key) · a new layer on top of `LLMBackend`/`_AUTH_ENV` instead of generalizing them (the milestone is a STABLE seam, not a second abstraction) · enterprise auth in this tree (ADR-056 §2; Apache is irreversible) |
| ADR-124 | 2026-08-14 | **The image installs browsers BY NAME instead of inheriting somebody's set (DIST-VARIANTS).** The base was `mcr.microsoft.com/playwright:v1.61.1-noble`, which ships all three engines while ADR-036 fixes Chromium-only. Measured before the change: image 2.79 GB, `/ms-playwright` 1.2 GB — chromium 379M · headless-shell 262M · ffmpeg 4.9M · **firefox 293M · webkit 290M**, plus the system dependencies of all three inside the 2.03 GB and 330 MB base layers. 583 MB of engines were never launched. The base is now `node:24-bookworm-slim` and the browsers are installed with `playwright install --with-deps chromium chromium-headless-shell`. The result is measured, not estimated: **1.58 GB (−1.21 GB, −43%)**, a run yields the SAME `plan_hash`, and the browser service still comes up. Full `chromium` is kept deliberately — `chromium.launch()` in `cdp-service` resolves to it, and headed mode and the LIVE arc need it. The browser directory's contents are DERIVED and compared with the declaration by a step of the `airgap` job: an engine that appears undeclared is red. | Accepted | Variant (a) "minimal" of DIST-VARIANTS. Registry and certified builds (variants c/d) do not belong in this tree — ADR-056 §2, they are `🔒COMM`. |
| ADR-125 | 2026-08-16 | **A run's video: the mode that WRITES A FILE carries the cursor and therefore inherits every constraint of `human`; over CDP it is IMPOSSIBLE and is refused at the door rather than degraded (LIVE-RECORD + PROD-FAIL-MEDIA part A).** Three decisions, each against a plausible alternative. **(1) The cursor.** Alex's requirement of 2026-08-02: a recording without one is as unreadable as a bare screencast. So `record` draws into the page — and drawing means `slowMo` and an overlay, i.e. ALL of `human`'s consequences: changed timing, unusable as a golden. Derived from ONE tuple, `observe.DECORATED`, so the two facts cannot be set apart; the rejected alternative, "record without a cursor", would have given a readable name and an unreadable file. **(2) Refusal, not degradation, over CDP.** `recordVideo` is a `newContext` option, and in CDP-attach the context is ADOPTED (`server.ts`). `slowMo` has the same shape of problem and degrades — the pacing is paid by our own pause. Video has nothing to pay with: the run would simply end with no file. So the combination is declined BEFORE the start (`observe.resolve`), and the executor carries a second, louder `videoUnavailable` guard in case the switch arrived by another route. The rejected alternative — quiet degradation — is exactly the class of silence this whole arc exists to remove. **(3) The decision is taken AT THE START, and that is recorded as the price.** ADR-084 decides about the trace at the END, so a green run's bytes never touch the disk. Not possible here: the file is written as the run goes, and only a deletion afterwards remains. Per Alex's decision (`[PROD-FAIL-MEDIA]`) it is dropped on green and `SENTINEL_VIDEO_ALWAYS=1` keeps it; the discard is ANNOUNCED with the lever's name, or a person who asked to record a passing run reads the absence as the mode being broken. ⚠ Part A of `[PROD-FAIL-MEDIA]` closed alongside: `capture_frame` moved out of `graph.py` into `brain/frames.py` and is called from replay at a SINGLE point — where the seven failure branches already converge on `report["steps"].append`. An eighth branch is covered by construction. | Accepted | Executes `[LIVE-RECORD]` and `[PROD-FAIL-MEDIA]` A. `NOT_YET` in `brain/observe.py` is now EMPTY: every declared mode is performed. Media encryption is NOT here — it is wider and lives in `[SEC-USER-DATA-AT-REST]` |
| ADR-126 | 2026-08-16 | **The orchestrator starts being called, and its form is a LONG-LIVED SERVICE: it DECIDES, control-api ENFORCES (ORCH-WIRE).** `cmd/orchestrator` had been built by every release and installed by the `.deb` since M8, and NOTHING launched it — no `docker-compose*.yml`, no `Dockerfile`, no `install.sh`, and no systemd unit. The consequence absent from the task's own wording: what was dead was not "the orchestrator's features" but **the hard budget ceiling (ADR-021), takeover and return (ADR-054) and the map gate (ADR-108c) — in EVERY shipped configuration**; `forwardControl` answered "no orchestrator wired" and `brain/runcontrol.py::_Noop.wired` was False. **The ADR-119 precedent (`report-service` deleted for exactly this) does NOT apply here, and that is said plainly:** the orchestrator has a live consumer in the code (`ws.go`); it was merely unconfigured. ⚠ **There were three gaps, all from one cause:** `proto` and `main()` wanted different things. The proto has `StartRun(run_id, limits)` — a registration call into a long-lived service; `main()` was a one-shot CLI wrapper that minted its OWN `run_id`, listened on the per-run socket `state/sentinel-orch-<id>.sock` (which a static `CONTROL_API_ORCH_ADDR` cannot name even in principle) and spawned `python -m brain` itself, bypassing `agentctl` — making it a SECOND owner of the run lifecycle. The one-shot path is deleted; `agentctl` is again the single owner, and the third gap dissolved on its own: `ReportEvent.run_id` already equals control-api's id (`SENTINEL_RUN_ID` → `RUN_ID`). ⚠ **The ADR-021 hard ceiling is not lost but relocated — a decision, not a side effect.** The old backstop was `cmd.Process.Signal(SIGTERM)`, possible only because that process was the brain's parent. A service cannot do it **physically**: under compose each service has its own PID namespace. Enforcement moved to control-api, whose mechanism is STRICTLY better — `cancel.go` signals the process GROUP (SIGTERM, then SIGKILL), reaching the brain, the executor AND Chromium, where the old one reached a single process. ⚠ **No `proto` change was needed, and that is a measurement rather than luck:** the poll is a ZERO-delta `ReportEvent` — an idiom this tree already has (`brain/runcontrol.py::poll()` is exactly that) — so there is no stub regeneration in two languages and no second way to ask one question. Delivery: the service in two compose files (the offline form carries no control plane at all — it is `sentinel`/`demo`/`ollama`/`airgap`), a FOURTH binary in the `Dockerfile` (three of four shipped before) and the `sentinel-orchestrator` unit in the `.deb`, installed DISABLED. ⚠ Fail-open every way in: no orchestrator, an unreachable socket, a service that dies mid-run — the run proceeds as before, because supervision is an ADDITION to a run and never a precondition. | Accepted | Executes `[QA-ORCHESTRATOR-UNLAUNCHED]`, Alex's decision "fix it, let it be called". A gap found along the way was closed with it: `internal/orchestrator/pb` NEVER had a stub-reproducibility gate, unlike `internal/store/pb` (ADR-109). **Rejected:** dropping the orchestrator from delivery per ADR-119 (it has a consumer) · keeping the per-run form (nothing can address it) · adding a pid field to `proto` so the daemon could kill (impossible across PID namespaces) |
| ADR-127 | 2026-08-17 | **The real screen behind the `vnc` profile — ONE image, not two: a measurement overturned the decision it had produced (LIVE-VNC).** A headed Chromium inside Xvfb `:99`, exported by `x11vnc` over RFB — the only transport with a REAL cursor, and the one that makes takeover (ADR-054) something a mouse can do. **(1) The default `browser` stays HEADLESS and is untouched:** a headed browser renders DIFFERENT pixels, and `screenshot_hash` is byte-stable only in headless (`docs/DETERMINISM.md`) — flipping the default would silently invalidate every golden on the next replay. Hence two services rather than a flag on one. **(2) ⚠ THE SECOND IMAGE WAS REJECTED BY MEASUREMENT, and that is this ADR's central decision.** It had been adopted on the number «+246 MB, xvfb alone is +225». Re-measured by building BOTH shapes: `runtime` 1513 MB → with a `vnc` stage 1524 MB, i.e. **+10 MB (0.66%)**. The discrepancy is explained: the earlier figure is right for a BARE base (on `node:24-bookworm-slim` the same two packages cost +235 MB), but our `runtime` already carries the X stack, installed by `npx playwright install --with-deps chromium` — the very line whose comment keeps the full Chromium «for headed mode and the LIVE arc». What is actually added is `x11vnc` and 17 dependencies. A second image would have bought those 10 MB at the price of a second GHCR tag, a second cosign signature, a second SBOM, a branch in `release.yml` and a `docker-compose.ghcr.yml` that only works if that image was PUBLISHED — five surfaces that execute only on a tag. That is exactly the defect class found three times in one session (the orchestrator · the ghcr form · the Windows cross-build). **(3) `xvfb` is named EXPLICITLY** although present transitively: depending on someone else's `--with-deps` list makes the profile hostage to a Playwright release, and the failure would arrive as «Xvfb: not found» in a container nobody rebuilt on purpose. **(4) `websockify` is NOT introduced.** The hub will reach the screen through control-api's own relay (the next PR of this arc), so a separate container would be a service nobody exercises between releases; `[LIVE-VNC-OWN-BRIDGE]` is closed early. **(5) ⚠ THERE IS NO PASSWORD, AND THAT IS A SECURITY DECISION RATHER THAN AN OMISSION — reworked 2026-08-18 after CodeQL.** The first version served RFB over TCP with classic VNC Authentication, and CodeQL raised two high `go/weak-cryptographic-algorithm`: that scheme is DES over the FIRST EIGHT BYTES of the password, over an unencrypted channel. Dismissing the alert as «mandated by the protocol» would have been true and beside the point: **the owner's rule is that weak ciphers do not ship** — so the algorithm goes, not the alert. Measurement found a replacement that is STRONGER, not weaker: `x11vnc -unixsock <path> -rfbport 0` → `/proc/net/tcp` EMPTY (no TCP listener at all) → security types `[1] = None` (DES appears nowhere) → a full session works (ServerInit + 655 360 bytes of pixels); the socket at 0600 → a foreign uid gets **EACCES** on connect, the owner connects. Authentication is therefore FILE PERMISSIONS, decided by the kernel before a byte of RFB is spoken. It also closes a surface measured the day before: the old RFB port answered on the container's bridge IP **straight from the host**, so «not published» never meant «not reachable from this machine»; a unix socket cannot be reached that way at all. The password took with it the `agentctl vnc-password` verb, the `internal/vncsecret` package and the `service.vnc_password_source` code — all of which existed only to feed the weak scheme. ⚠ Measurements that bought this shape (x11vnc 0.9.16): `-passwdfile` reads PLAINTEXT from the first line · a trailing newline does not interfere · VNC auth checks the FIRST EIGHT bytes (a server holding `ABCDEFGH12345678` accepted `ABCDEFGH` and refused `ABCDEFGX`) — hence exactly 8 generated characters, the only honest number. | Accepted | Executes `[LIVE-VNC]`. **Rejected:** a second `sentinel:vnc` image (measurement above) · `websockify` as its own container (a service with no consumer) · the obfuscated `-rfbauth` form (fixed-key DES — no confidentiality, and the password would not survive a restart) · publishing the RFB port to the host (eight bytes of DES over an unencrypted channel is not the equivalent of a bearer token) |
| ADR-128 | 2026-08-18 | **A run opens ITS OWN page in someone else's browser: the session is reused, the tab is not (LIVE-OWN-TAB).** ADR-037 promised to reuse the user's session **and** their open tab; the second half is withdrawn deliberately (Alex, 2026-08-16). **The measurement that bought it:** in CDP-attach the executor adopted `contexts()[0]` and then `pages()[0]`, so two concurrent runs drove ONE tab and announced ONE `targetId` — both `84DC6185`, measured live on 2026-08-10. ADR-121's label was correct and had nothing to separate: the browser had not separated them, so `/live/*` honestly refused BOTH, and the live view worked for nobody whenever two runs overlapped. **(1) `context.newPage()` separates them**, not a new context: a new context means new cookies, i.e. losing the very session CDP-attach exists for. Measured after the change: two runs → two `targetId`s, different `url`s in `/live/status` (`testdata/site` vs `site-v2`), frames differing byte-for-byte (9 882 vs 10 105 bytes), each carrying its own `X-Sentinel-Run`. NOTHING changed in launch mode: `browser.newContext()` returns a context with no pages, so a page was always created there — and every golden in existence was captured through that path. **(2) A run's pages are its own plus the popups whose `opener()` is one of ours.** A tab the human opened is not the run's: otherwise `browser.switchTab` could point the run at someone else's page and `attachAppCapture` (ADR-067) would copy its console into `runs/<id>/`. ⚠ The predicate was chosen by measurement rather than by plausibility: `opener()` survives `rel="noopener"` (Chrome severs `window.opener` in the DOM but keeps the target's `openerId`), so a popup from `target=_blank rel=noopener` — the strictest form — is tracked, while a bystander's `context.newPage()` yields `opener() === null` and is left alone. **(3) What the run opened, the run closes — and nothing else.** The browser and the context are untouched (ADR-037's guard is intact in that half). ⚠ The first version closed only the FIRST page, and a measurement caught the remainder: a run driving `l6-newtab.html` left its popup `l1.html` behind — half a leak is still a leak, and the browser service outlives every run. Every page in `pages[]` is closed, which is exactly its own. **(4) A KILLED run must hand its pages back too**, and there the hole was: the executor's JSON-RPC transport — the one that actually ships — had NO signal handler at all. It had been written for the MCP transport only: one rule stated twice, missing the second time. There is now one handler for both transports, time-bounded, because a browser that will not answer must not hold a dying process open. **(5) The "two runs share a page" refusal STAYS and changes meaning**: it is no longer topology but a symptom of version skew (an executor older than ADR-128 against a newer browser service). Its wording now says so, and it writes the `service.live_claim_conflict` event into the service journal — a branch that ought to be unreachable is precisely the one whose firing must reach the deployment's own record rather than the stderr of a container nobody tails. ⚠ **Its own PAGE does not fix `recordVideo` over CDP** — recording is a property of the CONTEXT at creation and the context stays adopted; ADR-125's refusal at the door stands. **Gates:** `test_live_video_offline.py` 8→10 checks (two REAL concurrent runs — the case that was inexpressible before this ADR), a new `test_own_tab_offline.py` (the first coverage `browser.tabs`/`switchTab` from M9.4 A6 ever had), and `ownership.ts` with two pure predicates and eight units. 10 mutations, 9 killed; one recorded as equivalent: closing an ADOPTED context does not close its pages, so that mutation collapsed into "close nothing". | Accepted | Alex's decision of 2026-08-16: the live view must work when two runs overlap. **Rejected:** a context per run (losing the session is exactly what CDP-attach exists to avoid) · leaving the page open after the run (one tab per run in a service that outlives them) · closing it only when the run claimed a page with the service (behaviour INFERRED from a side signal instead of declared) · deleting the now-unreachable refusal branch (it would return the original defect to a deployment running an older executor) |
| ADR-129 | 2026-08-22 | **The deployment reports on what it does NOT check: `/readyz` probes its own run executable and publishes the components carried without a probe (W6, `[READYZ-BLIND-TO-AGENTCTL]`).** Until this decision `/readyz` was a **list of probes** rather than a register of components, and that produced exactly two silent lies. **(1) It never asked about its own executable.** Measured 2026-08-21: a control-api started with `CONTROL_API_AGENTCTL=/no/such/binary` comes up silently — the start line PRINTS that path and does not complain — answers `status: ready`, accepts `POST /v1/runs` with **202**, and every run then dies at `fork/exec`. Spawning `agentctl` is the whole reason this process exists (ADR-032), and it was the one dependency with no probe. `probeAgentctl` **never answers `skipped`**, unlike the browser, orchestrator and `vnc` probes: a deployment without a browser is supported and working, a deployment without a run executable does not exist, and `skipped` does not affect readiness — it would leave `ready` standing over a doomed stack. ⚠ The question is asked through **`exec.LookPath`, not `os.Stat` plus the `x` bit** as the defect report proposed: `LookPath` is literally what `exec.Command` resolves with, so the probe cannot answer yes to a path the spawn will refuse; Windows has no execute bit at all (`PATHEXT` decides) and `mode&0111` would report a permanent FALSE `error` on every Windows deployment; `syscall.Access(X_OK)` answers for the calling uid, so under root a `0644` file reads as executable — green over precisely the stack that is broken for everybody else. `os.Stat` remains, but only to PHRASE the verdict (absent / a directory / no execute bit), which are three different one-line fixes. Nothing is executed: the prober fires every 15s, and a probe that forks is a process factory. **(2) A component with no probe did not exist for the reader.** `componentsWithoutProbe` has been there since HEALTH-006 and was read by two offline gates and by NOBODY else, so the one surface a person opens with the question «what is up» could not tell «this component is fine» from «nobody ever asks about this component». It is now served under a **top-level `unprobed` key** — a decision, not a shape: `tests/test_readyz_covers_the_stack_offline.py` derives the probe set by regexing `checks["<name>"]` out of the source, so writing the exemptions INTO `checks` would make an unprobed service read as probed to the one check obliged to notice — the feature would defeat its own gate. The key is always present (a missing one is indistinguishable from an older server), stays out of the `ready` aggregate, out of the memo and out of the transition records; an anonymous caller sees the names, and only a token-bearing one sees the reasons. **(3) Reasons became bilingual by PROVENANCE** (`[HEALTH-REASON-EN]`): everything our code authors end to end carries `detail_ru` in the SAME literal (`componentNote`), while anything with `err.Error()` in it stays English — translating the wrapper and leaving a foreign tail is worse than leaving the whole, and the view says so once in a footnote that is DERIVED from the content. ⚠ The first cut drew the boundary on STATUS (translate `skipped`, leave `error`) — the wrong axis, and a SCREENSHOT found it: `config: ОТКАЗ / no config stored; run the setup wizard` was the single English line in a Russian table, and it is our sentence from end to end. `tests/test_readyz_reasons_translated_offline.py` derives the must-translate set out of `readyz.go` itself. **(4) A failed spawn stopped being silent:** `service.run_spawn_failed` (`warn`, subject = the run's owner, or the record would be admin-only) and `exit_code: -1` instead of a zero that read as a clean exit — the `run.finished` frame has emitted `-1` since ADR-089, so a client that polled and one that listened disagreed about one run. **The Health view changed its axis** from «ok / skipped / FAILING» to «up / not up / FAILING / no probe»: «skipped» is a statement about the PROBER, and beside the new «no probe» two words would have read alike for two opposite states. **128→129 ADRs.** | Accepted | Wave W6, Alex's decision of 2026-08-22: a panel for «what is up, what is not, and why», READ-ONLY. **Rejected:** `skipped` for a missing `agentctl` (leaves `ready` over a doomed stack) · `os.Stat`+`mode&0111` (false red on Windows, false green under root) · an `unprobed` status INSIDE `checks` (defeats its own gate, lies to the rail's «checked» counter and enters the aggregate) · running `agentctl --version` as the probe (a process factory every 15s; a hung binary would hang `/readyz`) · translating in the UI through a dictionary (the file's first runtime dictionary, keyed by a string the server may rewrite) · a second translation map beside `componentsWithoutProbe` (two statements of one fact with nothing comparing them) · starting and stopping services from the interface (`[UI-SERVICE-TOGGLES]`, after production: `docker.sock` in control-api is a root equivalent on the host) |
| ADR-130 | 2026-08-23 | **The crawl boundary is a SECURITY boundary, and it is never wider than the target's own host.** `base_origin` was computed by one line — everything up to the last slash: `normalize_url(target).rsplit("/", 1)[0] + "/"`. For a target WITHOUT a trailing slash — that is, for the most natural way to write one (`--target https://myapp.com`) — the last slash was the second slash of the scheme, and the boundary degenerated to `"https://"`; the `nu.startswith(origin)` test in `brain/graph.py` is then true for ANY https address. **Measured by BEHAVIOUR rather than by reading** (two local sites, 8181 and 8182, with a link from the first to the second): `--target http://127.0.0.1:8181` → 6 steps, **THREE of them on the foreign host** (`/index.html`, `/b2.html`, `/b3.html`); `--target http://127.0.0.1:8181/` → 3 steps and none. The cost is not the step count: a UI-testing tool wandered onto third-party sites — sending them headers, capturing frames and filing their pages into the run's artifact — and the person who wrote the target without a slash had no way to learn it happened. The rule now: `base_origin_of` (`brain/state.py`) never returns anything wider than `scheme://host/` for http(s), while a path narrows it further (`https://app/shop/x` → `https://app/shop/`), because a crawl started in a section must not spread over the whole site; for `file://` the boundary stays the target's directory — that scheme has no host. Computed in ONE place: the defect lived in two spots of `brain/__main__.py` at once (cold start and conversation continuation), and fixing one would have left the other. `tests/test_crawl_boundary_offline.py` holds the target table, the LINKAGE between boundary and filter (a correct boundary compared the wrong way is its own failure mode), a ban on the hand-written formula, and the presence of the filter itself. **Found while preparing a live 50-step target: crawling somebody else's site was impossible while this boundary leaked.** | Accepted | A crawl must stay on the target it was given. **Rejected:** normalising the target by appending a slash (treats the spelling, not the boundary) · host comparison by suffix parsing (`https://app.evil.example` passes for target `https://app`; string comparison against `scheme://host/` does not, and the gate proves it with that twin case) · leaving it and documenting «write the slash» (documentation is not a boundary) |
| ADR-131 | 2026-08-23 | **The crawl survives any page, and a crashed crawl no longer loses what it found (W7).** Three things, each measured on a live target. **(1) THE SNAPSHOT TAKES THE ROOT THAT EXISTS.** `browser.snapshot` waited unconditionally for `locator('body')` and killed the WHOLE run on a document that has none: measured on `the-internet/nested_frames` — 45 steps, `exit 4`, no `plan.json` at all. ⚠ The subtlety that hid it: per the HTML spec `document.body` on a frameset page returns NOT null but the `<frameset>` itself, so JS reading `document.body` worked — while the CSS selector `body` does not match, because it compares the tag name. The root is now `body, frameset`, the timeout is explicit and short (the default 5 s was chosen for the `browser.expect` budget, not for a snapshot), and the absence of both is a degradation with a NAMED reason rather than a refusal: in this same process `decorate.ts` has long treated a missing `<body>` as an ordinary reason to stay quiet. **(2) A `<frame>` IS ADDRESSED BY ITS OWN TAG, and links inside frames are visible.** All three branches of `frameSelector` built only `iframe[…]`, and the index counted `querySelectorAll('iframe')`: a named frame came back as `iframe[name="…"]` — an address issued, resolving to nothing — and an unnamed one scored −1, so the root SILENTLY dropped out. `browser.links` never crossed a frame boundary at all and returned `{links: []}`, indistinguishable from «there are no links». Measured after the change: the whole of `the-internet` — **90 steps, 36 pages, coverage 1.0, exit 0**. **(3) COMPLETENESS BECAME A STATEMENT, AND A CRASH STOPPED ERASING THE WORK.** `coverage_achieved: 1.0` on a crawl that hit its step ceiling is a truth that lies: coverage is a fraction of what was SEEN. No field for «incomplete» existed anywhere: `exploration_complete` is set identically for convergence, the ceiling, empty candidates and an orchestrator abort, while `reason` went only to `llm-transcript.jsonl`, which is not in the artefact whitelist. `plan.json` now carries a `completeness` block (`complete`, `reason`, `stopped_at_step`, `frontier_left`), and an incomplete crawl logs `explore.incomplete` with `degrades: true`. A crash is caught around `app.invoke`: the state is taken from the graph (`app.get_state`) BEFORE the checkpoint is discarded, partial `plan.json` and `site-map.json` are written, and a scenario is PROPOSED from the map that was collected — the precedent is in the graph itself, in the `map.rejected` branch. The exit code is a new **5** with `fault: tool`. **(4) THE CRAWL'S DEGRADATIONS STOPPED BEING INVISIBLE IN THE ARTEFACT.** `eventlog.degradations()` was read in exactly one place — `replay.py:604` — so lost quality reached a replay's `report.json` and never once reached a crawl's `plan.json`: a crawl with no model key, on a spent budget, or one that did not cover the site left a file that reads as clean. The `degradations` key is now written by BOTH producers of a plan — the `report` node and the salvage path — and on a crashed run it matters most: there `plan.json` is all the person has left. ⚠ The list is incomplete BY CONSTRUCTION, and that is written above the line itself: the plan is frozen inside the graph while teardown runs after it, so `system.trace_missing` and the video events will never appear there — they reach the reader through the journal and the verdict. `plan_hash` does not move: it is `canonical_plan_hash(steps)`, over the steps alone. | Accepted | Alex's directive of 2026-08-23: any UI is crawlable, findings are not lost, tests are proposed for at least what was found, and the status says when the crawl was partial. **Rejected:** `exit 1` for a salvaged run (in the catalogue that is `fault: app` — we would call our own breakage a defect of somebody else's application, exactly what ADR-087 forbade) · reading `checkpoint.db` as a file instead of `app.get_state` (a second statement of one fact) · demanding a non-empty snapshot on a frameset (it has no accessible nodes by construction — the assertion would be false; measured 0 against 58) |

On the `testdata/site-spa` fixture the site map grew **1 → 12** pages (20 at a ceiling of 200). One cause: `normalize_url` drops the fragment, and SPA routes live precisely there. **(2) ONLY A ROUTE-LIKE FRAGMENT.** `semantic_id` is built from this value and feeds `plan_hash`; including the fragment unconditionally would move EVERY key and break 106 stored plans (`replay` compares the hash and hard-aborts with code 3), the goldens and the healing cache — and there is no key-migration mechanism in this project at all. A route is `#/…` or `#!/…`; an ordinary anchor (`#section`) does not change identity, or a table of contents would breed states on every documentation target. ⚠ VERIFIED BY MEASUREMENT, NOT BY REASONING: `testdata/site` and `site-v2` produced the **byte-identical** `plan_hash` they do on `main` (`0281a5e7…` and `9eed627a…`), and the whole offline suite stayed green. **(3) THE CRAWL BOUNDARY IS COMPUTED WITHOUT THE FRAGMENT.** The rule "a path narrows the boundary" is about a path; applying it to a route would narrow the crawl to a single screen. `base_origin_of` works on `normalize_url` and did not change — but its table held NO fragment targets at all, and they were added. **(4) A CLICK THAT CHANGED THE ADDRESS IS RECOGNISED AS NAVIGATION.** `browser.click` returned `url` and `act` threw it away. The contribution is narrow and named honestly: the next `perceive` re-reads the address, so the difference shows where there will BE no next `perceive` — the run ended on a click. **(5) THE INTERFACE SAYS THE CRAWL DID NOT COVER THE WHOLE SITE** (Alex's directive, 2026-08-23). `completeness` sat in `plan.json` and was read by the hub through no channel whatsoever; now an interrupted crawl removes the tick from "UI exploration" and prints the reason in words, the step out of the ceiling, what is left in the frontier and WHAT to raise — with the caveat that coverage is a fraction of what was seen and an interrupted crawl can still read 100%. Measured on `practice.expandtesting.com` (permission confirmed verbatim): the file says `Allow: /` followed by **six** `Disallow` rules — ⚠ the register said “seven”, and nobody had re-measured that number. The default run: **6 rules read, 3 addresses excluded** (`/notes/api/api-docs/`, `/download-secure`, `/digest-auth`), with **zero** forbidden paths in the site map. The same run with `--ignore-robots` **did visit** `/notes/api/api-docs/`. **(2) OUR OWN MATCHER RATHER THAN `urllib.robotparser` — MEASURED.** The standard library takes the FIRST matching rule rather than the longest, so on the shape “`Allow: /` above the disallows” it answers “allowed” for `/download-secure`: `RobotFileParser.can_fetch('Sentinel', …)` → `True`. That is precisely the file this work exists for — taking the ready-made one would have meant claiming compliance without complying. RFC 9309 §2.2.2 is implemented instead: the longest match wins, ties go to `Allow`. The trap is pinned by a gate that asks the standard library itself. **(3) AN UNREACHABLE `robots.txt` ALLOWS THE CRAWL AND SAYS SO LOUDLY.** RFC 9309 permits treating 5xx as a full disallow, and for a search engine that is right. Here the cost is different: a person aims the tool at THEIR OWN application, and a staging box whose `robots.txt` returned 503 would be refused a crawl of its own site — with a message about somebody's will that nobody expressed. So unreachability allows, but carries `degrades` and the sentence “we did not see the rules”. An absent file (4xx) is a legitimate answer and raises no alarm. **(4) WHAT WAS EXCLUDED IS ALWAYS WRITTEN.** Without the list, “excluded” is indistinguishable from “there is nothing there”: a person opens the map, does not see a section and concludes the crawl never reached it — the same class `completeness` closed in ADR-131. An empty list with `respected: true` is a DIFFERENT statement from the key being absent. **(5) IGNORING THE RULES IS A PERSON'S CHOICE.** The flag is negative (`--ignore-robots`); the default needs no key, and the choice is written to the journal as `run.robots_ignored` and to the artefact as `respected: false`. The flag reaches all three surfaces (CLI, control-api, a hub checkbox) — a capability that lives only in the terminal is the gap ADR-107 exists to close. **(6) THE ADR-130 RESIDUE IS CLOSED, AND IT TURNED OUT TO BE A DEFECT.** The rejected `rsplit("/",1)[0]+"/"` formula lived on in `brain/replay.py`, where it computed the bases for RE-TARGETING a frozen plan: for the target `https://old.example` both bases degenerated to `https://`, the replacement became a no-op, and a replayed plan silently stayed on the OLD host — a green run about a different system. The ban read only `brain/__main__.py`; it now reads all of `brain/`, and the move was extracted into `retarget`/`retarget_bases` and is asserted BEHAVIOURALLY. | Accepted | Alex's decision, 2026-08-23: respect by default, disable explicitly, write the exclusions into the artefact. **Rejected:** `urllib.robotparser` (measured: allows the forbidden on the live target's own shape) · a full disallow on an unreachable file (would break crawling one's own staging box) · reading `robots.txt` in the `ground` node (a request to somebody else's site on every step — exactly the rudeness `robots.txt` exists to prevent) |

While the frontier had one source that made no difference; with a second source it would bypass both checks entirely, and `tests/test_robots_offline.py` would NOT NOTICE — it feeds the graph anchors only and would stay green over the hole itself. Same class as the boundary defect of 2026-08-22: the tool goes where the owner forbade it while `robots.excluded` claims the ban was honoured. The decision moved into `admit_to_frontier` with a NAMED answer (`admit` · `outside` · `known` · `robots`): "not admitted" comes in three kinds and a person must see two of them differently in the artefact. Order matters — the boundary answers FIRST, or the exclusion list starts claiming that a neighbouring site's owner forbade us something. ⚠ `robots` judges by the document PATH: a hash route never reaches the server, so no rule about it exists — that used to fall out by accident and is now said. **(2) THE CLICK WAITS FOR STATE.** `browser.click` read `page.url()` in the SAME TICK. The `plan_hash` is byte-identical throughout (`0281a5e7…`). ⚠ **AND THE MEASUREMENT EXPOSED A DEFECT:** in Playwright `timeout: 0` means "wait FOREVER", so `SENTINEL_CLICK_NAV_SETTLE_MS=0` — the most natural way to say "off" — hung the run on the FIRST non-navigating click. Zero now switches it off. **(3) FIXTURE L13** — the first page in the corpus that changes the address WITHOUT navigating the document: a synchronous `pushState`, one deferred by 120 ms (deliberately longer than any protocol round-trip and shorter than the default bound), and a click that does not touch the address at all. Without the counter-case, "a click is a navigation" is satisfied by code that says so about every click. | Accepted | Alex's decision, 2026-08-24, on `[CRAWL-CANNOT-SEE-BUTTON-NAVIGATION]`: the crawl must FIND routes, not merely tell them apart; the mechanism is a click plus `pushState` interception. **Rejected:** grepping `startswith(origin)` as the filter's guard (an assertion about the SHAPE of source; it reddened on a deliberate change when the comparison moved into a function — rewritten behaviourally) · reading the router's route table (`window.__spaFixture` is declared a seam FOR THE MEASURER — reading it would be cheating our own fixture) |

The forecast failed because changing the axis changes THE RUN ITSELF: the freed budget goes into content. | ADR-138 | 2026-08-31 | **The recorder writes down a route change, and the bridge stops guessing at one (W10).** ⚠ **THE REGISTRY'S DIAGNOSIS WAS WRONG, AND THAT IS MEASURED.** `[RECORDER-BLIND-TO-PUSHSTATE]` blamed the re-injection filter (`background/index.ts:137`, "pushState never reaches `complete`"). EVERY route change without a load — `pushState`, `replaceState`, a bare `location.hash`, `history.back` — raises a PAIR, `{status:'loading', url}` then `{status:'complete'}`; the filter passes and injects (three times for a burst of three pushState calls). `e.source === window` measured true. **Why not `webNavigation`:** it does not fire for a fragment (a second subscription), cannot tell push from replace, needs a user gesture, and arrives in the service worker while actions arrive from the content script — nothing orders the two channels. **Why not "read `location.href` a tick later":** `server.ts` already measured an Angular router landing its route in a promise (hence `waitForURL` with a budget);
