the second half of A-3 (two same-named controls on different query routes collapsing to one `semantic_id`) is NOT closed and is registered as `[PAGE-IDENTITY-DROPS-QUERY]`, because moving that key would break every stored plan and no migration mechanism exists. **Control:** goldens `site` `0281a5e729786449` and `site-v2` `9eed627adb6ab033` did not move. | Accepted | W10; the route axis |
| ADR-139 | 2026-08-31 | **The failure signal and the exit code are derived from ONE source (W10).** "Did the run fail" was computed by FIVE independent expressions: the `len(steps) >= 5` threshold in `_run_explore`; the authoring rule in `_write_scenario`; `failed_run = bool(errors) or not_grounded` in the `report` node, which printed the `verdict` frame; `0 if scenario_steps else 1` on the cold chat turn; and `rc = 4` in the outer `except`, where there was NO frame at all. **MEASURED on shipped functions:** the first two share NOT ONE input — the frame reads `{errors, scenario_unmatched, scenario_steps}`, the code reads `{plan_file.exists(), len(steps)}` — so they were not "drifting apart", they answered different questions and agreed by accident; the disagreement ran BOTH ways: a short crawl gave frame `ok/0` against exit 1, and a converged crawl with one failed click gave `failed/1` against exit 0 (`errors` was not in the success criterion at all). **THE FIVE-STEP THRESHOLD IS GONE — it punished the SIZE of the application:** measured with the real `_run_explore`, a one-page site with one button crawled COMPLETELY (`converged`, `complete: true`, coverage 1.00) produced 2 steps and `exit 1`, i.e. `fault: app` "the test found a problem" over a run with no problem; a two-page site (button + link) gave 3 steps and the same. The threshold was a milestone-M1 acceptance gate for one fixture, and the `GATE NOT MET` line that explained it was deleted long ago, so `exit 1` for this reason was explained to nobody. **Decision:** a new `brain/outcome.py` — `Facts` (facts only) → the pure, total `decide()` → `Outcome`, where the word is DERIVED from the number by the table `replay` already used; `announce()` prints the frame AND returns the code, and there is no other way to obtain the code. **What physically prevents drift:** the literal `"verdict"` is gone from `graph.py` (a second emitter would have to be WRITTEN, not edited — asserted by an AST gate) · `decide` is pure, so a new reason to go red cannot be added without extending `Facts` · a sweep over the reachable cells asserts `frame.exit_code == the actual code` from two OBSERVABLE values without importing the rule. **⚠ A TRAP REMOVED BY MEASURING BEFORE CODING:** the proposed "the crawl saw nothing" predicate (`interactive_seen == 0` plus an empty map) would have given a LEGITIMATE text-only page `exit 4` "the tool broke" — it and a broken executor agree on EVERYTHING: 0 steps, empty `interactive_seen`, false `any(site_map.values())`, empty `errors`, `reason` = `no_candidates`. Exactly ONE thing separates them: the address (a vacuum reports `current_url` = `''` and a map key of `''`), hence the third clause `saw_an_address`. **Found beyond the task:** on the `--goal`/`--describe` path teardown NEVER RAN — the return stood before `_stop_trace`/`_stop_video`/`shutdown`, so `SENTINEL_TRACE_ALWAYS` did nothing there; fixed by construction, the ordinary path now has one return. **Evidence** moved from the number to the outcome: `keep = exit_code != 0 or (degraded and SENTINEL_TRACE_ON_DEGRADED=1)` — a lever, not a default, so ADR-084 (a privacy window over someone else's DOM) is not rolled back silently; the balance is positive anyway, because a run with failed steps used to exit 0 and THROW the recording away. **Rejected: a sixth exit code** ⚠ **[THE ENTRY CONTRADICTED THE CATALOGUE AS IT WAS WRITTEN: code 5 shipped under ADR-131 on 23 August, so a sixth code ALREADY existed. Resolved in ADR-141]** — ADR-113 already rejected one as "it would break readers of the exit code", `verdictEnum` sends any unknown code to the word `problem`, and the mechanism for "say it is incomplete without going red" exists (`explore.incomplete` with `degrades`). **Live control:** `qwen3:8b` on `testdata/site`, 3 runs of 3 — `rc` 1 → **0**, frame `ok/0` → `pass/0`, `plan_hash 344a88080103b118` unchanged; the `site`/`site-v2` goldens unchanged; `testdata/site` exits 0, so the `replay` job under `set -e` stays green. ⚠ **The indirect catch for a collapsed crawl is GONE** (only `set -e` over `agentctl run` in CI held it) — compensated by a floor in the offline suite, which runs on every PR. | Accepted | W10; one outcome |
| ADR-140 | 2026-08-31 | **An address the crawl ASKED for leaves the frontier — even when the application took us somewhere else (W11).** ⚠ **THE REGISTRY ENTRY WAS STALE, AND THAT WAS MEASURED BEFORE ANY CODE.** `[CRAWL-BUDGET-BURNS-ON-RAIL-OSCILLATION]` described re-clicking two rail buttons (12 "Dashboard" + 9 "Orders" = 52 % of the budget, measured 2026-08-24). ADR-137 killed that mechanism: re-measurement gives **1× Dashboard, 1× Orders**, the two most frequent controls taking 5 % of the budget, and ZERO repeats at either ceiling. But the disease did not disappear — it moved to the NEIGHBOURING AXIS and grew: at a ceiling of 200 the run is **144 `navigate` + 56 `click`, and all 144 navigations go to ONE address**; 143 steps (**72 % of the budget**) were idle, with not one click between them. **THE MECHANISM, MEASURED END TO END:** the frontier holds a bare `index.html`; the router redirects on load to `#/home` (the fixture declares `data-start`, confirmed in a live Chromium: asked for `index.html`, landed on `index.html#/home`); `ground` cleans the frontier by the LANDED address (`frontier = [f for f in frontier if f != path]`), removing `#/home` and leaving the requested one; `visited_paths` also accumulates landings, so `admit_to_frontier` re-admits the address — and the anchor `href="index.html"` offers it again on EVERY perception. **This is the third case of ONE class in a row:** ADR-134 unified the frontier gate, ADR-137 split control identity from occurrence identity, and here "what we ASKED for" diverges from "where we ENDED UP". **Decision:** after performing a `navigate`, the `act` node removes the requested address from the frontier AND adds it to `visited_paths`. ⚠ **THE FIRST VERSION OF THE FIX CHANGED NOTHING** — it touched only `visited`, and measurement returned the same 144 navigations: `visited` guards ADMISSION of new addresses, and this one was ALREADY in the frontier. Both lists are needed, and the reason is recorded beside the code. **A/B (`site-spa`, ceiling 200):** 200 steps → **58**, navigations 144 → 2, idle steps 143 → 1, `reason` `max_steps` → **`no_candidates`**, `frontier_left` 1 → 0, with THE SAME 23 pages and THE SAME coverage 0.6667. The `chain` door (ceiling 40): 40 steps → 18, navigations 22 → 1; reach was checked separately — at a coverage target of 1.0 the same door gives the previous 17 exercised and 0.9444 in 19 steps instead of 40, so at the 0.85 target the crawl stops HAVING REACHED IT rather than falling short. The `site` `0281a5e729786449` and `site-v2` `9eed627adb6ab033` goldens did not move. **⚠ WHAT MATTERS TO THE READER OF THE ARTEFACT:** a crawl that stood on one address for 143 steps reported `max_steps` — "the ceiling was too low" — i.e. it invited the reader to raise a budget that was never the problem. The reason is now honest, asserted by a gate that ties two observable values together: fewer steps spent than the ceiling ⇒ the ceiling cannot be the reason. **⚠ REJECTED BY MEASUREMENT, NOT BY ARGUMENT:** seeding the start address into `visited_paths` (to remove the last remaining navigation) produced 53 steps but **22 pages instead of 23 and 52 clicks instead of 56**. Of the 144 navigations, 143 were idle and ONE was work: returning to the start unlocks controls unreachable from deep inside a route. The change was reverted; the cure proposed by `[EXECUTOR-GOTO-IS-UNCONDITIONAL]` — "do not goto if already there" — would lose exactly that return. | Accepted | W11; the requested-address axis |
| ADR-141 | 2026-08-31 | **The exit code reaches a human THE SAME WAY EVERYWHERE; the verdict word and the colour are derived from the catalogue (W11).** ⚠ **ADR-113 DECLARED THIS DONE, AND MEASUREMENT REFUTED IT.** Measured 2026-08-31: the exit code became visible to a human at **THIRTEEN** addresses and the catalogue was read by **ONE**. There were **EIGHT** hand-kept tables: `B_SEVCOL`, `chFinalize`, `tRenderRuns`, `verdictColor`/`verdictIcon` (hub), `verdictEnum` (Go), `_EXIT_COLOR` (`report.py`), the `exit_code == 3` branch (`junit.py`) and `VERDICT_WORD` (`outcome.py`) — the last of which named the catalogue as its source of truth in its own comment while not reading it. **WHAT THIS DID TO A PERSON:** one and the same `exit 5` run rendered three contradictory ways — a GREY badge in Build (the severity `tool_failure_salvaged` was absent from `B_SEVCOL`, so the lookup fell through the SILENT fallback to `var(--mut)`, the colour this page uses for "no information"), a muted `exit 5` in the runs list, and an UNCOLOURED bare string in Chat — while the results panel said `problem` in amber, because `verdictEnum` ate the word: `default: return "problem"` rewrote `tool_failure` (4), `tool_failure_salvaged` (5) and `not_started` (-1), all of which brain already pronounced. So BOTH halves of the boundary independently told the user that THEIR application was at fault when WE were the ones that broke. **Decision:** the catalogue gains a `verdict` field (the word a code means); Go calls `eventcatalog.ExitInfoOf` — a ready reader that had sat unused; `VERDICT_WORD` is derived from the catalogue; the page gets a SINGLE resolver, `bExitPresent`/`bExitBadge`, through which all six sites go. Colour REMAINS a property of the page (as `B_SEVCOL` records, and rightly), but the table must cover the catalogue's severities EXACTLY, checked in BOTH directions. `junit.py` derives "the run aborted" from the `fault` axis (`test`/`tool`) rather than from the number 3. **⚠ THE CATALOGUE GATE'S FLOOR WAS THE TUPLE `("0","1","2","3","-1")`:** measured by mutation, deleting `exit_codes["5"]` outright passed GREEN (rc=0, the banner printing "6 exit codes"), and only THREE of an entry's seven fields were constrained; stripping the hints from all seven, deleting `icon`, and inventing a `severity` all passed green too. The floor is now a COUNT (7) and every field is required. **⚠ WHAT THE DERIVATION ITSELF FOUND:** the registry named THREE pairs of prose tables, reconnaissance found FOUR, and walking the tree found **SIX** (`DETERMINISM`, `QUICKSTART`, `WINDOWS_TESTING` were invisible to everyone, including those looking). That is precisely why the set is derived. **⚠ ALEX'S DECISION:** in the public CI templates 4/5/-1 are `unstable`, not a failure — a red build in someone else's pipeline caused by OUR breakage sends them to debug an application that is certainly not at fault; the cost (the verdict softens for anyone who copies the template afresh) is named in the PR body. `-1` is DELIBERATELY absent from the templates: it is synthetic and no process returns it. **⚠ ADR-139 REJECTED "a sixth exit code" CITING ADR-113 while code 5 had already shipped under ADR-131 on 23 August** — the ADR record disagreed with the catalogue, and that is corrected here. | Accepted | W11; the "what happened" axis vs the "whose fault" axis |
> ADR template for new decisions:
> ```
> ### ADR-NNN: Title
> - Date / Status (Proposed/Accepted/Deprecated/Superseded) / Context / Decision / Consequences
> ```

## 4. Constraints

| Constraint | Type | Impact | Mitigation |
|------------|------|--------|------------|
| **BUILD-ONLY — no off-the-shelf products** (§0) | Business/strategic | Must write the TS Playwright executor ourselves; cannot adopt `@playwright/mcp` | ADR-001; thin tool layer over Playwright's stable lib API; contract tests; pin version |
| LLM non-determinism | Technical | Autonomous explorer not bit-reproducible | Explore-once/replay-many + `plan_hash` hard-abort (ADR-006) |
| LLM token cost | Business | Explore on large SPAs is expensive (Opus) | Coverage convergence (ADR-010), per-run budgets, graceful degradation, Go-side hard ceiling |
| a11y-tree blind spots | Technical | Shadow DOM / canvas / custom elements / cross-origin iframes give partial perception | ⚠ **MEASURED, NOT MITIGATED (re-verified 2026-08-10).** This cell used to read "`completeness_ratio` metric → gated visual fallback". Neither half was ever true: `grep -rni completeness` over `*.py/*.ts/*.go` returns zero, and the visual tier is gated by `use_visual && backend.supports_vision` alone (the Tier-7 branch of `Heal.heal` in `brain/healing.py`; `HEAL_VISUAL=1`), never by a ratio. The historical promise is kept where it belongs — the ADR-005 row — not here. What EXISTS today (ADR-092/093/095/097) is a MEASUREMENT, not a fallback: `browser.perceptionAudit` reports what was seen with a breakdown, `plan.json` carries `perception.worst_ratio` beside `steps`, control-api exports it as the `visibility` metric, and `report.html` renders "not measured" differently from "all seen"; frames were closed as an axis by ADR-095. `[PROD-BLINDSPOTS]` was CLOSED 2026-07-28; canvas and CLOSED shadow roots are declared boundaries rather than debt, and the open remainder is `[HEAL-VISUAL-FRAME]`. Asking the AUT to add `data-testid`/ARIA stays advice, not a mitigation. |
| Home-lab target (K3s/ArgoCD/Proxmox/Ceph) | Technical | Deploy as GitOps service @ M5 | Helm chart + ArgoCD Application; Postgres swap behind trigger |
| Chromium-only @ MVP | Technical | Golden a11y/screenshot hashes differ per engine | Firefox/WebKit deferred; baseline portability is an open question |

---

## 5. Principles
1. **Trust is the product** — a non-deterministic LLM explorer must be *structurally* unable to silently rewrite its own baseline or run a tampered plan.
2. **Build, own, control** — no turnkey third-party products; we own every boundary (build-only sovereignty).
3. **Buy nothing, but reuse OSS libraries** — write code against Playwright/LangGraph/Anthropic SDK; build the *components*, not the *primitives*.
4. **Defer infrastructure behind named triggers** — gRPC @ M2, Postgres @ M5, OTel @ M4 — no speculative gold-plating.
5. **Verify before trust** — every healed locator is re-probed against the live DOM before acceptance; every confidence threshold is calibrated, never a magic constant.
6. **Measure convergence, don't assert it** — coverage metric over LLM "done" flag.
7. **Nothing happens silently** (Alex's requirement, 2026-07-27) — every tool behaviour, every error, every move of the exploration and every result must simultaneously be: **(a)** intelligible to a human IN THE INTERFACE, not only in an artefact on disk; **(b)** logged under a catalogued code carrying level, phase and text in the reader's language; **(c)** measured — available to metrics, traces and the observability stack. The three channels do not substitute for one another: a file on disk is not visible to a person, a log does not aggregate, a metric does not explain. A swallowed exception and a bare "it failed" are direct violations, because silence reads as success.
8. **The two planes do not mix** (Alex's requirement, 2026-07-27) — what the TOOL does and what the APPLICATION UNDER TEST does must be distinguishable on EVERY surface: in the event, the verdict, the report, the metric, the trace and the log. The user must be able to answer "did my application break, or the tool?" at any moment by looking at what is in front of them, not by correlating artefacts. Done for events (ADR-067) and the verdict (ADR-072); still to be extended to metrics and traces, where an application error and a tool error become one number today.

The "an interactive browser run remains manual" clause of the PR-4 row is hereby closed. | ADR-061 | @AlexGromer |

The wizard gains a "💾 Save to server" button whose document is built from the non-secret controls only. ## 7. Where the details live (`docs/`)
| File | Contents |
|------|------------|
| `docs/STATE_MACHINE.md` | The full LangGraph: 10 nodes (explore graph), every edge (conditional/takeover included), the schema of the shared `RunState` object; replay/baseline go through a separate `run_replay()` that bypasses the graph |
| `docs/SELF_HEALING.md` | The 10-step self-healing algorithm, L1–L6 strategy priors, the confidence gate, calibration |
| `docs/DETERMINISM.md` | Explore-once/replay-many, `plan_hash` hard-abort, immutable golden baselines, flake quarantine, exit codes |
| `docs/MEMORY_PERSISTENCE.md` | Short-term / long-term memory, the SQLite schema (every table), checkpoint GC |
| `docs/OBSERVABILITY.md` | OTel tracing, the LLM transcript, token budget + hard caps, Prometheus metrics |
| `docs/OUTPUTS.md` | The 10 generated artefacts |
| `docs/ROADMAP.md` | Milestones M0→M7 with acceptance gates in Given/When/Then form + build-only deltas |
| `docs/M6_CONTRACT.md` | The M6 contract: a provider-agnostic LLM backend (`brain/llm.py`, ADR-019) |
| `docs/M7_CONTRACT.md` | The M7 contract (Proposed): exposing the brain as an MCP server + `SamplingBackend` (ADR-020) |
| `docs/DESIGN_RECORD.md` | The full design history: 4 architect proposals + 3 judge verdicts + the synthesis decision history |
| `GAPS.md` | Open questions, VERIFY items, risks, build-only consequences |

## 8. Top risks (a summary — the full list, and the status of each, live in `GAPS.md`)

> This is a SELECTION (five risks out of ten + two security gaps), not the registry. Status lives in the
> "Status" column of the risk table in `GAPS.md`; it is repeated here in one word per item so that the summary
> cannot be read as "risk mitigated" while the registry still has it open. On a discrepancy, `GAPS.md` is right.
1. **`pw-executor` is now on the critical path** (build-only): the largest single unit of build volume + permanent upkeep against Playwright API churn. *Mitigation:* a thin instrumentation layer over the stable Playwright Locator/accessibility API; contract tests; pin version. ⚠ *Audit 2026-07-27, re-checked 2026-08-10: of the three mechanisms named, exactly one is real — `contract tests` do not exist (there is no `contracts/` directory, and the TS suite does not import `server.ts`).* *Status: **OPEN** — `GAP-RISK-001`; the unit of work is `[ARCH-001]`.*
2. **There is no confidence model** (wording corrected 2026-07-26, ADR-080 — "cold start" understated the problem: it is not that the data is not there YET). The number comes from a static dictionary `brain/strategies.py::PRIORS` keyed by the strategy NAME; neither `healing_audit` nor anything else feeds into it, and there are zero human-labelled outcomes. *There is no mitigation* — the earlier wording, "threshold 0.90 until N labelled examples", described a mechanism that never existed in the code (the 0.90 in `brain/calibrate.py::calibrate(cold_start=…)` is reporting into `state/calibration.json` and nothing else; `healing.py` imports nothing from `calibrate.py`). The boundary is held by model-independent gates: `AUTO`/`FLAG` = 0.85/0.60, env-tunable via `SENTINEL_HEAL_AUTO`/`SENTINEL_HEAL_FLAG` (ADR-080) · the ADR-080 cap "a re-bind is never ACCEPTED silently" — `_gate` demotes it from the AUTO band to FLAGGED but does not stop applying it (ADR-005/017) · the ADR-082 identity predicate (`verified`/`contradicted`/`unverifiable`, with no new threshold) · verify-before-accept + post-heal verify. See `GAP-RISK-002`. *Status: **OPEN**, no mitigation.*
3. **Token-cost blowout on large SPAs** (`GAP-RISK-003`). ⚠ *Mitigation partial — of the six mechanisms claimed, one is real (audit 2026-07-27).* What stood here was "coverage convergence, per-page budget, graceful degradation, incremental explore". In reality: **graceful degradation** exists; **coverage convergence** — the exit branch exists, but it requires coverage ≥ target AND an empty frontier AT THE SAME TIME, which on a 50+-page SPA (that is, in the very scenario the risk describes) never happens; **per-page budget** and **incremental explore** are absent (budgets are per-run only). ⚠ The change log entry of 2026-07-05 recorded "Clears RISK-003" — that wording is wrong and is corrected in place: M15.1 delivered the EMISSION of token numbers, i.e. it unblocked the MEASUREMENT; it did not clear the risk. *Status: **OPEN** — units of work `[RISK-003]`, `[PROD-CRAWL]`, `[PROD-FIXTURE-SPA]`.*
4. **Heal-storm latency in the deterministic replay hot path.** ⚠ *Mitigation partial — all three claimed mechanisms are absent (ADR-088 audit, re-checked 2026-08-10; details in `GAP-RISK-004`).* What stood here was "a hard cap of 2 attempts + a per-step deadline + amortization of cached locators (`dom_subtree_hash`)". In reality: **there is no attempt limit, no step deadline and no auto-skip in the code** (`grep -rn 'heal_attempt|max_heal|per_step_deadline|step_deadline'` over `*.py/*.go/*.ts` → zero). There is no retry at all — `brain/replay.py` calls `heal.heal(ctx)` **exactly once** per failed step (`docs/SELF_HEALING.md` §Step 10), which is stricter than the two attempts promised; but **the cost of that single call is bounded by nothing in time**: a full a11y snapshot + `browser.interactives` + a probe for every frozen alternative + up to one LLM request + up to one vision request per step. The only limiter is the token budget of the `heal` role (`HEAL_TOKEN_LIMIT`, 20000 by default, `brain/budget.py`), and it throttles only the LLM and visual tiers, not the deterministic L1–L6 rotation. Amortization through the `healed_locators` cache does exist, but it is keyed by a hash of the **whole page** rather than of a subtree (see risk 5), so in the scenario the risk is about — an actively changing AUT — it is invalidated by the first unrelated markup edit. The step deadline and the auto-skip are an open item. *Status: **OPEN** — `GAP-RISK-004`.*
5. **`dom_hash` fragility — the risk is NOT mitigated, and the column name misleads** (`GAP-RISK-006`, `GAP-ARCH-004`). *The claimed mitigation does not exist:* `brain/replay.py` puts `dom_hash = _a11y_hash(ariaSnapshot)[:16]` into the heal context, where `_a11y_hash` is a raw SHA-256 of the a11y snapshot of the WHOLE page, and that same value is stored in a column named `dom_subtree_hash` (`healed_locators`, `internal/store/server.go` and its mirror `brain/store.py`). The consequence: an unrelated edit anywhere on the page invalidates the cache of ALL of that page's heals, while the schema tells the reader the opposite (ADR-088). Subtree narrowing, a configurable scope and a CSS ignore-list are not in the code. *Remainder:* `GAP-ARCH-004` (P1) — how to determine the target subtree reliably; or else rename the column so that it stops promising what it does not do. *Status: **OPEN** — `GAP-RISK-006`.*
6. **env inheritance + secrets in Helm** (GAP-SEC-001): *the Helm/env half is **CLOSED** (M11.3/ADR-035):* `agentctl filteredEnv()` is now **default-on** (a prefix allowlist `LLM_`/`OTEL_`/`PW_`/`PLAYWRIGHT_`/`SENTINEL_` + an exact list for `NODE_OPTIONS`/`GIT_SSL_*`; `NODE_`/`GIT_` are deliberately NOT prefixes — #25), Helm supplies the key and the DSN through `secretKeyRef`; AUT secrets go through `secretRef`+`PW_NO_TRACE` (GAP-RISK-010). *Remainder:* dynamic Vault/CSI-driver secrets. The full threat model is `docs/THREAT_MODEL.md`. *Status: **PARTIALLY MITIGATED** — `GAP-SEC-001`.*
7. **Supply chain** (GAP-SEC-002): *mostly **CLOSED**:* gitleaks/govulncheck/pip-audit/npm audit gates (§1) + a committed `brain/uv.lock`; **M11.1** — keyless Cosign signatures + syft SBOM + GHCR; **M11.4** — an air-gapped bundle + an offline verifier. *Remainder:* an actual signed release/bundle — maintainer-gated on the first `v*` tag. *Status: **PARTIALLY MITIGATED** — `GAP-SEC-002`.*

---

## Extensions by type — Development

### Build and CI
- **Languages/tools:** Go (control plane), Python 3.x (brain, LangGraph), TypeScript/Node (pw-executor, Playwright).
- **CI:** GitHub Actions. The list of jobs and steps is **not duplicated** here: the authoritative one lives in `docs/PR_ACCEPTANCE.md` §1 and is cross-checked against `.github/workflows/ci.yml` by the `tests/test_pr_acceptance_offline.py` gate **in both directions** — a job the document is silent about is as red as a documented check that no workflow runs. Architecturally significant here is only what the job list does not show: the deterministic push/PR gates and the scanners of LIVE advisory databases are deliberately split across different workflows (`ci.yml` versus `security-nightly.yml`, which is why a fresh upstream CVE does not redden a PR), the `explore` job is not part of push/PR gating (`if: github.event_name == 'workflow_dispatch'`), and `replay` runs as a matrix in which every leg is a separate runner, so a run's SQLite is isolated by construction rather than by configuration. ⚠ **There is NO proto stub generation and no `.proto` hash assertion in CI** (`grep -rni proto .github/workflows/` → zero): what of that gate actually exists, and why it is silent in CI, is in the **proto** component row in §2; the open items are `GAP-RISK-008` and `[PROTO-STUB-GATE]`.
- **Pre-commit:** gitleaks; `.claude/` is git-ignored (never committed).

### Testing strategy
- **Self-test breakdown:** Go unit (orchestrator FSM, budget reconciliation), Python unit (node logic, confidence model), TS unit (the pw-executor instrumentation layer), contract tests (proto stubs, MCP tool schema), e2e (the agent against a fixture app).
- **Acceptance = milestone gates** (`docs/ROADMAP.md`), expressed in Given/When/Then form with threshold values.

### Dependencies (OSS libraries — permitted under build-only)
Playwright (pinned), LangGraph + checkpointer, Anthropic SDK, gRPC/protobuf (buf/protoc), SQLite (WAL), Prometheus client, OpenTelemetry SDK. **No off-the-shelf third-party servers/SaaS.**
